But she too had risen and would hear no word, crying on her women to lift down the rich-wrought byrny and great eagle-winged helm of Eric the King, and the rimmed shield and the greaves of polished bronze. These last she herself buckled on Styrbiorn’s legs, he laughing the while at the whole matter, somewhat shamefacedly, as at some mad prank scarce fit for him to play with, being no longer in his child’s age. Yet is it to be thought that he felt it was not wholly child’s play: the touch of the Queen’s hands gliding with a swift and caressing motion along the great muscle of his calf, as she made fast the fastenings of the greaves. He stepped back as a man might step who has blundered into another’s chamber. Not to lose countenance with her, he laughed yet the more boisterously, taking the King’s helm that a woman proffered to him and setting it on his head. So that there he stood in the firelight’s splendour, helmed and byrnied and with shield on arm, and girt with the King’s sword silver-studded. Erect and grand, as of an eagle alighting from his skyey eminence, the brown wings spread upward from the helm on either side. With lips parted, the Queen looked. She spake no word.


===Chinese===
“这是什么？”斯蒂尔比约恩说。“我叔父王的？”他从座位上站起，略带迟疑，仿佛她这番心血来潮，把玩笑开得过了界。可她也站了起来，一句也听不进，只唤侍女们把王那件精工锁子甲和鹰翼大盔取下来，还有镶边圆盾和磨亮的青铜胫甲。胫甲是她亲手替斯蒂尔比约恩扣上腿的；他一面任她摆布一面笑，神情有些赧然，仿佛这是桩孩童式的胡闹，他这般年纪的人本不该再玩。然而不可不想，他并未全当它是孩童的玩闹：王后的手指顺着他的小腿大块肌肉滑过，动作轻捷爱抚，替他系紧甲扣。他倒退一步，像误闯他人内室的人。为了不在她跟前失态，他越发粗声大笑，接过一名侍女递来的王盔，扣在头上。于是他立在火光辉煌之中，戴盔着甲，臂挽盾牌，腰悬王的嵌银宝剑。挺拔雄伟，如自九天高处降落的鹰隼，棕色双翼从头盔两侧向上张开。王后唇儿微启，望着他，一言不发。


===Original===
“The gear fitteth me?” said Styrbiorn.

The Queen met his laughing glance with no answering smile. Only under the silken bosom of her gown her breast lifted suddenly like the sudden filling of a sail at sea, and her dark eyes opened on him very wide and tense. She mastered herself on the instant, and was all cool and easy jollity. But to Styrbiorn, who was not yet so young but he had learned to know well enough by this time of day where the little coney loves to scout, that wide-eyed look spoke a language plump and plain.

“What of my young kinsman?” said he. “Wilt sail with me, lad, when thou’rt of age?” And he took the child from the nurse and held it high over his head, dancing it in air. But the child was frighted, and puckered up its face and screamed.


===Chinese===
“这行头合我的身么？”斯蒂尔比约恩说。

王后迎着他含笑的目光，却无笑意相报。只有她丝袍的前襟底下，胸乳蓦地高高隆起，像海上骤然兜满风的船帆；那双黑眼睛睁得极大、极紧地望着他。她当即自持，立刻换出一副从容轻快的高兴。可是斯蒂尔比约恩——他年纪虽轻，却早已深谙小野兔爱在何处探头窥望——那双睁大的眼睛对他说的，是一番直白露骨的语言。

“我这小亲戚怎么说？”他说。“小家伙，等你长大了，肯随我出海么？”说着从奶娘手里接过孩子，高高举过头顶，在空中颠着逗他。孩子却吓着了，皱起小脸，尖声哭叫起来。


===Original===
Now it so befell that Helgi was stood in the doorway looking on in this nick of time. He went now to find his friends Thorir and Thorgisl, and they talked together long and low. After that, they went to find Thorgnyr the Lawman. They were somewhat slow in coming to the point with him, but Thorgnyr was pleasant with them and led them on to talk freely. And at length they unlocked to him that which abode in their mind, that it was now the happy hour to pull the bench from under Styrbiorn, and that the means thereto lay ready to hand: namely, to let the King know plainly that it was common talk in the house that Styrbiorn and Sigrid were overgood friends together, and to let him know that Styrbiorn was minded to snatch by force not his own heritage only before his time but the whole kingdom from out of his uncle’s hand: “And that he sitteth openly in the Queen’s bower, and all as if he were already set in the King’s stead, both in bed and hall; and striketh too and spurneth the King’s own son, which crieth out and weepeth pitifully.”


===Chinese===
恰在这个当口，海尔吉正站在门口望见了这一幕。他随即去找托里尔和索尔吉尔，三人低声密议了许久。此后他们便去寻法律宣讲人松格尼尔。他们吞吞吐吐不肯直说，松格尼尔却和颜悦色，引他们畅所欲言。到头来他们向他吐露了心底的话：如今正是抽掉斯蒂尔比约恩凳脚的良机，而且手段现成——便是去让王明白知晓：府中人人都在传，斯蒂尔比约恩与西格丽德过从甚密；又让王知道，斯蒂尔比约恩居心不止要提前强夺自家的继承权，还要把整个王国从叔父手中一并抢去：“他公然坐在王后的闺阁里，俨然已坐上了王的位置，床笫与厅堂都算数；他还动手打王自己的儿子，那孩子哭得可怜。”


===Original===
“Which of you will tell this tale to the King?” asked Thorgnyr.

“This was in our mind,” answered Thorir, “that this should be the hopefullest way, if thou wouldst be willing to talk to the King, Thorgnyr: if indeed thou deemest well of our redes.”

“When it cometh all to all,” said Thorgnyr, “is there a word of truth in the whole story?”

“Say there were not,” said Helgi: “ ’twill come true afore the tale be told.”

“Fruit is best gathered when ’tis ripe,” said Thorgnyr. “As for this tale of yours, it will not fill half the nostril of a cat. I’ll have naught to do with it.”


===Chinese===
“这故事谁去讲给王听？”松格尼尔问。

“我们打的算盘是，”托里尔答道，“最有望的路子，便是由你去对王说，松格尼尔——倘若你真觉得我们的计策使得。”

“归根结底一句话，”松格尼尔说，“这故事里可有一句真话？”

“就算一句没有，”海尔吉说，“不等故事讲完，它也会成真。”

“果子熟了摘才好，”松格尼尔说。“至于你们这故事，还填不满猫的半个鼻孔。我不沾它。”


===Original===
“Come with me,” said Helgi then. “I’ll show it thee through the chink of the Queen’s door.”

“No,” said Thorgnyr. “I am not a pryer into chinks and look-holes.”

So they went out from Thorgnyr very ill content.

“What’s to do next?” said Thorgisl.

“What but to go to the King ourselves?” said Helgi.

“He will not heed us,” said Thorir.


===Chinese===
“随我来，”海尔吉说。“我从王后门缝里指给你看。”

“不去，”松格尼尔说。“我不是扒门缝窥孔洞的人。”

三人满怀不快，从松格尼尔处出来。

“接下来怎么办？”索尔吉尔说。

“除了咱们亲自去见王，还能怎样？”海尔吉说。

“王不会理睬咱们。”托里尔说。


===Original===
In the end they were of this mind, that may be it was best for them to say naught of it for the present. “Only we will not cease to flatter and egg him forward to some open violence, which shall give the King just pretext to do him away ere a worse thing befall.”

Styrbiorn put off the King’s armour and betook him to the great hall. Here he sat quiet till supper time, and spake to no man. Men deemed it strange to see him so quiet and brooding. Before supper he came to the King. “Lord,” he said, “I did ill to come back before the time. I will ride down to the ships at daybreak and fare abroad again.”


===Chinese===
末了三人商定：眼下还是只字不提为妙。“只是奉承鼓动不可停，总要撺掇他闹出一场明火执仗的暴行，好教王名正言顺除掉他，免得日后出了更糟的事。”

斯蒂尔比约恩卸下王的甲胄，独自去了大厅。他静坐到晚饭时分，不与一人说话。众人见他这般安静沉郁，都觉蹊跷。晚饭前他来见王。“主上，”他说，“我提前回来是做错了。天一亮我就下船队去，重新出海。”


===Original===
“That is well spoken,” said the King, and took him by both hands.

“It was not to vex you I came hither,” said Styrbiorn. “It was a long time. It seemed to me I could not help but come. You have been good to me; and now I will go away and keep to the bargain.”

They gripped hands and said no more. But there was great content in the King’s eyes as they met Styrbiorn’s.

The King brought Styrbiorn and his men down to the ships next day. They took leave of both parts with great kindness, and the ships rowed out of the Low and were no more seen. The King rode with Thorgnyr on their way home. Thorgnyr held his peace. After a while the King said, “Thou art a wise man, Thorgnyr, but I think I have taught thee somewhat.”


===Chinese===
“这话痛快。”王说，双手握住他的手。

“我来这里不是要惹恼你，”斯蒂尔比约恩说。“日子太长了。我觉着不由自主，非回来不可。你一向待我好；如今我便走，守住咱们的约定。”

两人紧握了手，再没多说。但王的眼里迎着斯蒂尔比约恩的目光时，盛满了称心遂意。

次日，王亲自送斯蒂尔比约恩一行登船。两方殷殷道别，船队驶出低泽，再也望不见了。王与松格尼尔并辔回程。松格尼尔一路默然。行了一程，王说：“松格尼尔，你是智者，可这回，我想是我教了你一手。”


===Original===
Thorgnyr looked at him awhile in silence. Then he said, “It is not to be denied, King, that you have played well; and you have gotten that you played for.”

The King was like a man that hath borne over long time a difficult burden and, casting it down at length where he would have it, breatheth free and seeth all fair before him. He looked at Thorgnyr with a twinkling eye. “It is hard for thee, Thorgnyr, but thou must own thou wast wrong.”

“I was not wrong,” said Thorgnyr.

“No more,” said the King then in a sudden anger. “Thou wast wrong.”

But that old man looked sullenly before him, riding northward at the King’s side. He said again, under his breath, “I was not wrong.”


===Chinese===
松格尼尔默然望了他一阵，才说：“王啊，不可讳言：你这一局走得漂亮；你要赢的，也都赢到手了。”

王像一个久负重担的人，终于把它卸在自己要卸的地方，长出一口气，眼前一片坦荡。他目光闪亮地看着松格尼尔。“松格尼尔，这话叫你难受，可你必得认错。”

“我没有错。”松格尼尔说。

“休得再言，”王勃然作色。“你错了。”

老人却阴沉着脸目视前方，骑在王侧一路向北。他又低声说了一遍：“我没有错。”

## VIII / 八

===Original===
The King and the Queen

It was yet dark winter. Styrbiorn, he and his, sailed south along the land and came, after an ill voyage yet without unhap or loss, to Skaney. Here they put in with their ships and went up to the great house of Strut-Harald the Earl, who gave them noble welcome and kept them with him till winter’s end.


===Chinese===
王与王后

时值隆冬，天犹晦暗。斯蒂尔比约恩率部众沿海岸向南航行，一路风浪艰辛，幸无灾无损，到了斯科讷。他们在此收船入港，上到斯特鲁特-哈拉尔伯爵的大宅。伯爵殷勤相待，礼数极隆，留他们住到冬尽。


===Original===
Strut-Harald sat in Skaney in those days in state like unto a king, and was a very magnificent man in his housekeeping and had alway guests coming and going. In all Skaney-side his word went as a king’s, and there was no man there but held him in the greatest worship and esteem and was ready at all tides to do his bidding. He stood no more now in wars and harryings, being very old and unmeet for fighting. Yet was he no whit the more for that thrust into the corner, as sometimes it befalleth to an old man past his strength, for he had powerful sons and sons-in-law and young men of his blood always under his hand honouring him and upholding his state. And he was a man glorious to look upon even in his deep old age, being nowise bowed down as is the manner of old men, but whether walking or sitting, straight-shouldered and broad-chested and with a proud and high carriage of the head. A big man and a tall he was, great-boned, lean in his old age. He would be clad always in exceeding costly attire, having his garments of rich and lovely colours, and jewelled ornaments of price, and his weapons fairly fashioned and of rare workmanship. And he wore always on his head, within door and without, an hat which was covered with round plates of gold of the bigness of the palm of a woman’s hand. So that many there were who thought, when they considered Earl Strut-Harald in his state and wearing that hat which had more gold about it than many a king’s crown, that none save only the mightiest kings dwelt in greater circumstance of splendour and magnificence than he.

Sigvaldi and Thorkell the High were there with the Earl their father in Skaney, and had been there all the winter. They were glad at their friends’ coming and Styrbiorn’s, and the days and weeks went by in good pleasure and contentment until spring.


===Chinese===
斯特鲁特-哈拉尔伯爵那些日子端坐斯科讷，排场俨然王者，治家豪奢，宾客往来不绝。斯科讷全境，他的话便如王命一般，人人对他敬奉到十分，随时听候差遣，无一人例外。他如今不再亲临战阵劫掠——年事已高，不堪厮杀——却丝毫没有像力气已衰的老人那样被挤到墙角：他手下始终有势大的儿子、女婿和族中子弟侍奉尊崇，撑持他的威仪。即使到了迟暮之年，他依然仪表堂堂，全不似寻常老人那般伛偻：无论行走端坐，都是肩背挺直，胸膛宽阔，头颅高昂，气度傲岸。他生得高大，骨架硕大，老来清瘦。他向来衣着奢华无比：衣裳颜色富丽悦目，缀着价值连城的珠宝，兵器打造精美，工艺罕见。而且无论室内户外，他头上总戴着一顶帽子，帽上覆满圆形金片，每片都有女子手掌大小。于是许多人揣想：看斯特鲁特-哈拉尔伯爵这般威仪，头上那顶帽子所嵌的黄金比许多王冠还多，除了最强盛的君王，怕没有人家的富贵气象盖得过他。

西格瓦迪与高个托尔克尔正在斯科讷父亲伯爵膝下，已住了整整一冬。见朋友和斯蒂尔比约恩到来，二人大喜。日复一日，周复一周，众人尽欢称意，直到春天。


===Original===
When Styrbiorn came to Strut-Harald to bid him farewell, the Earl held him a long time by both hands and looked at him long time without word spoken. Then he began to say, “I would thou wert my son. For my sons, good though they be, I deem not good enough. Heming can steer a ship and can make good play with sword and spear, but methinks he will ever have that nature to follow still where another leadeth. Thorkell is a man of war, but he hath little wit. Sigvaldi is a fox. He will get him lordship and wealth and fame and a long life: many men will follow and obey him, but the best men will not praise him. And, true it is, unto few men is it fated to be great, and of fair fame, and long-lived. I think, Styrbiorn, that the first two of these will be thine. But I think thy life will be short.”

The Earl’s eyes were very blue and keen-glancing. It seemed to Styrbiorn that they rested not on himself when they looked at him, but on some matter afar off, unseen by other men. Styrbiorn said, “I reck not the number of my days, so they be good.”

“Fare thee well,” said the Earl. “I wish thou wert my son.”


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩来向斯特鲁特-哈拉尔辞行时，伯爵双手握住他的手，握了许久，望着他也望了许久，一言不发。然后才开口：“但愿你是我的儿子。我这些儿子，虽都不差，我总觉得还不够好。赫明会掌舵，剑矛也使得漂亮，可我看他的性子，永远只会跟着别人走。托尔克尔是员战将，可惜没什么头脑。西格瓦迪是只狐狸。他自有办法弄到权位、财富、名声，还会长寿：许多人会追随他、服从他，但真正的好人不会称许他。说实话，既能伟大，又有令名，还得享高寿——命中注定得此三者的人寥寥无几。斯蒂尔比约恩，我想，前两样会是你的。只是你的寿数，只怕不长。”

伯爵的眼睛极蓝，目光锐利逼人。斯蒂尔比约恩觉得，那双眼望向他时，其实并不落在他身上，而是落在某件远处的事上——一件旁人看不见的事。斯蒂尔比约恩说：“我不计较我的寿数长短，只要日子过得堂堂正正。”

“愿你平安。”伯爵说，“但愿你是我的儿子。”


===Original===
---

			

From Skaney Styrbiorn sailed first south over the sea to Jomsburg. There sailed with him those sons of Strut-Harald, Sigvaldi namely and Thorkell, with many ships of theirs and a great following, and they abode certain days in Jomsburg. And thither came, some today and some tomorrow till they were all gathered thither, the other lords of Jomsburg, Palnatoki and Bui of Borgundholm and Sigurd his brother and many more, until the burg was filled with men and the harbour thick with long-ships, like a stagnant pool under sallows in the season of the year when the leaves do fall, and the leaves of the sallows lie so many on the face of the pool that hard it is to tell whether it be water there or firm land hid with the leaves.


===Chinese===
---

			


离开斯科讷，斯蒂尔比约恩先渡海南下，前往约姆斯堡。斯特鲁特-哈拉尔的两个儿子，西格瓦迪和托尔克尔，率自家船队、携大批随从与他同航，在约姆斯堡盘桓数日。约姆斯堡其余的首领们也络绎赶到——今天到几个，明天到几个，直到齐聚一堂：帕尔纳托基、博恩霍尔姆的布伊及其弟西格德，还有许多别的人——直到堡中挤满了人，港里挤满了长船，恰似落叶时节柳荫下的一汪死水：柳叶层层铺满池面，难辨下面是水，还是盖满落叶的实地。


===Original===
Now they took counsel together, spring being come and the time now ended of Styrbiorn’s being abroad. Many were fain to have fared north with Styrbiorn into Sweden to see the King there and to see Styrbiorn received into kingdom. But Styrbiorn said he would not go home thither until summer’s end. “How is that?” said they. “Is not thy time come now that summer setteth in, and thou hast been abroad three winters?” He answered, “That may be, but I shall have mine own way in this.”

So Styrbiorn sailed a-harrying the fourth summer with Palnatoki and the host of the Jomsburgers. Biorn Asbrandson the Broadwicker’s Champion was his shipmate, and men thought they could see how day by day the love and friendship of those two waxed and strengthened. Biorn was with him when he and Bui fared north into Biarmaland and the unknown places of Kirialaland, being thither drawn by the many tales in men’s mouths concerning that land: how that in Kirialaland only. Finns and skin-changers do inhabit, such as be not alway of one shape nor alway in that same place where they do seem to be; moreover that they of Kirialaland do observe an idol of great note, Jomala by name, that weareth a silver belt about his middle, in a temple in the darkness of a wood that is fulfilled of trolls and evil wights so as a man shall be drove clean out of his wits for very fright sake if he durst adventure there save by favour of Jomala. Styrbiorn and his folk coming to that place found not indeed any troll nor skin-changer, but they sacked Jomala’s temple and took the belt of him, that was of a fashion that none yet had seen, wrought with nickers and other worms intertwisted, and took besides so great a spoil of silver and costly treasures as no man could ever remember to have seen taken in one place and in a single day.


===Chinese===
春天已至，斯蒂尔比约恩客居外邦的期限也到了，众人于是聚议。许多人巴不得随他北上瑞典，去见那里的王，看斯蒂尔比约恩受立为王。斯蒂尔比约恩却说，夏末之前他不回去。“这是怎么说？”众人道，“夏天都到了，你出海已历三冬，你的期限不是到了么？”他答道：“也许是。但这事得依我的意思。”

于是第四个夏天，斯蒂尔比约恩随帕尔纳托基和约姆斯堡大军出航劫掠。阔湾勇士比约恩·阿斯布兰德松与他同船共事，众人眼见这两个人的情义与日俱增，越发牢固。他与布伊北上比亚尔马兰、深入基里亚拉兰的未知之地时，比约恩也在船上——把他们引到那里去的，是众人口中关于那片土地的种种传说：说基里亚拉兰只住着芬兰人与变身者，那些东西并不永远是一个形状，也未必真在它们看去所在的地方；又说基里亚拉兰人供奉一尊大名鼎鼎的神像，名唤约马拉，腰间束一条银带；神庙坐落在林中幽暗处，满林是巨怪与恶灵，人若没有约马拉的眷顾而敢闯进去，光凭那份惊骇就足以把人吓得魂飞魄散。斯蒂尔比约恩一行到了那里，倒真没遇上什么巨怪变身者，却洗劫了约马拉的神庙，夺下他腰间那条带子——那带子的式样世人从未见过，上头盘绕纠结着水怪大蛇的纹样；此外还掳获了巨量白银与珍宝——如此一天之内、一处地方所得的横财，谁也记不起可曾有人见过。


===Original===
Besides Biorn, Styrbiorn had now more than three score men who were his shipmates always and followed him as a picked bodyguard in every warlike enterprise he took in hand. The more part of these had been many years vikings in Jom, as Bessi Thorlakson, Thorolf Attercop, Howard the Hewer, and Alf Braggart: others, as Gunnstein Lowry, were of his first following out of Sweden before he came to Jomsburg: other some he had taken to in many lands, men who had first felt the might of him as their conqueror in battle and were now the closer bound to his love and service, lords and princes of the east, Valdimar, the great Prince’s son of Holmgarth; Ere-Skeggi, that was young brother to an earl in Estland; Olaf the Wend, and many more. And like as Palnatoki by the mastery of his mere presence kept peaceable all those overweening men of war as many as followed in those days the Lay of the Jomsburgers, so in his own ship’s company Styrbiorn held in comradeship men of far diverse speech and blood, and so proud withal and quarrelsome of nature that, but for him, they had fallen daily like wolves each at another’s throat. So Styrbiorn made great warfare eastaway, and fought both by sea and land and subdued many warlike peoples that withstood him long time, but ever in the end Styrbiorn had the victory, and much wealth they took there.


===Chinese===
除比约恩外，斯蒂尔比约恩如今还有六十多名始终同船的伙伴，每逢战事，便作为挑选的亲随跟定他。这些人多半在约姆做过多年维京人，如贝西·索拉克松、“毒蛛”索罗尔夫、劈砍手霍华德、吹牛皮的阿尔夫；另有如贡斯泰因·洛里，是他出瑞典、初来约姆斯堡之前的老部下；还有一些，是他在各处地方收服的——这些人起初是在战场上领教过他作为征服者的威力的，如今反而更紧地系在他的情义与麾下：东方的君长王孙，如霍姆加德大公之子瓦尔德马尔，埃斯特兰一位伯爵的幼弟埃雷-斯凯吉，文德人奥拉夫，等等。一如帕尔纳托基仅凭他本人的威仪，便镇得那些年月里凡遵约姆斯堡会规行事的骄兵悍将个个俯首；斯蒂尔比约恩在自己船班之中，也一样能让言语血统各殊、生性高傲好斗的汉子们亲如袍泽——若没有他，这些人怕不天天像恶狼一般彼此咬喉。于是斯蒂尔比约恩在东方大张兵威，海陆并战，降服了诸多长年顽抗的尚武之邦；但到头来胜利总归斯蒂尔比约恩，他们也在那里发了大财。


===Original===
But when it wanted now but five weeks of winter, Styrbiorn busked him for his faring into Sweden. He deemed it not good that he should come home this time with too great a force of ships nor too many men along with him, lest the King his uncle should think that showed unfriendly, as if he, to whom the King had plighted his faith solemnly and obliged himself to grant him kingdom now and his father’s heritage, should come in a manner to misuse his favourer and claim it with drawn sword. Howso, there fared with him Bessi Thorlakson and Gunnstein and others of lesser note: ten ships in all. Biorn fared not this time with him into Sweden, but being taken with a sickness abode in Jomsburg. But the more part of the Jomsburgers held the sea still, for it was not their way to leave harrying till winter set in.

Styrbiorn and his men sailed north with a fair wind, and in good time came through the Low and went ashore at Sigtun. And there was Eric the King with a great company ridden down to Sigtun to welcome Styrbiorn. The King, when he saw him come aland, lighted down from his horse and made haste to meet him, and Styrbiorn came striding quickly over the big stones and up the shelving rough ground of the firth’s margin, and they gripped hands and stood so a minute, and then the King drew Styrbiorn to him and embraced and kissed him. Styrbiorn had grown and waxed mightily even in these six months since he came before to Sweden, and albeit King Eric overpassed most other men in greatness of growth, yet men marked now that even with the advantage of the ground (for the King stood on the slope and Styrbiorn below him towards the waterside) the King’s eyes looked but level with Styrbiorn’s, and the limbs of Styrbiorn seemed greater than the King’s and his breadth of shoulder broader and his chest deeper. Men that watched them at that meeting said that never had they seen very father and son fainer of one another than those two. And certain it is, there was no child of his own body that King Eric set such store by as he set by Styrbiorn.


===Chinese===
离入冬只剩五个星期时，斯蒂尔比约恩整装预备启程赴瑞典。他心想，这回回国，不宜带过多的船队人马，免得叔父王多心，以为他不怀好意——仿佛王既已郑重立誓、答应现在就授予他王国和他父亲的产业，他却偏要剑拔弩张地来逼恩人兑现。话虽如此，随他同行的仍有贝西·索拉克松、贡斯泰因和另外几个名声稍逊的，共计十船。比约恩这次没有随他进瑞典，害了病，留在约姆斯堡。至于约姆斯堡人的大半，则仍留在海上：不到冬深，他们从不住手劫掠。

斯蒂尔比约恩一行乘着顺风北上，及时穿过低泽，在西格图纳登岸。埃里克王已率大队人马驰到西格图纳迎候。王见他上岸，翻身下马，快步迎上前去；斯蒂尔比约恩跨过大块卵石、踏上峡湾边倾斜的碎石坡，大步流星地迎去。两人紧握双手，如此站立了一分钟，随后王将斯蒂尔比约恩拉进怀里，拥抱亲吻。距上回来瑞典不过半年，斯蒂尔比约恩又长高了、长壮了；虽说埃里克王的身量超出常人，可众人都看得分明：即便占了地势的便宜（王站在坡上，斯蒂尔比约恩在坡下临水一边），王的眼光也才勉强与斯蒂尔比约恩平齐，而斯蒂尔比约恩的四肢似乎比王的更粗壮，肩膀更宽，胸膛更厚。在场看着他们相聚的人都说，从未见过哪对亲父子有这两个人这般亲热。的确，埃里克王自己的骨肉，没有一个像器重斯蒂尔比约恩这样被他看重。


===Original===
So now was all made ready, and the ships drawn up aland, and the gear packed on wains and a-horseback, both their own stuff and many good gifts which Styrbiorn had brought from Jomsburg to give unto the King his uncle, and they took horse and rode all in company northward towards Upsala.

Styrbiorn rode hand by hand with the King. They talked of this and that. Styrbiorn was somewhat silent and ever again let fall their talk, as if there were something he was eager to hear but would not himself speak first of. As they rode on, and the King still spake of this and that but naught of the main matter, Styrbiorn waxed yet the more ill at ease and yet shorter of speech; but he held himself in well. The King, who saw all this very plainly, made no haste to end it. At length, when their journey was three parts done and they rode up over the brow of a little wooded knoll of ground which had the top as it were a table of the native rock bare as a man’s hand above the trees, whence they looked north over the vast uplandish country and wood and water and might see, some miles off, the houses and temple at Upsala, the King drew rein and said to him, “Three weeks hence hath a Thing been called whereat thou shalt be taken to King in Sweden, along with me, even as was thy father aforetime.”


===Chinese===
于是诸事齐备：船只拖上岸，辎重装上牛车马背——既有他们自家的物件，也有斯蒂尔比约恩从约姆斯堡带回、准备献给叔父王的许多厚礼。众人上马，结伴北行，往乌普萨拉去。

斯蒂尔比约恩与王并辔而行。两人闲话种种。斯蒂尔比约恩有些沉默，几次三番把话头撂下，仿佛有什么事急于听王开口，自己又不肯先提。一路行去，王东拉西扯，偏不涉正题，斯蒂尔比约恩越发不安，话越发少，却始终按捺得住。王把这些看在眼里，故意不忙了结。最后，行程走过四分之三，两人骑上一座小树林丘的顶——丘顶天然岩石平坦如桌，光秃秃高过树梢——从那里向北俯望，高旷的原野、林莽与川湖尽收眼底，数里之外，乌普萨拉的屋宇和神庙已然在望。王勒住缰绳，对他说：“三个星期之后，将召开国民会议，届时你将与我一同在瑞典受立为王，一如当年的你父。”


===Original===
“Have my thanks for that, Lord,” said Styrbiorn, and took the King’s hand in his. The King was looking on him, but he on Upsala. And the King marked how the whole port and bearing of him was altered now, and how the face of him, that was clouded, was bright now like the land’s face at point of day.

They rode on. After a time the King began to say, “That were good now, Styrbiorn, now that thou art come to man’s estate and takest up kingdom, that thou shouldest take a wife. Or what thinkest thou of that?”


===Chinese===
“多谢主上。”斯蒂尔比约恩说着，伸手握住王的手。王望着他，他却望着乌普萨拉。王看得分明：他的整个神情举止骤然变了，那张方才还阴郁的脸，此刻豁然明亮，如破晓时分的大地。

两人继续赶路。行了一程，王开口道：“如今好了，斯蒂尔比约恩，你既已成年，又将承接王位，就该娶一房妻室了。你自己怎么说？”


===Original===
Styrbiorn answered, “I have thought on’t. And ’tis most needful.”

“Doth thine eye rest anywhere that seemeth good to thee for this?” asked the King. “Thou hast fared abroad in many lands these three years, and I never yet heard tell of so bad a land that it bred not a fair woman or twain.”

Styrbiorn laughed. “That is true enough, King. Yet I know not. For a wife﻿—”

“ ’Tis two matters: to have, and to keep?” said the King.

“I have found none yet,” said Styrbiorn.


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩答道：“这事我想过。确是少不得的。”

“你可曾看中过什么人？”王问，“这三年你走遍外邦诸国，再坏的国土，我还没听说生不出一两个美人的。”

斯蒂尔比约恩笑了。“这话不假，王。可我说不好。至于妻子——”

“这原是两回事：到手，和留得住？”王说。

“我至今没有遇上。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。


===Original===
“I will tell thee,” said the King, “what hath been in my mind. There is King Harald Gormson hath a daughter, Thyri. That were a match many kings would deem them lucky might they get her. The maid is fair, too, as men tell me, and good-mannered, and of fourteen winters old.”

Styrbiorn said, “I knew her, when I was there a-guesting last Yuletide.”

“Well?” said the King.

“I like her well. But I had not thought on’t,” answered Styrbiorn.

“If she should please thee, that should please me well,” said King Eric. “But it is for thine own choosing.”

Styrbiorn said, “I had not thought on’t. But here, as in other things, your rede shall be mine, Lord.”


===Chinese===
“我告诉你我心里是怎么打算的，”王说，“丹麦哈拉尔王·戈姆松有个女儿，蒂丽。多少王侯若能娶到她，都要自认走运。听说那姑娘人也标致，知书识礼，今年十四岁。”

斯蒂尔比约恩说：“去年尤尔节我在那里做客，见过她。”

“觉得如何？”王说。

“我很喜欢她。只是没往这上头想过。”斯蒂尔比约恩答道。

“你若中意她，我便再高兴不过，”埃里克王说，“不过主意得你自己拿。”

斯蒂尔比约恩说：“我是没想过。可在这件事上，也同别的事一样，主上，你的主意便是我的主意。”


===Original===
When they were come home to the King’s house in Upsala there was great welcoming of Styrbiorn, wherein was none more forward to take him by the hand than Helgi and Thorgisl and Thorir, they saying many times the same thing till he was something weary of it: that there was not a man in the realm of Sweden but was blithe and glad to see Styrbiorn come again into the land and would be fain of him as King and would back him and further him every way they might. Thorgnyr welcomed him with such warmth and eagerness as a curst old ban-dog or mastiff might welcome a stranger withal whom his master will entertain but himself would gladly grip his teeth in him if none else were by to hinder it. The Queen greeted him coldlier than for their old friendliness might have been looked for. She said little, came little in his company, went soon away whenever they chanced together, yet would still be looking on him when she saw her time and neither he nor any other could observe it.


===Chinese===
回到乌普萨拉王宅，迎接斯蒂尔比约恩的场面盛大热烈，其中最抢先来握他手的，莫过于海尔吉、索尔吉尔和托里尔三个。同一句话他们说了又说，直教他听得发腻：瑞典全国上下，人人喜见斯蒂尔比约恩重回故土，人人愿奉他为王，人人愿倾力扶持帮衬。松格尼尔迎接他的那副热络劲儿，活像一条恶老看家狗或獒犬迎接生客——主人要款待这客，它自家却巴不得咬进他肉里，只碍于有人在场不好下手。王后接待他，却比照旧日的情分冷淡得多。她话少，少与他同处，偶然碰在一起，也早早走开；可一瞅准时机，她仍旧要望着他，趁他不知、旁人也不觉的时候。


===Original===
Oft would the King take Styrbiorn and show him all that was there, both old and new, and among other good things the old house of the King his father, King Olaf’s, where he dwelt when he was yet alive and where Styrbiorn was born; and King Eric had let mend and dight it within and without, both the main hall and the chambers thereof, and fit it with new hangings and all kind of gear: “And this shall be thine own house, Styrbiorn, to dwell in when thou takest kingdom. And I think it is no worser an house than mine own.” Styrbiorn showed the King the great sword that hung at his thigh, the same which the King had given him when he went first abroad three years ago. The King asked if that were a good sword. Styrbiorn said it was the best of swords. He said he had had no other sword with him in battle all these years, and it was better now than at the first. He said it seemed to him that if he should lose that sword he should lose all his good luck. “It is like enough,” said the King, “that there is somewhat of me in that sword, who gave it thee. And like enough my good will goeth with it, and maketh it do good for thee.”


===Chinese===
王时常带着斯蒂尔比约恩，遍观那里的一切，新新旧旧都看。诸般好处之中，有一处是他父王奥拉夫王的故宅——奥拉夫王在世时居住的地方，也是斯蒂尔比约恩出生的地方；埃里克王已命人里里外外修缮布置一新，正厅与各间居室都配了新帷幔、新陈设。“等你即位，这宅子便归你自己住，斯蒂尔比约恩。我看它不比我那座差。”斯蒂尔比约恩给王看他悬在大腿旁的那柄大剑，正是三年前他初次出海时王所赐。王问这是不是柄好剑。斯蒂尔比约恩说，这是剑中最好的剑。他说这些年临阵，从未用过第二柄剑，如今它比当初更好了。他说，在他看来，若失了这柄剑，他一生的好运也就失了。“那很可以，”王说，“这剑既是我给你的，里头便有几分我的魂魄。多半是我的好意随剑同行，才教它处处为你出力。”


===Original===
The King sent Earl Wolf now in embassage to King Harald Gormson to bespeak his daughter in marriage for Styrbiorn. King Harald at first boggled at this, saying she was promised to the Wend-King. Yet in the end he was fain to own that this was yet but talked on and naught settled nor agreed yet; and when he saw how much King Eric was set upon the match, and bethought him too that the Swede-King’s friendship should profit him well, and that Styrbiorn withal was an ill foe to quarrel with, and that Jomsburg lay at his own door too, in the end he gave Earl Wolf the answer he would have, and promised to send her to Upsala with as short delay as might be. And yet it was with little good will that he consented in this; for there weighed ever on his mind (like as weigheth a surfeit of tough meat on the belly that cannot deal with it) the memory of Styrbiorn’s lording it over him last winter in Roiskeld, and of his saying (albeit in jest) that he would do of him and the Dane-realm as he should think fit.


===Chinese===
王随即派沃尔夫伯爵出使丹麦哈拉尔王·戈姆松处，为斯蒂尔比约恩求聘他的女儿。哈拉尔王起初支吾推诿，说她已许给文德国王。但末了他也不得不承认，那桩婚事不过风闻商谈，并未定局；待他见埃里克王如此属意这门亲事，又想到瑞典王的交情于他大有益处，想到斯蒂尔比约恩这个仇家万万结不得，还想到约姆斯堡就近在他的家门口——到头来，他还是给了沃尔夫伯爵如愿的答复，答应尽快把女儿送到乌普萨拉。只是他应允得老大不情愿：去年冬天斯蒂尔比约恩在罗斯基勒骑在他头上发号施令的情景，还有他说的那些话——虽是玩笑——说他要把他连同丹麦国一并随意处置——始终压在他心头，好比一堆嚼不烂的韧肉，积在一个化不动的肚肠里。


===Original===
It was not long before every man that was there in Upsala knew in his bones what manner of lord they had gotten now in Styrbiorn. And they saw very well that he had slipped his neck out of the collar, and was King already in deed, and bare him like a King and let all feel the weight of the might of him. And they marked too how those two, the King and Styrbiorn, were ever in company and were so glad of each other that it was a wonder to see; and most men praised these things and deemed well of their boding, but some wagged their heads in secret.

Only Thorgnyr came on an evening to the King, if he might sway him yet afore it should be too late. The King let him say his say. “You have heard, King,” said that old man, “all that I have heard: you have seen what I see. He is altered not a whit. In Holmgarth and the east he hath overborne all. Jomsburg he holdeth in his hand. He hath browbeat the Dane-King in his stead, and gone forth of the Dane-realm with threats and scornings. He is tied to no place, but all are tied to him. Save you only, there is none here in Sweden he entreateth in other wise than as his thrall or bondman. Give him all the world else, King, but give him not Sweden.”

The King heard him out with so much good temper and patience it was a strange thing to see; then told him kindly ’twas but sawing in the air, since as touching his taking of Styrbiorn into kingdom his mind was set, and not Thorgnyr nor the Swede-folk nor all the Northlands might avail to shift it. Thorgnyr spake no more of that.


===Chinese===
没过多久，身在乌普萨拉的每一个人都从骨子里明白，他们如今迎来的斯蒂尔比约恩是怎样一位主子。众人看得分明：他已挣脱了套在颈上的轭，实际上早已是王——举手投足俨然王者，让人人领受他威权的分量。他们也看得分明：那两个人，王与斯蒂尔比约恩，形影不离，彼此亲热得教人称奇；多数人称道这些，以为是吉兆，有些人却暗暗摇头。

唯独松格尼尔，一晚来见王，想趁为时未晚再劝他一回。王由他把话说完。“王啊，”老人说，“我所听见的，你都听见了：我所看见的，你都看见了。他分毫未改。在霍姆加德和东方，他压倒了一切。约姆斯堡攥在他手心。他在丹麦王自家的座上挫辱了丹麦王，扬着威胁与讥嘲出了丹麦国境。他不系于任何地方，任何地方都系于他。除了你，瑞典这里没有一人，他不当自家奴仆佃农使唤的。王啊，把全世界都给他都使得，只是别把瑞典给他。”

王耐着性子听他讲究，那份和气耐烦，看去竟觉稀奇；随后和颜告诉他，这不过是白费唇舌：关于立斯蒂尔比约恩为王这件事，他心意已决，松格尼尔不能，瑞典百姓不能，整个北国也不能叫他挪动分毫。松格尼尔便不再提这事。


===Original===
Well nigh every day Styrbiorn would still divert himself with wrastling and sporting with Moldi, that he had left with a thrall of his named Erland to tend for him whiles he fared abroad. Moldi was now come to his full growth: not big as other oxen, but exceeding sturdy and heavy. He was something cross-grained in his temper, and the thralls durst not touch him save when he was in the mood for it. But he knew Styrbiorn when he first came home, and ran to him as soon as he saw him, which was a wonder after these years. And albeit there was no man else might handle him except in his own time, with Styrbiorn he was as gentle as a little calf might be.

On a day while Styrbiorn was a-wrastling with Moldi on Upsala brink, Sigrid the Queen stood and looked on their sport even as she had looked on it when he wrastled with Moldi on Olaf’s howe that morning after Aki’s slaying. When their bout was over and Styrbiorn stood up breathing hard, saw the Queen and gave her greeting, she said, “Thy little ox is grown too big for thee now, for all that men have given thee that by-name of Styrbiorn the Strong. It is only to please thee he letteth thee get the upper hand of him. If he would, he could toss thee off like a ball.”


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩出海期间，把莫尔迪托给一个名叫埃尔兰的奴隶照看；如今几乎每天，他仍要同莫尔迪角力戏耍为乐。莫尔迪如今已长足了个头：不似别的公牛那般高大，却异常结实沉重。它脾气有些别扭，奴隶们不敢碰它，除非它自家来了兴致。可斯蒂尔比约恩才回家，它一眼便认了出来，一见就奔了过去——隔了这些年还这样，真是奇事。虽说除它自家高兴之外谁也近身不得，在斯蒂尔比约恩手里，它却温驯得像头小牛犊。

一日，斯蒂尔比约恩正在乌普萨拉崖畔与莫尔迪角力，西格丽德王后站在一旁观战，一如阿基被杀那天早上，他在奥拉夫王的坟冢上与莫尔迪角力时她望着的那样。一场角罢，斯蒂尔比约恩站起身大口喘气，看见王后，向她问安。她说：“你这头小公牛如今长得太大，你制不住了——尽管人家给了你‘强者斯蒂尔比约恩’这个诨名。它是讨你喜欢，才让你占它上风。它若认真起来，能把你像颗球一样抛上天。”


===Original===
“Why,” said Styrbiorn, laughing, “I think thou’dst not say so if thou couldst have felt the push of him as I did now.”

“ ’Tis the same,” said Sigrid, “with other folk, and not thy little ox only. They must still be pleasing thee, and serving thy turn. I think it is not good for thee.”

“I know not,” said Styrbiorn, rubbing his face against Moldi’s soft grey hairy muzzle. “What think’st thou on’t, Moldi?”

“So thou weddest Thyri?” said the Queen after some pause.

“So it is,” said he.


===Chinese===
“嗨，”斯蒂尔比约恩笑道，“你要是方才像我一样挨过它这一撞，怕就不会这么说了。”

“别的那些人，”西格丽德说，“也是一样，不光是你这头小公牛。他们个个都得讨你喜欢、供你驱遣。我看这于你没有好处。”

“这个我不知道。”斯蒂尔比约恩说着，把脸贴上莫尔迪那柔软的灰绒鼻吻蹭了蹭。“莫尔迪，你说呢？”

沉吟片刻，王后说：“这么说，你要娶蒂丽了？”

“是这样。”他说。


===Original===
“Thou hadst not the King thine uncle to do thy wooing for thee there,” said she.

“He sent my foster-father,” said Styrbiorn.

“The King thine uncle is a good wooer,” said Queen Sigrid. “Yet ere this he hath gotten no for an answer.”

Styrbiorn said, “Never that I heard tell of.”

“There be things yet in the world that thou hast not heard tell of,” said she, “for all thou knowest so much.”


===Chinese===
“那时你可没有叔父王替你去求亲。”她说。

“他派了我的养父去。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。

“你叔父王是求亲的好手，”西格丽德王后说，“只是这回之前，他从没吃到过回绝。”

“我没听说过这样的事。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。

“世上还有你没听说过的事呢，”她说，“凭你知道得再多，也一样。”


===Original===
Styrbiorn said, “Little wit in a maid to say no to him.”

“I will tell thee,” said Sigrid. “It was I said no to him. But I will tell thee why I said no. It was because I thought he would woo me not for his own hand, but for another.” So saying she looked swiftly at him; then, turning her eyes away, “I must learn thee somewhat in court terms,” said she. “Thou shouldst now say: ’Tis a good wooer picketh wisely the best for himself.”

Styrbiorn said, “Thou wouldst ever be mocking at me, Sigrid.”

Sigrid reached out her hand to Moldi to lick. He licked hard and eagerly the hand and wrist and up the fair white arm of her, nuzzling back her sleeve with his sweet-breathing nose. She drew back her arm with a little shudder, then put her hand out again to fondle and play with his woolly jowl. “Thou and Thyri,” she said, after a pause, “will be well matched. She hath ugly hair, I am told, but is very meek, and will do all thy bidding and say naught.”

Styrbiorn was silent. But the Queen with her eyes bent still on Moldi, not on Styrbiorn, would still be talking. “I am in a manner thine aunt,” said she. “I will give thee some wise counsel out of mine own wisdom. When thou art wed to Thyri, keep not wish-wives and bondmaids, o’ thine uncle’s fashion. And this I counsel thee for thine own comfort, kinsman Styrbiorn, not thy wife’s. For doth it not seem to thee both just and fair: if one woman be not enough, why then must one man be?” With that she thrust Moldi from her, turned swiftly, and was suddenly gone.


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩说：“哪个姑娘回绝他，才是没脑子。”

“我告诉你吧，”西格丽德说，“回绝他的正是我。可我也告诉你我为什么回绝。因为我想，他求我，不是为他自己，是为另一个人。”说这话时她飞快地瞥了他一眼；随即又把目光移开。“我得教你学学宫廷辞令，”她说，“此刻你该说：会挑最好的留给自己的，才是求亲的高手。”

斯蒂尔比约恩说：“西格丽德，你总是拿我打趣。”

西格丽德伸手让莫尔迪舔。它舔得又急又用力，舔她的手、她的腕，一路舔上她雪白的小臂，用它气息香甜的鼻子拱开她的衣袖。她微微一颤，缩回手臂，随即又伸出手，抚弄它那毛茸茸的腮颊，同它玩耍。“你和蒂丽，”她歇了片刻说，“倒是般配。听说她头发生得丑，性子却极温顺，样样依你，一声不吭。”

斯蒂尔比约恩默然。王后眼睛仍旧望着莫尔迪，不望斯蒂尔比约恩，嘴里却说个不停。“论名分我算你的婶娘，”她说，“我要拿自家的一点智慧送你几句忠告。你娶了蒂丽之后，别学你叔父那样子，外头养情妇、玩使女。我这话，亲戚斯蒂尔比约恩，是为你自己安逸着想，不是为你妻子。你想，这话岂不公道：一个女人若不够，一个男人又何必够？”说完，她把莫尔迪一推，倏然转身，眨眼便不见了。


===Original===
Styrbiorn stood a minute looking after her as she went with swift and sure steps, daintily swaying from the hips, daintily gathering the skirt of her purple broidered gown to clear her ankles. As he so stood watching her, he was ware of Moldi that was nuzzling close to him now, busily licking his hand and arm. Suddenly he withdrew his arm, even as the Queen had hers, with a little shudder.


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩伫立片刻，目送她走去：脚步轻快而稳，从胯处袅袅摇曳，又袅袅地拢起紫绣长裙的下摆，露出脚踝。他正这样望着，忽觉莫尔迪偎了过来，正忙着舔他的手和手臂。他蓦地抽回手臂——同王后方才一模一样——微微打了个颤。

## IX / 九

===Original===
A Banquet in Upsala

King Eric made ready now a great feast of many days, and summoned a Thing to be holden in the middle term thereof, at which Thing he was minded to make over and give unto Styrbiorn, his brother’s son, with all lawful ceremonies and before the face of the Swedes in lawful Thing assembled, that half share of the kingly power in Sweden which King Olaf had held aforetime. And the feast was in honour as well of this greater matter as of Styrbiorn’s betrothal unto Thyri the daughter of King Harald Gormson. Thyri was come now out of Denmark with a great and honourable company to bring her home to Upsala. The folk when they saw her deemed well of her and praised her beauty. That she loved Styrbiorn was clear as day, and men thought the two of them must have laid their plans for this in Denmark when Styrbiorn was with the King her father last winter.


===Chinese===
乌普萨拉的一场盛宴

如今埃里克王筹备一场连日的大宴，并传召国民会议，定在会期正中举行；他有意在这次会上，以一切合法仪节、当着依法与会的瑞典人的面，把从前奥拉夫王所掌有的那一份瑞典王权，转让授予他的侄子斯蒂尔比约恩。这场盛宴，一是为这件大事，二是为斯蒂尔比约恩与丹麦哈拉尔王·戈姆松之女蒂丽订婚。蒂丽已从丹麦启程，随行是一支盛大而尊贵的送亲队伍，前来乌普萨拉。众人见了她，都甚有好感，称赞她的美貌。她爱慕斯蒂尔比约恩，明白得如同白昼；众人都想，这两个必定是去年冬天斯蒂尔比约恩在她父亲王处做客时，便在丹麦把这门亲事商量定了的。


===Original===
The third night of their feasting was a banquet holden in the King’s hall, and before that banquet was the troth-plighting of Styrbiorn and Thyri performed with due ceremony, and the banquet was the banquet of their betrothal, but the wedding should be after the Thing, when Styrbiorn should have taken kingdom.

Eric the King was set in his high seat on the upper bench, and the Queen at his right hand. Styrbiorn sat in the high seat on the lower bench over against the King, and Thyri his betrothed sat at his side. There was at that banquet every lord and man of mark that was of greatest account in all the land of the Swedes, both Earls and landed men and the King’s counsellors and friends and the great men of his household, and the wives and kinswomen of these, and Styrbiorn’s men that followed him out of Jomsburg: in all, so great a press of noble persons and those of their following who made shift to find place in every nook and corner of the hall, that never was so great a throng of folk gathered together in the King’s house, and the thralls and serving men had much ado to serve meat and drink to all that company.


===Chinese===
连日欢宴的第三夜，王的大厅里设下大宴；宴前，斯蒂尔比约恩与蒂丽依礼行了订婚之仪，这场大宴便是二人的订婚宴。至于婚礼，则在国民会议之后、斯蒂尔比约恩受立为王时举行。

埃里克王端坐上席高座，王后坐在他右手。斯蒂尔比约恩坐在对面下席的高座上，他的未婚妻蒂丽坐在他身旁。赴宴的，有瑞典全境最显要的每一位贵族名流：伯爵、领主、王的谋臣故旧、王室的大员，还有他们的妻女眷属，以及随斯蒂尔比约恩从约姆斯堡来的部众——总之，济济一堂的贵人连同各自的随从，把大厅的每一个角落旮旯都挤满了；王的宅邸里从未聚过这么多的人，奴隶仆役端酒上菜，忙得脚不沾地。


===Original===
Helgi and Thorgisl and Thorir were sat cheek by jowl on the upper bench at the end nighest the door. They liked well of the good eats and drinks and made game together. They spoke softly so as they should not be overheard.

Thorgisl said, “Well fares he that sits quiet with his own. Sith Styrbiorn is come into kingdom now in our despite, let us drink and be glad and kick at the pricks no more.”

“That is well said,” said Helgi. “And yet, it dislikes me he should hold his chin so high, sitting there so glad, with his backside rooted in yonder high seat as though he were King already. If he be puffed up now so big, who shall abide to live under him when he shall be King indeed?”

“He thinketh,” said Thorgisl, “on the Swede realm, which now lieth loose before him.”

“Well,” said Thorir, “so the ale runneth trill-lill down the throat, what need grieve us?”


===Chinese===
海尔吉、索尔吉尔和托里尔三个挨肩坐在上席靠门的尽头。他们对美酒佳肴颇为受用，一路说笑取乐。他们说话声音压得低低的，生怕叫人听见。

索尔吉尔说：“守住自家份内、安坐不动的人有福了。如今斯蒂尔比约恩到底不顾我们反对做了王，咱们便喝酒行乐，别再拿脚跟去踢刺棒了。”

“这话在理。”海尔吉说。“只是我看不惯他那样昂着下巴，坐在那儿欢天喜地，屁股像生了根似地扎在那高座上，俨然已经是王。他如今就这般趾高气扬，等他真做了王，谁还受得了在他底下过活？”

“他心里想的，”索尔吉尔说，“是瑞典的江山——如今正敞开在他面前呢。”

“得了，”托里尔说，“只要麦酒还滴滴溜溜顺着嗓子往下灌，咱们愁个什么劲？”


===Original===
Helgi drank and said, “None shall deny that Styrbiorn hath a pretty piece of flesh o’ the right hand side of him. Would I were nearer, to mark if, spite o’ that, his eye stray not toward the upper bench.”

“A point eastward o’ the King?” said Thorir: and they laughed. “ ’Tis not every man loveth the same meat, though, Helgi. I have marked the King look seldomer tonight of’s own right hand than thereaway, to the cross-bench.”

“How? there?” said Helgi, craning forward over the board, and scanning the other women where they sat, seen dimly fair through the flicker and reek of fire and torchlight. “I see,” he said, sitting back again and wiping the mead-froth from his moustachios with the back of his hand. His eyes wandered back toward the cross-bench: they glittered, and he ran his tongue over his lips.

“ ’Tis not every man hath his pick of suchlike morsels,” said Thorgisl. “He that had his pick, and picked not, should be a fool, to my thinking.”

Thorir took him round the neck and said in his ear. “Helgi will not be slow to pick there, when his turn cometh: and little blame to him, think’st thou?”


===Chinese===
海尔吉喝了口酒，说：“谁也不能否认，斯蒂尔比约恩右手边坐着个标致人儿。可惜离得远，看不出他的眼睛会不会撇下她，往咱们这上席瞟。”

“往王座偏东瞧？”托里尔说；众人都笑了。“不过海尔吉，各人爱的肉不一样。我倒留意到，今晚王的眼睛往自家右手边瞧得少了，反倒常往横席那边去。”

“怎么？那边？”海尔吉说着，探身越过桌案，眯眼打量坐在那边的女眷——火光火炬明明灭灭，烟气腾腾，她们看去影影绰绰，别有一番朦胧的美。“明白了。”他说着坐回去，用手背抹去髭须上的蜜酒沫。他的眼睛又溜回横席那头：目光发亮，舌头舔了舔嘴唇。

“这样的口福不是人人都挑得着的，”索尔吉尔说。“依我看，挑得着却不挑的，才是傻子。”

托里尔搂住他的脖子，凑在他耳边说：“等轮到海尔吉挑的时候，他手脚不会慢的：这也怪不得他，你说是不是？”


===Original===
“Is it any one?” said Thorgisl.

“Art thou blind?” said Thorir. “She, third from the end: the King’s latest bondmaid. O’ the Erse King’s blood, men say. If black women be ugly, there’s argument for thee that foul is fair.”

“Black women’s the fashion tonight,” said Thorgisl.

Helgi was yet licking his lips and shifting uneasily in his seat, his eyes yet on the black-haired damosel on the cross-bench. Looking round and seeing his comrades’ laughing gaze upon him, he squared his shoulders, crammed his mouth with mutton, hailed the cupbearer for more drink, and said through his munchings, “Women’s but cattle: one’s like another, when all’s said and done.”

“So much the better for thee,” said Thorir; “for I think thou’lt have to wait a weary while for this one.”

Helgi drank and spat. “O, the trolls take thee and thy talk,” he said.


===Chinese===
“说的是哪一个？”索尔吉尔说。

“你瞎了么？”托里尔说。“喏，那头数第三个：王新近收的使女。据说有爱尔兰王的血统。黑发女人若是丑，这一位就教你明白：丑的也是美的。”

“今晚时兴黑发女人。”索尔吉尔说。

海尔吉还舔着嘴唇，在座位上不安地挪动，眼睛仍黏在横席上那个黑发姑娘身上。他环眼一望，见伙伴们含笑都盯着自己，便挺了挺肩膀，往嘴里塞了一大块羊肉，扬声唤斟酒人再来一杯，一面嚼一面含混地说：“女人不过是牲口：说到底，一个跟另一个都一样。”

“那对你再好不过，”托里尔说，“因为依我看，想轮到这一个，你可有的等了。”

海尔吉喝了一口，啐了一口。“呸，叫巨魔把你和你这张嘴一块儿收了去。”他说。


===Original===
“Thorgnyr receiveth all this wondrous calm,” said Thorgisl after a time. “What if we have but mistook him all this while?”

“Never think it,” said Helgi, looking up the table to where that old man sat, near the King. “Sometimes the sea will moan in a calm. I hear it now, methinks.”


===Chinese===
“松格尼尔对这一切安静得出奇，”过了一会儿索尔吉尔说，“会不会这些日子咱们一直看错了人？”

“休要这么想，”海尔吉说着，目光沿宴席上望，望到那老人挨着王坐的地方。“风平浪静的时候，海有时也会呜咽。这会儿，我像是听见它在呜咽了。”


===Original===
The hall was hung with outlandish hangings, curtains of rich crimson stuff and cloth of gold and broidered work brought out of Micklegarth or Garthrealm or the Western Isles, plunder of war or gifts of peace and tribute made to Styrbiorn by kings and lords that he had brought under him; and these treasures he had given now to the King his uncle, so many and rich and goodly as had not heretofore been seen, not one tenth the number nor goodliness, in the realm of Sweden. And there were plates of gold and great cups and breakers and goblets of gold and silver, rough with jewels and beautifully enchased, that made the rude, smoke-darkened and ponderous boards of the long tables blossom, like the brown earth in spring, with shining splendours. But the magnificence and glory of that banquet had root not in the fair setting only of gold and jewels and gorgeous tissues and weapons hung on the walls and armour glinting, but in the living presence and splendour of the men and women that feasted in that pomp: Eric the King, his youth come back upon him, eating and drinking and making merry, oftenest with his eye on Styrbiorn that sat there over against him in the beauty of his youth and strength; and that young affianced bride of Styrbiorn’s, white-skinned and with night-dark hair, quiet, with eyes and ears only for him; and opposite, on the King’s right hand, that fair young Queen of his. She too was quiet. The lids drooped over her dark eyes that looked out like some animal’s eyes, profound and of doubtful import, under shadowing lashes, unsounded pools. Her face was flushed. Her red-gold hair, swept back in heavy shimmering and luxurious masses, shadowed her brow on either side, and was gathered and coiled again under strings of jewels darkly sparkling. Her gown was of silk, a treasure of untold price out of the land of the Greeks, deep purple and purfled with gold. There was a strange and disturbing grace in her quiet pose and carriage, as if the fair and lovely body of her with the burning fire of its beauty pierced the rich attire that hid it, giving to every silken fold and to every glittering gem a warm and breathing loveliness as of very flesh and blood. And yet her lord, sitting there at handreach, had eyes and mind, as it appeared, only for his nephew, if it were not for a sidelong glance now and again at that newest damosel of his on the cross-bench. Styrbiorn for his part had eyes for all, merry of heart and at ease and at peace, as for that while, with all the world.


===Chinese===
大厅里挂满了异域的帷幔：绛红的华贵织物、金线锦缎和绣品，有的是从米克加德、加德王国或西方诸岛带来的，战争的掳获，和平的赠礼——都是那些臣服于斯蒂尔比约恩的王公君长进献的贡礼；如今他把这些珍宝献给了叔父王，数量之多、品色之富丽，瑞典王国此前从未见过，连十分之一也不曾有过。还有黄金的盘盏、大杯、角杯、金银的高脚杯，嵌满宝石，雕工精美，教长桌上粗笨乌黑的厚木板面，像春天褐色的大地一般，开出一片晃眼的华彩。然而这场宴会的富丽堂皇，根子并不全在金宝珠玉、绫罗绸缎与壁上兵器的寒光甲胄的陈设，而在赴宴男女活生生的丰采：埃里克王返老还童，吃喝取乐，眼光时时落在他对面那位正当青春力盛之美的斯蒂尔比约恩身上；斯蒂尔比约恩那位年轻的未婚妻，肌肤雪白，头发夜一般黑，安安静静，眼里心里只有他一个人；还有对面、王右手边那位年轻貌美的王后。她也是安静的。深色的眼睛藏在低垂的眼睑底下望出来，像某种兽的眼睛，幽深难测，如荫翳覆盖的两泓深潭。她的脸泛着红潮。赤金色的头发沉甸甸地拢向脑后，丰盈莹泽，两侧掩映着前额，末了收拢盘起，绾在一串串幽幽生光的珠宝底下。她身穿一袭希腊之地来的绸缎长裙，价值连城，深紫的颜色，镶着金边。她那安静的姿态里有一种奇异的、教人心神不宁的优雅，仿佛她那具美好的身体连同它美色的烈焰，穿透了掩藏它的华服，给每一道绸褶、每一粒闪烁的宝石，都注入了温热的、血肉一般的生气。而她的夫君坐在她一臂之遥的地方，心思眼目——看那样子——却只在侄子身上，至多不过时时斜眼瞟一瞟横席上他最新宠的那位姑娘。至于斯蒂尔比约恩，他的眼光照应着所有人，心里欢畅、安适、平和，至少在这段时间里，与全世界都相安无事。


===Original===
Now it began to be late, and men fell to telling of stories, and later on to man-matching. But the King made them give over this, deeming it likely to turn out too little peaceable a game in such a company as was there, being not of one land nor of one allegiance but of two or three. And he bade instead a skald of his sing them somewhat, whether some lay or drapa. “And best of all, some old love-song, sith this is a betrothal feast tonight.” So the skald stood forth and in obedience to the King spake forth that song which men call the Hell-ride of Brynhild, in manner following﻿—

The Ogress speaks.


===Chinese===
夜渐深了，众人先是讲起故事，后来又比起人物高下。王却叫他们停了：在座的人不属一国、不奉一主，而是两三国人聚在一处，这样的游戏怕要生事。于是王命他的一名诗人唱些什么，歌谣也好，颂诗也好。“顶好是首古老的情歌——今晚是订婚宴嘛。”于是诗人上前站定，遵王之命诵起了那支人们称作《布伦希尔德的冥府之行》的歌，歌辞如下——

女巨魔言。


===Original===
“Hold! for thou gettest gangway never
						

						Thorough this grit-built garth of mine.
						

						Should better beseem thee to broider at home
						

						Than to woo another’s wedded lord.
						

						What cam’st thou to woo from Valland hither
						

						O fickle head, unto house of mine?
						

						Gold lady, thou hast, (and thou list to know),
						

						Those milk-white hands of man’s blood washen.”


===Chinese===
“站住！你休想从此通过

穿过我这砾石筑的院墙。

你本该安坐家中做针黹，

而不该勾引别人家的夫君。

你从瓦兰跑到这里抢什么，

反复无常的女人，到我家来做甚？

黄金贵妇，你既想知道，

你那双奶白的手，是拿人血洗过的。”


===Original===
Brynhild speaks.

“Braid not me therefor, O Bride of the Stone,
						

						Though I of old did a-viking fare.
						

						I shall be still the stronger called
						

						Of us twain, whereso our tale men know.”


===Chinese===
布伦希尔德言。

“石头新娘啊，休要骂我，

虽然我当年也曾出海劫掠。

凡有人知晓我们故事的地方，

人们仍会说我们俩中我更强。”


===Original===
The Ogress speaks.

“Thou, O Brynhild, Budli’s daughter,
						

						For an omen of ill on earth wast born:
						

						The children of Giuki a-gley thou smotest,
						

						And their good house didst hurl in wreck.”


===Chinese===
女巨魔言。

“你，布伦希尔德，布德利之女，

生在世上便是凶兆：

你把吉乌基的儿女毁于歧路，

又把他们兴旺的家业摔成齑粉。”


===Original===
Brynhild speaks.

“I shall tell thee a true tale,
						

						O nothing knowing (if know thou wilt):
						

						What guerdon I had of Giuki’s heirs﻿—
						

						To be reft of my truelove and troth-forlorn.
						

						I was with Heimir in Hlymdale of old:
						

						Seasons eight I sat there in joy.
						

						Twelve winters had I (if wist thou wilt)
						

						Ere oath I sware to any prince.
						

						All they hight me in Hlymdale of old
						

						Hild the Helm’d, whoso knew me.
						


===Chinese===
布伦希尔德言。

“我告诉你一个真实的故事，

无知的人啊（你想知道便听）：

吉乌基的子嗣给了我什么报偿——

夺走我的挚爱，叫我背弃誓约。

从前我在希姆达尔与海米尔同住：

八个季节，我坐享欢乐。

那时我十二岁（你想知道便听），

未曾向任何王侯起过誓约。

从前在希姆达尔，人人唤我

戴盔的希尔德——凡识得我的都这样叫。


===Original===
						Then let I, in the land of the Goths,
						

						Helm-Gunnar the old to Hell go down:
						

						To Aud’s young brother I brought the glory:
						

						Over wroth waxed Odin with me for that.
						

						He lock’d me with shields in Skatalund,
						

						Red shields and white; rim touch’d rim:
						

						Bade he then that man break my slumber,
						

						Who in the wide world wist not of fear.
						

						About my stately southern hall
						

						High he let blaze the bane of woods:
						

						Bade he then only over it ride
						

						Him who should get me that gold that ’neath Fafnir lay.
						


===Chinese===
后来在哥特人的国度，我放

老赫尔姆-贡纳尔坠入冥府：

我给奥德的幼弟带来荣光：

奥丁为此对我大发雷霆。

他用盾牌把我锁在斯卡塔隆德，

红盾白盾，盾缘相抵：

他吩咐唯有那世上

不知恐惧为何物的人，才能唤醒我。

在我南方的华堂四周，

他教林木之祸高高燃起：

又吩咐唯有那取得

法夫纳底下黄金的人，才能驰过火焰。


===Original===
						Riding on Grani, the good gold-scatterer
						

						Came to my fosterer’s famous steadings:
						

						A viking better beyond all other
						

						Deem’d they him in the host of the Danes.
						

						Slept we and abode in one bed together,
						

						As though he my brother born had been:
						

						Not an hand of either drew nigh to other,
						

						Eight nights long of our lying so.
						

						Upbraided me Gudrun, Giuki’s daughter,
						

						That I had slept in Sigurd’s arms;
						

						Then wist I this clear which I would not wist:
						

						That they had beguil’d me in bridegroom-getting.
						

						World without end in woe and anguish
						

						Must mankind and womankind quicken and live.
						

						Now shall we twain never part,
						

						Sigurd and I.﻿—So sink thou: sink!”


===Chinese===
骑着格拉尼，那善撒黄金的骏马，

来到我义父有名的庄园：

丹麦大军之中，众人公认

他比一切维京人更出色。

我们同床共枕，厮守一处，

仿佛他是我的同胞手足：

两人谁也不曾伸手碰谁，

如此同眠，共八夜之久。

吉乌基之女古德伦责骂我，

说我睡在西格德怀里；

那时我才看清我不愿看清的事：

他们骗我，错配了新郎。

世世代代，直到永远，

男女众生都将在悲苦中生育存活。

如今我们两个永不分离，

我和西格德。——你沉下去吧：沉吧！”


===Original===
Now all were silent listening while the skald spake that lay, for he spake it well and in a manner to touch men’s hearts. Styrbiorn sat still, harkening attentively; and while he harkened his gaze was bent on Sigrid the Queen, where she sat over against him at the King’s right hand. She sat there as heretofore looking down, so that her eyes were hid under their long lazy lashes. One arm of her rested on the table before her, toying with a fallen cup. Now Styrbiorn was held with the music of that song, and his thoughts within him were on the sadness of the song, so that, looking on the Queen, he saw not her, but in imagination that Queen of old time, Brynhild. So watching, he heard, as in a dream, the skald’s sounding voice:

“Thou, O Brynhild, Budli’s daughter,
					

					For an omen of ill on earth wast born:
					

					The children of Giuki a-gley thou smotest,
					

					And their good house didst hurl in wreck.”


===Chinese===
诗人诵那支歌时，满座都静下来听：他唱得极好，唱得人人动容。斯蒂尔比约恩端坐不动，凝神细听；听着听着，他的目光落到了西格丽德王后身上——她坐在对面王的右手。她一如既往地垂目而坐，双眼掩在慵懒的长睫底下。她一只手臂支在面前的桌上，摆弄一只倒下的杯子。此刻斯蒂尔比约恩被那歌的乐音摄住了，心里的念头都系在歌中的悲哀上，以致望着王后时，看见的已不是她，而是想象中那位远古的王后——布伦希尔德。他这样望着，恍如梦中，听见诗人宏亮的歌声：

“你，布伦希尔德，布德利之女，

生在世上便是凶兆：

你把吉乌基的儿女毁于歧路，

又把他们兴旺的家业摔成齑粉。”


===Original===
And as the song went on, Styrbiorn thought in himself: Brynhild? Why was she to blame for it? It was Odin set that fire about her, and that weird upon her. And that was Sigurd that rode through the fire. And yet, it was not to Sigurd that she was wed, but to Gunnar, son of King Giuki: and Sigurd wedded not her, that was his right love, but Gudrun instead, King Giuki’s daughter. And then Brynhild slew herself on Sigurd’s funeral pyre. It is a strange unlucky tale, and not easy for a man to tell the rights and wrongs of it. And now she is riding down the cold and stony way of Hell, and this Ogress would plague her now and hold her back from Sigurd.

In that study, and still looking with bodily eye on Queen Sigrid, he saw in her now in his mind’s eye Brynhild in her free and glorious time, “Hild the Helm’d,” the Valkyrie, Odin’s shield-may. Like as in a trance he watched and marked, with wondrous clearness yet with a mind removed and dispassionate, the proud-curved luxurious lips half-open; the white throat of her, strong and delicate; the bosom of her, pressed a little, as it rose, against the edge of her gown, then as it fell leaving a hollow that opened on sweet unseen depths of softness and beauty. His inward gaze moved downward, unhindered by the table that stood in the way of his bodily sight; downward to the jewelled girdle, the byrny’s skirt where it shaped its close-lying texture of shining interwoven rings of iron about the large rondure of her hips.


===Chinese===
歌声继续，斯蒂尔比约恩心里暗想：布伦希尔德？这怎么怪得着她？是奥丁在她四周点起那圈火，是奥丁降下那道命运。驰过火焰的是西格德。可她嫁的偏偏不是西格德，而是吉乌基王之子贡纳尔；西格德也没有娶他真心所爱的她，反倒娶了吉乌基王的女儿古德伦。到后来，布伦希尔德在西格德的火葬堆上自刎而死。这是个奇特而不祥的故事，谁是谁非，人不容易说得清。此刻她正驰下冥府那条冰冷多石的路，这个女巨魔要折磨她，要把她同西格德隔开。

他这样出神，肉眼仍望着西格丽德王后，心眼中看见的却是布伦希尔德当年自由自在、荣耀煊赫的时光——“戴盔的希尔德”，瓦尔基里，奥丁的盾牌少女。他如入迷离之境，看得清清楚楚，心思却又超然冷静：她那傲慢丰润的唇微启着；雪白的颈项，既有力又纤秀；胸口随着呼吸微微抵住裙裾的边，落下去时又凹成一片幽影，隐约透出底下甜蜜柔软的深处。他心中的目光向下移去，那挡住肉眼视线的桌面拦不住它——一直向下，到那缀宝的腰带，到锁子甲的下摆：铁环交锁、莹莹发亮，紧贴着她丰臀的圆润线条。


===Original===
The skald spake the words:

“A viking better beyond all other
					

					Deem’d they him in the host of the Danes.”


===Chinese===
诗人诵到这几句：

“丹麦大军之中，众人公认

他比一切维京人更出色。”


===Original===
And Styrbiorn seemed in himself to be drowned yet deeper in the song, so that himself too was lost in it, and that which meant Sigurd meant him.

Then, at the words, “Upbraided me Gudrun, Giuki’s daughter,” he looked up: met, for the first time, Sigrid’s glance, and became on the instant like a man drunk. He sat staring with eyes wide open into her eyes that, wide open too and unblinking, looked full in his. Then the Queen looked away. Styrbiorn heard, as if from afar off, Thyri whispering in his ear some trifle of lovers’ talk. The gallop of his blood shook him so fiercely that he might not trust himself with speech. He reached out his hand to the mighty jewelled goblet before him, brimming with the froth of mead, and emptied it at a draught.


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩仿佛在歌里越陷越深，连自己也沉没了进去；歌里唱的既是西格德，也是他。

唱到“吉乌基之女古德伦责骂我”时，他抬起眼来：头一回撞上了西格丽德的目光，登时如痴如醉。他睁大双眼直直望进她的眼里；她也睁着眼，一眨不眨，正眼迎着他。随后王后把目光移开了。斯蒂尔比约恩听见蒂丽在耳边絮絮说着情人的体己话，声音仿佛从极远处飘来。血液在他体内奔驰轰鸣，震得他不敢开口说话。他伸手抓过面前那只嵌宝的大金杯——满杯蜜酒浮着酒沫——仰头一饮而尽。

## X / 十



===Original===
Broken Meats in Upsala

Styrbiorn slept the night after that banquet a sleep tumultuous with visions. In sleep, he rode a swift horse through lands silent and unpeopled, white with moonlight. He rode now through fires, as it were Brynhild’s fiery girdle about Skatalund, and now down deep wooded valley-slopes of darkness, where the young leafage brushed his hair and lips and hands as he passed. Then, in the swirling about of the visions which belonged to that unquiet slumber, he seemed to behold suddenly Sigrid the Queen naked before him in a whiteness of blinding brilliance; with the glory of which sight, sleep broke, and he opened his dazzled eyes on lamplight, and on Sigrid indeed in very presence standing beside his bed, but cloaked in her great scarlet cloak lined with swansdown.

She stood there by his feet, holding the lamp high. Her eyes were large and shining. Beholding him awake, she said, “I slept ill, and the whim took me to see if thou didst sleep tonight. I would not have waked thee.” Her breath caught as she spoke.

Styrbiorn rose sharply on his two elbows. His eyes, broad awake now, were fixed on her. And there was in that look, and in the whole frame of him, a tenseness like as is in a bowstring stretched. His face, flushed with sleep, took while he looked a yet darker red.

===Chinese===
乌普萨拉的残羹

那场宴会之后的夜里，斯蒂尔比约恩睡得不安稳，梦中纷纷攘攘。睡梦里，他骑一匹快马，穿行于沉寂无人的旷野，四野月光皎白。他时而驰过重重火阵，恍如布伦希尔德环绕斯卡塔隆德的火焰束带；时而驰下幽深林木的谷坡，暗影沉沉，幼嫩的枝叶拂过他的头发、嘴唇和手背。而后，在那不安眠的纷乱梦境翻卷之间，他仿佛骤然看见西格丽德王后赤裸在他眼前，周身一片炫目的白光；这景象的荣光击破了睡眠，他睁开眩晕的双眼，看见灯光之下，西格丽德果真就立在他床边——只是裹着她那件天鹅绒衬里的猩红大氅。

她立在他脚头，高擎着灯。她双眼大而明亮。见他醒来，她说道：“我睡得不好，忽起念头，想来看看你今夜睡得如何。我本不想吵醒你。”说话时她呼吸一窒。

斯蒂尔比约恩猛地用两肘撑起身来。他此刻全然醒了，双眼直直盯着她。那目光里，以及他整个身架里，都有一种绷紧，如满张的弓弦。他的脸原被睡眠熏得通红，看着看着，红色愈发深了。

===Original===
“This would I know,” he said, and his speech came hoarse and stumbling: “is it with the King’s leave thou goest o’ these night-walkings?”

Sigrid’s mouth hardened. She gave him an odd look from half-closed eyelashes, then, daintily as a seagull settles on the sea, sat down at the far end of the bed. Styrbiorn’s stillness was like the stillness of great clouds brooding before the lightning.

After a little he said, “Come nearer.”

Sigrid marked his voice, and the look of him. These things gave her a delicate pleasure, as of dangerous steering round a rocky headland in a strong sailing breeze blowing on the land. “O no,” said she. “I can hear thee very well so.”

===Chinese===
“我倒要问个明白，”他说，嗓音嘶哑而生涩，“你这夜行夜访，是得了王的允准么？”

西格丽德的嘴抿紧了。她自半垂的睫毛下斜睨他一眼，神色古怪，随即像海鸥落波纹一般轻盈，在床的另一端坐了下来。斯蒂尔比约恩不动，那静默如大块乌云酝酿闪电前的静默。

过了片刻，他说：“再过来些。”

西格丽德听出他声音里的东西，也看出他神色里的东西。这些给了她一种精细的愉悦，好比乘着强劲的向岸风，驾船绕行嶙峋的海岬。“哦，不，”她说，“我在这儿听得真切。”

===Original===
Styrbiorn moved a little. “Why needest thou have waked me out of my dream? Shall I not have atonement of thee for that, Sigrid? Shall I tell thee,” he said, and his voice came like that light and sudden wind that sets a-quiver the leaves before a storm, “what I was a-dreaming on?” With that, he would have caught her into his arms; but she was a wary steersman, too swift for this gust of his, sudden though it was, and was leapt up and out of the door ere he could reach her. Styrbiorn, barefoot, guided by the leaping flicker of the windblown lamp, overtook her at her own door, flung himself in betwixt door and doorpost before she might shut him out, and had her in a moment alone with him in her own chamber, trapped.

The Queen stood facing him between the bed-head and the wall. She had set the lamp on a shelf, shoulder-high on her left, and stood there rigid, cloaked to the eyes in her great scarlet mantle, her eyes fierce and bright, like some beautiful beast brought to bay, her breath coming and going in pants. She did not speak. Styrbiorn abode some paces off, by the closed door. He reached a hand behind him, fumbling for the bolt, found it, and shot it softly home. He abode there silent, his hand still on the bolt, leaning towards her as the setting moon leans towards the sea. So for a minute’s space they face one another, Styrbiorn and the Queen, alone with the lamplight and its shifting shadows: with the velvet dark (with here and there a faint star shimmering) that filled the window above the Queen’s bed with the silence of night, so deep that each seemed to hear the other’s heartbeats: night, that is of kin with those shadowy-visaged and iron-handed Fates that lay men at their length: summer night, and the glittering of her eyes and his in the beams of the watchful lamp.

Styrbiorn came a step nearer to her, his gaze fixed, like a sleepwalker, saying, hardly above his breath, “Sigrid.” She neither spoke nor moved, but abode as if fascinated. Like the passing shadows of the moon, so silently he drew towards her, or like some motion of those grey Fates, or of things drawn by them blindly. He was kneeled now at her feet, his arms locked about her above her knees. The Queen rested motionless, only he felt the quivering of her flank under the rich mantle where his cheek was pressed. Styrbiorn lifted his eyes to her face; and now time past and time to come went for him clean away out of mind and caring, so that he was ware now no longer of any other thing save only of her: the perfume of her presence, her lips that parted a little, her eyes that looked down on him dark and wide. His hands reached upward and, as if afraid all should on the motion vanish in air, paused, scarcely touching her either shoulder. She, still gazing on Styrbiorn out of her eyes’ unsounded darks, suddenly let slip with a noble and divine grace her great red mantle, and stood there in her white loveliness before him.

===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩动了动。“你何苦把我从梦里唤醒？西格丽德，这笔账你不该赔我么？要不要我告诉你，”他说，声音像暴雨前那阵骤起的轻风，吹得树叶簌簌发颤，“我梦见了什么？”说着，他便要伸手把她揽入怀中；但她是个警觉的舵手，任他这一阵风来得如何突然，她比它更快，早一跃而起，抢在他够着她之前出了门。斯蒂尔比约恩赤着脚，循着被风吹得跳动的灯光追去，在她自己的房门前赶上她，趁她关门不及，将身子挤进门与门柱之间，转眼间便把她困在了她自己的闺房里，与她单独相对。

王后立在床头与墙壁之间，面对着他。她已把灯搁在左手齐肩的搁板上，僵立在那里，猩红大氅直裹到眼边，双眼凶狠而明亮，像一头被逼到绝境的美丽野兽，呼吸急促，一起一伏。她不说话。斯蒂尔比约恩立在数步之外，关着的门边。他反手到身后，摸索门闩，摸到了，轻轻推上。他立在那里不出声，手仍搭在门闩上，身子朝她倾着，如西沉之月倾向海面。就这样相对了约一分钟，斯蒂尔比约恩与王后，独对着一盏灯和它浮动的暗影：对着王后床上方窗口里那丝绒般的黑夜（其间偶有一两点微弱的星光闪烁），夜的静默如此之深，彼此仿佛听得见对方的心跳：这样的夜，与那些面色阴沉、手段铁硬的命运女神本是同族，她们把人放倒，直挺挺放平在地：夏夜，以及在警醒不眠的灯光里，她的眼睛与他的眼睛的灼灼交辉。

斯蒂尔比约恩朝她又近了一步，目光凝定，如同梦游之人，声音低得几乎不成声息：“西格丽德。”她不言不动，仿佛中了定身之法。如月光移动的影子，他无声无息地向她挨近，又像那些灰色命运女神的某种动作，像被她们盲目牵引之物。此刻他已跪在她脚前，双臂环锁住她的双膝之上。王后一动不动，只是他觉出，他面颊偎贴之处，华贵大氅之下她的腰侧在微微颤动。斯蒂尔比约恩抬眼看她的脸；这时过去与将来的一切都从他心里干干净净地褪去了，他再觉察不到任何别的事物，只有她：她周身袭人的香气，她微微张开的嘴唇，她低头望他时又黑又大的眼睛。他的双手向上探去，仿佛生怕这一动之间一切都要化作空气散去，停在那里，几乎不曾碰到她的双肩。她呢，仍从眼底那深不可测的幽暗里凝望着斯蒂尔比约恩，忽然以一种高贵而神圣的优雅，任那件猩红大氅从身上滑落，把她洁白的可爱赤呈在他面前。

---

===Original===
The night wore now, and the stars moved on, and those unseen powers which weave the web of destiny threw the shuttle yet again, and Styrbiorn, with his wits now awake again, looked and beheld in the lamplight stretched at his side there Sigrid the Queen, and, in his mind’s eye, that which he had this night accomplished. He leaped from the bed.

The Queen, startled so out of her sweet and pleasant dreams, sat up, first in amaze: then, meeting her lover’s wild and unfriendly regard, her proud face darkened and she rendered him back look for look in that kind. He turned with a sudden blundering twist of the body; but she was, as for this time, the swifter, and, leaping up and catching her cloak about her, stood betwixt him and the door. He swerved sidelong from her and came heavily against the wall, face to the wall, his eyes buried in his great hands.

The Queen beheld him awhile in silence. “It is likely thou art some churl’s son,” said she at length; “or some changeling. No man of kingly blood would carry it thus, after honours the like of which I’ve done thee: more’s the pity.”

Styrbiorn moved like a blinded man towards the door; then, finding her in the way, gave back a pace. Then he said, yet with eyes averted and in alien and hard tones half-choked, “Let me go, Sigrid.”

===Chinese===
夜渐深了，星辰移转，那些编织命运之网的无形力量又掷了一梭。斯蒂尔比约恩神智复醒，就着灯光看去，见西格丽德王后伸展着躺在自己身旁，又在心眼里看见这一夜他做成的事。他从床上一跃而起。

王后正做着她甜美的好梦，被这一惊坐了起来，先是怔住；随即迎上她情郎那狂乱而冷酷的眼神，她高傲的脸沉了下来，以同样的眼色还敬他。他猛地一个踉跄转身；但这一回是她更快，她跳起来抓起大氅裹在身上，挡在他与门之间。他从她身侧一闪，重重撞在墙上，脸对着墙，双眼深深埋进他的大手之中。

王后默然看了他一阵。“你多半是哪个村夫的儿子，”她终于开口道，“要么就是个偷换儿。王族血统的男子，受了我方才那般的恩宠之后，断不会是这般做派：可惜了。”

斯蒂尔比约恩像个瞎子似的朝门口挪动；见她拦在那里，退了一步。然后他开口，眼睛仍望着别处，声音陌生而僵硬，半是哽咽：“放我走，西格丽德。”

===Original===
“I’ll let thee go,” said she, “when thou speakest to me like my noble kinsman, not like a baseborn thrall.”

For a moment he paused as if doubting what were best to do, then lifted up his head and strode forward as if he were minded to thrust her aside by force. At hands’-reach he halted. The ghastliness of his look as he stood and looked upon her took from her for a minute all power of thought or motion. Then he opened his mouth and said, “What have I to do with thee, a faithless bitch?”

With that, he turned from her, catching in his two hands the pillars of the bed. Under the grip of his hands and the weight of him flung between them the great oak pillars shook and creaked. He turned again, dazed yet with this nightmare, steadying himself yet with one hand by the pillar of the bed. He looked at her now with eyes like some dog’s eyes asking to be let out: naught else matters.

But the Queen faced him, back to the door, staring. Under the injury of those words she had moved not an eyelid. But instant by instant she seemed stonier grown; her face whitened, even to the lips; and then the blood flooded back terribly. She said in a low tone, the words even and steady like water dropping and clear as the clicking of blades, “But this shall be thy death, then.” Therewith so loud shrieked Sigrid the Queen that the cups rang on the wall and the geese screamed in the King’s garth.

===Chinese===
“我便放你走，”她说，“等你说话像我高贵的姻亲，而不像个下贱奴隶的时候。”

他迟疑了片刻，仿佛拿不定怎样才好，随后昂起头，大步向前，似乎要用蛮力把她推开。到了伸手可及之处，他停住了。他站在那里看着她，那面容的狰狞使她一时全然失去了思考与行动的能力。然后他开口道：“你这无信的母狗，与我何干？”

说罢，他转身背对她，两手抓住床柱。在他双手的紧握和他掷在柱间的体重之下，粗大的橡木床柱摇晃呻吟。他又转回来，噩梦的晕眩尚未褪尽，一只手仍扶着床柱支撑自己。此刻他望着她的眼神，像一条哀求放它出去的狗的眼睛：其余的一切，都无所谓了。

王后却面对他站着，背抵着门，目光灼灼。受了那句话的重伤，她眼皮都不曾动一下。但她一分一分地仿佛变得更冷更硬，石头一般；她的脸白了，白到嘴唇；随即血色猛地可怕地涌了回来。她低声说道，字句均匀而平稳，如水滴落下，又如刀剑轻叩般清亮：“那么，这便是你的死期了。”随即西格丽德王后高声尖叫起来，叫得墙上的杯盏嗡嗡作响，王家院子里的鹅也惊叫起来。

===Original===
She gave him way now: but he was not quicker in the doorway than those women of hers, hearing this larum, and others running. Styrbiorn, thrusting past them like one straught of his right wits, butted into old Thorgnyr three paces without the Queen’s chamber-door. He swerved past the old man, and Helgi caught at him. He smote Helgi so good a whirt on the ear as laid him out senseless. Styrbiorn came so to his own chamber, yet not so well but that Thorgnyr and Helgi and some four or five women of the Queen’s had seen him in such sort rush out from the Queen’s bedchamber.

Eric the King came now, roused by that great cry, cloaked and with bare sword in one hand and a lamp in the other, along the passage to the Queen’s chamber. They gave back all before him to right and left: not one, neither Thorgnyr nor any other, took heart to speak word to him, but gave back and let him by. He came in, looked on her an instant, then shut to the door behind him. Sigrid fell down at his feet and clasped him about the knees in a great passion of tears. The King suffered that to have its course. Choking and sobbing she let him understand little by little to what vile use his darling nephew, lust-burned and ale-heated, had by violence turned her. The King heard all out, silent and without sign or stir, looking down the while on her head bowed and shaken with her sobbing and crying, on the nape of her neck where the first little hairs shadowed the white skin with their prettily curled growth, and, where her cloak opened at the throat, on that sweet and shadowy place where her breasts pressed one against the other like two doves perched together on the edge of a roof. When she had told her tale, she looked up in his face and cried and said, “Lord, why didst thou leave me?”

Like a great tower and big the King stood over her. He said nothing: only lifted up his jaw a little and so stood looking steadily before him over her head, with set face. Sigrid, frighted with his coldness, rose up, clinging her arms now about his neck, shivering and weeping with her face hidden on the King’s breast. Stone still he stood. His face looked drawn and hard, awful to look on, unsearchable as the brow of night, and sad like the sea under a winter dawn. At length, coming out of that study, he looked down at her again, gently loosed her hands from about his neck and without word said went from the chamber. Sigrid, looking at his face, deemed it wholesomer to speak no word but let him go.

===Chinese===
这回她让开了路：可他出门也快不过她的那些侍女，她们听见了这阵惊叫，还有旁人也跑了来。斯蒂尔比约恩从她们中间直撞过去，像个失了心窍的人，一出王后寝房三门之外，一头撞在老松格尼尔身上。他侧身闪过老人，海尔吉又赶来抓他。他一巴掌狠狠掴在海尔吉耳上，把他掴得人事不省。斯蒂尔比约恩这样回到自己房中，只是没能干净脱身：松格尼尔、海尔吉，还有王后的四五个侍女，都看见他这般模样从王后寝房里冲了出来。

埃里克王被那声大叫惊起，披着大氅，一手仗着出鞘的剑，一手提着灯，沿走廊来到王后房中。众人纷纷向他左右退开：没有一个人，松格尼尔也罢，旁人也罢，胆敢跟他说一句话，只是退开让路。他走进去，看了她一瞬，随手把门关上。西格丽德扑倒在他脚下，抱住他的双膝，泪如泉涌。王由她哭去。她哭着噎着，一点一点让他明白：他那位心肝内侄如何欲火焚身、酒气冲脑，竟用强暴把她作践了。王从头听完，沉默着，无一丝声息动静，只低头看着她那颗随呜咽颤抖垂伏的头，看着她颈后初生的细发如何以可爱的鬈曲在雪白肌肤上铺下浅浅的影，看着她大氅在喉口敞开之处，那处甜美的幽暗里，她的一双乳房相互偎依，如并栖在檐头的两只鸽子。她讲完了，抬头看他脸，哭道：“主上，你为何丢下我？”

王耸立在她身前，如一座巨塔。他一言不发：只微微抬起下颚，就这样目光平视着她头顶的前方，面色凝定。西格丽德被他的冷淡骇住了，站起身来，双臂改而攀住他的颈项，瑟瑟发抖，把脸埋在王的胸前哭泣。他石像般立着不动。他的脸绷紧而僵硬，望之可畏，深不可测如夜之眉额，悲沉如冬晓之下的大海。终于，他从那出神中回转，又低头看她，轻轻解开她攀在颈上的双手，一言不发地走出了寝房。西格丽德看着他的脸，觉得还是一言不发、由他去，更为妥当。

===Original===
A bleak greyness of morning began now to pale their lamps and the last embers of the long fires in the King’s hall. In a little while there came a man to Thorgnyr from the King bidding him come and see the King straightway. Thorgnyr went, and found the King sitting armed in his chair with a drawn sword laid across his knees. The King looked at him a long time in silence. Thorgnyr stood with head bowed. At length the King said, “Let me see thy face, Thorgnyr.” That old man raised his eyes and looked the King in the face. After a minute the King spake again and said, “Forty years hast thou been my man. It is well that thou shouldst know my mind.” And he said, “Of these things that have this night befallen I will have no man speak to me, neither thou nor another, on pain of death. For the Queen, every woman should be forgiven once. For Styrbiorn, thou shalt go thyself and find him and say unto him that he shall have free way out of the Swede-realm so he be gone this very day. But if ever he shall come into the land again so long as I be alive, or shall come anigh me, that shall be his death.”

Thorgnyr said naught, looking on the King from under the dark eaves of his brows. His lean hands twitched a little. Then he spoke, “You have sometimes thought I played for mine own hand, Lord, and not for yours. Will you not see him?”

“If I should see him,” answered the King, “that should be his death. Go thou, and do my bidding.”

Thorgnyr went out from before the King. There was great stir throughout the house, and a noise of horses in the King’s garth. Thorgnyr went out into the garth and came upon Styrbiorn as he went a-horseback, and his Jomsburgers were mounting round about him on every side. Thorgnyr came close to Styrbiorn, so that none might hear save the twain of them, and gave him the King’s message neither adding aught to it nor taking aught away. Styrbiorn had the look of a man stupid for sleeplessness during many nights. Thorgnyr, knowing not for sure if he had heard the message aright, spake it over again word by word. But Styrbiorn answered and said, “I heard thee very well. Thou hast gotten the goal, then. What need to trattle more on’t?” Therewith he swung his leg into the saddle and, without looking back or giving further heed to Thorgnyr, rode with his men out of the King’s garth and out of Upsala southward to the sea.

===Chinese===
惨淡的灰白晨光渐起，把他们的灯火和王家厅中长灶里的最后余烬照得黯淡了。不多时，有个人从王那里来见松格尼尔，传他即刻去见王。松格尼尔去了，见王披挂坐在椅中，膝上横着一柄出鞘的剑。王默然注视他良久。松格尼尔垂首而立。终于王开口道：“让我看看你的脸，松格尼尔。”老人抬起眼来，直视王的脸。过了一分钟，王又说道：“你做我的人已经四十年了。你也该知道我的心意。”又说：“今夜出的这些事，我不许任何人对我提起，无论你还是别人，违者处死。至于王后，凡为女子者，都该饶恕一次。至于斯蒂尔比约恩，你亲自去找他，告诉他：只要他今日之内离开，瑞典国境任他自由出去。但只要我活在世上一天，他若再踏上这片土地，或是走近我一步，那便是他的死期。”

松格尼尔一声不吭，自眉毛的浓黑檐下仰视着王。他瘦削的手微微抽动。然后他说话了：“主上，您有时以为我营私自利，不为您打算。您不肯见他一面么？”

“我若见他，”王答道，“那便是他的死期。你去吧，照我的话办。”

松格尼尔从王面前退出。整座宅邸大乱，王家院子里一片人喊马嘶。松格尼尔走到院中，正遇斯蒂尔比约恩上马，他的约姆斯堡人正在四下里纷纷乘马。松格尼尔凑近斯蒂尔比约恩，近到只有他们两人听得见，把王的话一字不加一字不减地传了。斯蒂尔比约恩一脸许多夜晚不曾安睡的呆滞神情。松格尼尔吃不准他是否听真了，便又逐字逐句说了一遍。斯蒂尔比约恩却答道：“我听得很清楚。你的图谋到底成了。何须再多絮叨？”说罢，他一腿甩上马鞍，再不回头，也不再理会松格尼尔，率他的人马出了王家院子，出乌普萨拉，向南往海边去了。

## XI / 十一



===Original===
Jomsburg Seawalls

Styrbiorn stood on the outer seawall at Jomsburg while they brought his fleet in through the sea-gates: a tricky work, seeing there was a heavy sea running, and the last part worst of all, for it was now long past sunset, and their only light torchlight and the moon shining fitfully through flying racks of vapour. But it was by his command; and there was that in his eye since they sailed out of the Low three days gone that made his folk count it safer to risk the smashing may be of one ship or two sooner than meddle with him. Styrbiorn stood there in his war-gear, wearing under his gold-edged byrny that Greek kirtle of cramoisy silk and gold which the princess of Holmgarth had given him in days gone by, and about his middle that mighty silver girdle with horse-headed serpents intertwined at every link, the same which he had taken from about Jomala’s middle in the temple amid the wolds of Kirialaland, and the barbarous people had watched in vain to see earth gape for him that wrought so impiously; and from that girdle hung the heavy two-edged sword, Eric’s gift, wherewith he had made so many famous conquests: the same wherewith he had fought and prevailed against Palnatoki’s self when first he came to Jomsburg. He was bareheaded and, according to his common wont in these days, without cloak or mantle, so that the glory of his arms was naked to the eye, and the breadth of his shoulders, that cast about him, in their proud shapely poise, a mantle of more kingliness than royal and costly stuffs might ever shed about a king.

Fierce and sullen was the countenance of Styrbiorn, yet quiet, as of a fierce beast charmed with music, as he watched that dance of the rolling surges sweeping and pausing and falling and rising again: Ran’s eternal children leading their round as if in sad ceremonial observance of some divinity hidden apart, removed from all knowledge or communion of human kind; and listened to the swelling roar of the breaker as it rode on, the thud and thunder of its fall, and the grinding hiss of the shingle in the backwash, as if wrath, which is older than the world and older than the Gods, drew in its breath once again, pondering some greater mischief.

Stepping back to avoid a wave that flung itself with more than ordinary violence against the seawall and tossed high above it a wild white man of spray, Styrbiorn found himself in the arms of Biorn Asbrandson that was come up behind him unheard.

===Chinese===
约姆斯堡海堤

舰队正从海门驶入约姆斯堡，斯蒂尔比约恩立在外层海堤上看着：这是件棘手的活计，海上浪头正高，最后一段尤其凶险，因为这时早已日落多时，他们唯一的照明只有火把，和从飞掠的水汽罅隙间时隐时现的月光。但这是他的命令；而且自从三天前驶出低地以来，他眼中有一种东西，使他的人觉得，宁可冒险让一两艘船撞碎，也别去触他的霉头。斯蒂尔比约恩一身战装立在那里，金边锁子甲下穿着霍尔姆加德公主昔年所赠的那件希腊绯红金线束腰长衣，腰间束着那条巨大的银带——每个链环上都交缠着马首蛇身的巨兽——正是他从基里亚拉兰荒原神庙里约玛拉腰上解下来的那条；那些蛮人眼睁睁看他行此大不敬之事，徒然等着大地裂开吞他。银带上悬着那柄沉重的双刃剑，埃里克王所赐，他用它创下过许多赫赫武功：也正是这柄剑，他初到约姆斯堡时，用来与帕尔纳托基本人交手并占了上风。他光着头，照这些天的老习惯，也不披斗篷大氅，让臂膀的雄伟赤裸裸呈现人前；他那宽阔的双肩，以其骄傲俊伟的架势，给他披上一件比王家贵料所能加于国王的更为王者的大氅。

斯蒂尔比约恩面色狞厉阴沉，却又安静，像一头被乐声催眠的猛兽。他望着翻滚的巨浪那场舞蹈：扫过来，停一停，跌下去，又立起来——拉恩的永恒儿女们领着她们的环舞，仿佛在悲戚地依礼敬奉某位隐秘的神明，远离人类的一切知晓与交通；他听着浪头驰来时渐涨的咆哮，坠落时的轰然重击，回波里砾石碾磨的嘶嘶声，仿佛那比世界更古老、比诸神更古老的愤怒，又吸进一口气来，正在思量什么更大的祸祟。

一个浪头以异乎寻常的猛势扑上堤壁，把一个狂乱的人形白浪高高抛过堤顶；斯蒂尔比约恩退身避让，却发现自己落进了比约恩·阿斯布兰德松的怀里——他悄悄来到身后，竟无人听见。

===Original===
“What fiend bewitched thee?” said Biorn. “Could’st thou not have beached ’em in the firth where calm water is and a sheltered shore? There’s one stove in but now by the gate, and men drowned, like enough: we cannot tell i’ this windy dark.”

“Let them drown, then,” said Styrbiorn; “and thou too, unless thou mend thy talk.” He swung round away from Biorn, and the great sword at his side, Eric’s gift, clanked against his thigh. He took it in his two hands, peered at it for a moment in the glimpsing moonbeams as if at some strange thing, then unfastened it sheath and all from his girdle and sent it hurtling into the sea. “And that, afore all,” he said.

Biorn, being a man of sense, held his peace.

The last ship was in. After a while Styrbiorn, still in his former posture watching the endless procession of surges, said in hard toneless accents, “Shall I tell thee what I did in Sweden?”

===Chinese===
“是什么恶鬼迷了你的心窍？”比约恩说，“你本不能把船带进峡湾里去么？那里水波平静，岸有遮蔽。方才门口就撞瘪了一条船，兴许还淹死了人：这么个刮黑风的夜里，谁说得清。”

“那就叫他们淹死，”斯蒂尔比约恩说，“你也一样，除非你说话放规矩点。”他猛地转身背对比约恩，腰间那柄巨剑——埃里克王所赐——在大腿上铿然一响。他双手取下它，趁着月光明灭端详了一瞬，仿佛在看一件陌生物事，随即连鞘带剑从腰带上解下，扬手掷入海中。“头一件，就是它。”他说。

比约恩是个明白人，便不再作声。

最后一条船进了港。过了一阵，斯蒂尔比约恩仍保持着原来的姿势，望着无穷无尽的浪的行进，用生硬平板的声调说：“要不要我把在瑞典做的事讲给你听？”

===Original===
“Am not I thy brother?” said Biorn.

Styrbiorn said, “I dallied with a whore, and I lost a kingdom.”

Biorn, who knew when silence is best, said nothing.

Styrbiorn lifted his eyes to the moon, high over Wendland to the east, that showed like a dead queen’s face, white and forlorn, behind a drifting waterish veil of broken cloud that turned all the sky to tarnished silver. Nearer at hand, through a lower level of air, woolly clouds black as coal were charioted by the tearing wind one by one across the face of the moon. Each as it passed seemed to catch and retain some tincture of her brightness, and fled down the wind eastward mantled with a darkness less pitchy than before. Here and there for an instant a star beamed down.

===Chinese===
“我不正是你的兄弟么？”比约恩说。

斯蒂尔比约恩说：“我与一个娼妇厮混，丢了一个王国。”

比约恩深知何时以沉默为上，便一言不发。

斯蒂尔比约恩抬眼望月。月亮悬在东方文德兰的上空，像一张死去王后的脸，苍白而凄惶，隔着一层飘移的、水汽般的碎云之纱，整个天空都被染成了晦暗的银。近处更低一层空气里，煤炭般漆黑的絮云被烈风一辆一辆驾着车，逐一碾过月面。每一朵经过时，仿佛都沾染并带走了一点她的光辉，顺着风向东逃去，披着比先前稍淡一层的黑。这里那里，偶有一颗星倏然一闪。

===Original===
Styrbiorn said, “I lied to thee. Thus it was: I have foully belied a Queen, and lost that which all the world and the kingdoms of the world might not avail to purchase again for me.”

Biorn, thinking in himself that such speeches are but as so many catches and scrabblings of a man over head and ears in water, said nothing, but gripped him by the hand. Styrbiorn kept the hand in his, tightening his iron grasp upon it whenever it offered to move away, and standing in all other respects motionless for a long time with feet firm planted wide apart, like some colossus brooding above the flood. Biorn, his hand in his, felt the throb of his veins, not less thunderous nor less deep-drawn from the ultimate springs of life and fate than that thunder of waters about Jomsburg seawall, the pulse of the elemental sea.

Stybiorn said at last, “Men of lore will tell thee that adulterers when they come to die must wander in streams of venom, at the strand of corpses remote from the sun, in that castle which is woven of the spines of snakes. Is that true, foster-brother, thinkest thou?”

Biorn answered, “I do not know.”

===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩说：“我对你撒了谎。实情是：我恶毒地污蔑了一位王后，失去了一件普天之下、万国王土也再赎不回来的东西。”

比约恩心想，这类话无非是一个没顶之人在水里乱抓乱扒罢了，便不作声，只握住他的手。斯蒂尔比约恩把那只手攥在自己手里，它一要抽走，他那铁钳般的握力便收紧一分；除此之外他久久纹丝不动，双足大开、牢牢钉在地上，像一尊临流沉思的巨像。比约恩的手在他掌中，觉出他血脉的搏动——那搏动不比约姆斯堡海堤四周水声的雷霆稍弱，也不比元素之海的脉搏更少从生命与命运的最深泉源里汲取：一样轰然，一样深沉。

斯蒂尔比约恩终于开口：“有学问的人会告诉你：奸夫淫妇死后，要在毒液汇成的河流里漂泊，在远离太阳的死者之滨，住那座用蛇的脊骨编成的城堡。义兄弟，你说，那是真的么？”

比约恩答道：“我不知道。”

===Original===
“Thou art mine elder by ten year, and shouldst know more than I,” said Styrbiorn.

For a great while Styrbiorn was silent, following with his eye the mighty rhythm of the waves, where one after another stormed up the wall, clutched, and fell, and swept back to sea; and every wave as it plunged back seaward from the wall met another wave coming on, and like young living things in their boisterous sport the two waves meeting clasped and tumbled one another, clashed together with a shout and reared high in the air, a single sudden pillar of flashing foam. Then he said, “Snorri the Priest drove thee out of Iceland because of a woman.”

“Not drove me,” said Biorn. “I went, though.”

Styrbiorn turned sharp upon him, set a hand on either shoulder of him, drew him close, and looked him close in the eye. He said, “Follow me, and I shall show thee wonders. There is no good thing under sun or moon, Biorn. He that will follow me shall get no good by it. There is no good for me to give him, in all the world. But dominion he shall get, and power, and this withal: that when my foot is on the neck of the King of Danes, and of King Burisleif, and the great King in Micklegarth, and﻿—No more; but his foot that will follow me shall be there beside my foot, ’stead of his neck beside theirs.”

===Chinese===
“你长我十岁，理当比我知道得多。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。

好长一阵，斯蒂尔比约恩默然无语，眼睛追着波涛宏大的节律：浪一个接一个扑上堤壁，抓一把，跌落，扫回海去；每一波浪从堤上退回海里，都撞上迎面而来的另一波浪，两个浪像年少活物在撒野嬉戏，一相遇便相互搂抱翻滚，轰然相撞，高高立起在空中，成了一道倏忽即逝的闪亮水柱。然后他说：“斯诺里祭司为了一个女人，把你逐出了冰岛。”

“不是驱逐。”比约恩说，“不过我到底是走了。”

斯蒂尔比约恩猛地转向他，两手各按住他一边肩膀，把他拉近，逼视他的眼睛。他说：“跟我走，我会让你看见奇迹。太阳月亮底下没有好东西，比约恩。肯跟我走的人，得不到任何好处。普天之下，我没有好处给他。但他会得到统治权，得到权力，还有这一样：当我的脚踏上丹麦王的脖颈、布里莱夫王的脖颈、米克加德大王的脖颈，还有——不说了；但肯跟我走的人，他的脚会踏在我的脚旁边，而不是他的脖颈垫在他们的脖颈旁边。”

===Original===
“Thou art a blasphemer,” said Biorn. “And I think thou art fey.”
