“Is that all thou hast to tell me?” said Styrbiorn.

Biorn answered and said, “There is this, too: that I will not leave thee nor forsake thee so long as both thou and I be alive.”

===Chinese===
“你是个渎神的人。”比约恩说，“而且我想，你是个死期已定的人。”

“你要对我说的，就只有这些？”斯蒂尔比约恩说。

比约恩答道：“还有一句：只要你我二人尚在人间，我必不离弃你，不抛下你。”

## XII / 十二

===Original===
The Cowing of the Dane-King

The lords of Jomsburg came in now from their summer viking. Styrbiorn would speak to no man of those things which had come about in Upsala at summer’s end; but they remained not hidden, for they that had fared with him into Sweden told it to their messmates, and it was in most men’s minds that he was not likely to sit quiet under that shaming.


===Chinese===
慑服丹麦王

约姆斯堡的众首领结束了夏季的维京劫掠，陆续归来。夏末在乌普萨拉发生的那种种事，斯蒂尔比约恩对谁也不提；但事情终究瞒不住：随他去过瑞典的人讲给了同船的伙伴，众人心想，受了这番羞辱，他多半咽不下这口气。


===Original===
All that winter Styrbiorn abode in Jomsburg. He was moody and ill to do with. Biorn was with him winter-long, but the rest of them went every one to his own place: Sigvaldi and his brethren to Skaney, Bui and Sigurd to the old man Veseti their father in Borgundholm, and the rest accordingly. Palnatoki, ere that he went home into Fion, took Styrbiorn apart and said, “Few be they that can stand alone. I would have thee remember this: whatsoever thou art minded to do or whithersoever to fare next summer, we will lend thee aid as thumb serves finger.” Styrbiorn gripped him by the hand.

Styrbiorn grew blacker of mood as winter deepened. He would be oftenest alone; out of doors all day long in the wildest weather, walking hours together on the seawalls; rowing out to sea sometimes in a boat alone; once or twice, in weather when no boat could live long, swamped and swimming ashore out of seas which no man, save by luck, could look to win out of alive. Other whiles he would go alone upon the mainland, ranging among the hills and sea-cliffs. Now the thrall Erland, at Styrbiorn’s riding from Upsala, had bethought him to bring Moldi and ship him aboard and carry him south to Jomsburg. For it seemed to him that his lord was now leaving behind him in the Swede-realm many a thing should grieve his heart to lose it, and that here was one thing might be saved for him and that he should be glad of, albeit he had no mind as for that while neither for that nor for naught else beside. And so it was that when Styrbiorn fared alone upon the mainland, Moldi would ever follow some way behind him. But Styrbiorn seemed neither to see him nor to know he was there, taking, if he saw him indeed, no more note of him than he might take of his own shadow. In such times, not Moldi only shadowed him unnoticed, but Biorn also was wont to follow him all day afar off with a band of men, lest he should be set upon by the Wends. But, whether because they were ware of Biorn and his company at hand, or for their old fear of Jomsburg and of Styrbiorn, or (which was scarce reason enough) because peace was between King Burisleif and the Jomsburgers, the Wends held off from them.


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩整个冬天住在约姆斯堡，郁郁寡欢，难以相处。比约恩陪了他一冬，其余的人各自回家：西格瓦迪兄弟回斯科讷，布伊和西格德回博恩霍尔姆他们的老父维塞蒂处，其他人也各归其位。帕尔纳托基回菲英之前，把斯蒂尔比约恩拉到一边，说：“能孤身立于世的人没有几个。你要记住这一条：来年夏天，无论你决意做什么、去哪里，我们都会助你，如拇指之助手指。”斯蒂尔比约恩紧紧握住他的手。

冬意越深，斯蒂尔比约恩的心绪越发阴沉。他多半独来独往：终日在户外，顶着最狂的天气，在防波堤上一走就是几个时辰；有时独自荡一叶小船出海；有一两回，天气恶劣到任何小船都撑不了多久，船翻了，他从浪里游上岸——那样的海，人不靠运气休想活着出来。还有些时候，他独自登上大陆，在群山与海崖间游荡。原来那个奴隶埃尔兰，在斯蒂尔比约恩骑马离开乌普萨拉时，心想主人如今把许多教他心疼的东西丢在了瑞典，这一件总算还能替他保住，他见了必定欢喜——虽然主人眼下这个样子，这个也罢，别的也罢，什么都顾不上。于是埃尔兰把莫尔迪装上船，一路带到南边的约姆斯堡。此后斯蒂尔比约恩独自去大陆时，莫尔迪总是远远跟在他身后。斯蒂尔比约恩却像没看见它，不知它在跟前——即便真看见了，也不过拿它当自己的影子，不加理会。这样的时节，悄悄随在他身后的不止莫尔迪：比约恩也常带一队人马，整日远远跟着他，生怕文德人半路袭他。可是文德人始终没有来犯——或因他们望见了近旁的比约恩一伙，或因他们对约姆斯堡和斯蒂尔比约恩旧有的畏惧，或（这理由本不充分）因布里莱夫王与约姆斯堡人之间订有和约。


===Original===
When Yule-month was well past and the days began to lengthen, Styrbiorn began to come out some little from his black and sullen frame of mind. On an evening as they walked on the wall in the bright beams of the westering sun, he spake and said to Biorn his foster-brother, “There was one thing I was wrong to leave behind me. And I have not missed him until now.”

“What was that?” said Biorn.

“That was Moldi.”

“Moldi,” said Biorn, “hath followed thee all winter long. Thou hast never all winter walked abroad but he was behind thee.”

“Thou mockest me,” said Styrbiorn, staring at him.

“It is very truth I tell thee,” said Biorn. “ ’Twas thy man Erland brought him for thee out of Sweden.”

“Erland!” said Styrbiorn. “And thou!” And he turned away his face.


===Chinese===
尤尔月过尽，白昼开始变长，斯蒂尔比约恩才从阴郁沉闷的心境里稍稍缓过来。一天傍晚，两人在西沉的夕阳里沿着堤墙散步，他对义弟比约恩说：“有一样东西，我丢下它是不该的。直到如今，我才觉出想它。”

“是哪样？”比约恩说。

“莫尔迪。”

“莫尔迪，”比约恩说，“跟了你整整一冬。整个冬天，你每次出门，它都在你身后。”

“你取笑我。”斯蒂尔比约恩说，直瞪着他。

“我说的千真万确，”比约恩说。“是你的人埃尔兰把它从瑞典给你带来的。”

“埃尔兰！”斯蒂尔比约恩说。“还有你！”他把脸扭了过去。


===Original===
---

			

Now was winter over, and Jomsburg filled again, and Palnatoki according to his wont held council with the lords of the Jomsburgers to determine of their summer’s work. So they were met together on the seawall overlooking the open sea and the sea-gates and the harbour and the ships. Palnatoki took his seat on a high buttress top that jutted a little above the wall: Styrbiorn sat at the right hand of Palnatoki and Sigvaldi at the left, and the rest sat or stood round about on either side. Every man of them was helmed and byrnied and armed as if for battle, for in such array held they ever their councils of war.


===Chinese===
---

			


冬天过完，约姆斯堡又住满了人。帕尔纳托基依惯例召集约姆斯堡众首领议事，商定今夏的行止。众人在防波堤上聚齐，那里俯瞰着外海、海口、港湾和船只。帕尔纳托基坐在一处高高的堡垛顶上，那垛头微微突出于堤墙之上：斯蒂尔比约恩坐在帕尔纳托基右手，西格瓦迪坐在左手，其余的人或坐或立，分在两边。人人戴盔着甲，全副武装，如临大战——他们的军议向来是这副装束。


===Original===
When they were set, Palnatoki spake among them: “That hath been our custom until now, that I should ask each man in turn what seemeth likeliest and best to him, and what rede he hath to lay before us. But this summer I think we shall choose the rather to hear what Styrbiorn will do. For I think there is no man here in Jomsburg who will not wish to put aside his own enterprise, how needful soever it be, and give aid to Styrbiorn.”

So said Palnatoki, and they took his saying well. Only Sigvaldi leaned closer to Palnatoki and spake somewhat in his ear. “I care not for that,” said Palnatoki: “I will have no conditions.” And he said aloud, “And this is our mind, as it seemeth to me, that we shall give Styrbiorn aid not here nor there, nor for this or that, but freely and for what thing soever he will take in hand.”

Sigvaldi bit his lip but kept silence. And now he with the rest rose up, and every man that was at that council bared sword now and clashed iron on iron, sword on shield, crying aloud that he would aid Styrbiorn.


===Chinese===
众人坐定，帕尔纳托基开口道：“往日的惯例，是我挨个问每个人：何为上策，有何良谋。可今年夏天，我想我们还是先听听斯蒂尔比约恩要做什么。因为约姆斯堡这里，没有一个人不愿撇下自家的营生——哪怕再要紧——来助斯蒂尔比约恩一臂之力。”

帕尔纳托基这样说了，众人都服他的话。只有西格瓦迪凑近帕尔纳托基，在他耳边低语了几句。“那个我不理会，”帕尔纳托基说，“我不要任何条件。”又朗声道：“依我看，我们大伙的心意是这样：助斯蒂尔比约恩，不拘何地，不拘何事，全然无条件，凡他着手要做的事，一律相助。”

西格瓦迪咬了咬嘴唇，忍住没言语。这时他随众人一齐起立，与会的每一个都拔剑出鞘，铁器相击，剑敲盾牌，齐声高喊愿助斯蒂尔比约恩。


===Original===
Styrbiorn swept his eye round from man to man. For a moment he scarce seemed able to speak word. Then in his eager and halting utterance he said, “ ‘Bare is back without brother behind it.’ And I see that shall not be said of me. And now there is but one man and one country I will not bear war against: every land else, but not against the Swede-King. Many will think wonders at this, but I will for no sake bear war against King Eric. This I say, that you may know. But if I fight not against him, fight I must with someone, or I shall burst.”

“What’s this?” said Bui then. “Must thou be cast out o’ the Swede-realm and thine own lawful right for a wench sake? And were it possible for any shame-swollen toad to have the spit-proof face to outlive this disgrace, and not make garters of the guts of him that put it on us?”


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩的目光从一张张脸上扫过。有一瞬他几乎说不出话。随后，用他那种急切而迟滞的语调，他说：“‘背后无兄弟，脊背便裸着。’这话用不到我身上了。如今，只有一个人、一个国，我不愿对之兴兵：其余任何地方都可以，唯独不打瑞典王。许多人会觉得奇怪，但我无论如何不对埃里克王兴兵。这话我说在明处，叫你们知道。可我若不打他，就得打别人，不然我要憋炸了。”

“这是什么话？”布伊说。“为一个女人，你就该被赶出瑞典、丢了自家名正言顺的权利？天下哪只臊气冲天的癞蛤蟆，竟能有这么厚的脸皮，受了这等奇耻大辱还活得下去，不把给我们扣上这耻辱的人开膛破肚、拿他的肠子做绑腿？”


===Original===
Styrbiorn met his scowling and staring look with that smile with which, once his mind was set on anything, he was wont to receive advice or protest or upbraiding. “I will not fight with the Swede-King,” said he.

Bui said, “Thou dost jest with us. This is but to try us. Come, I’ll go with thee. He shall find a hath laid for a pickrell and caught a firedrake. And I’ll ask no guerdon but this, that I may lay in the first brand at the burning of Upsala.”

“I’ll give thee quarry enough, Bui,” said Styrbiorn, “but never that.” Men might see, for all his holding of himself in hand, that this talk tried his temper over much.


===Chinese===
布伊怒目而视，斯蒂尔比约恩却报以微笑——每当他主意已定，劝谏、抗议或是责备，他向来是这么一笑挡回去的。“我不和瑞典王交手。”他说。

布伊说：“你是在耍我们。这不过是试探。来，我随你去。教他晓得：下了套要捉狗鱼，速上来的却是条喷火龙。我不要别的报酬，只求一件事——火烧乌普萨拉时，让我亲手投第一支火把。”

“够你猎的，布伊，”斯蒂尔比约恩说，“唯独这个不行。”看得出，尽管他极力自持，这番话也把他的火气逼到了顶。


===Original===
Sigvaldi spake: “We have promised Styrbiorn this, to follow and uphold him this summer. And methinks there is little wisdom in this, to egg him on to so unhopeful and bad an enterprise as that should be, to go up against the King o’ the Swedes in Upsala. For many a man would find it a hard thing and an ill to keep to his oath, were it to draw us into suchlike folly.”

But the more part of them were of Bui’s mind, that they would back Styrbiorn, were it in this or in aught else beside.

After this Palnatoki spake to each apart, and said it would be best for men to speak no more to Styrbiorn of the ill turn things had taken betwixt him and the Swede-king, and most of all say naught, as Bui had said, of bearing war against the King; “For that is furthest from his mind, and the mere thought thereof is like a spark to a vetch-stack, to light up his grief and ruth anew.”


===Chinese===
西格瓦迪说：“我们已向斯蒂尔比约恩许诺，今夏追随他、扶助他。可依我看，撺掇他去干这般无望又凶险的营生——杀上乌普萨拉去打瑞典王——实在算不得明智。真要把我们拖进这等蠢事里，许多人会发现，守誓竟是件又难又恶的事。”

但多数人站在布伊一边：无论这一件还是别的什么，都要帮斯蒂尔比约恩到底。

事后，帕尔纳托基私下对每个人叮嘱：最好谁也别再向斯蒂尔比约恩提起他与瑞典王之间那桩坏了局的事，更休要说布伊方才那种话，说什么对王兴兵；“那离他的心思最远，那念头一冒头，就像火星落进豆草垛，要把他的悲恸重新点着。”


===Original===
---

			

Styrbiorn sailed east now with the whole power of the Jomsburgers, and made war far and wide in the east countries and laid under him there many kings and folk both of the mainland and of the isles, in such sort that the terror of the name of Jomsburg was upon all the dwellers in those lands beyond aught that had been heard of aforetime. For Styrbiorn, sailing oft in raids and onslaughts with but a few ships, seemed to run all danger out of breath, nor might any host hold ground against him nor escape his onset. And the report did fly of him throughout Wendland and Garthrealm and Estland and all those lands of the eastern seas, that he was no man, but a troll which irons bite not; and under that fear went his enemies against him into battle. But as many as he overcame and conquered he bound to him with oaths and treaties and obliged men far and wide to host-faring with him. And men marked, too, this strange thing of him, that, for all the awe and fear folk had of him, yet there was a scarce a man that once spake with him and knew him face to face but was glad ever after to do his will and be his man. For he drew men to him as the lodestone draws iron.


===Chinese===
---

			


于是斯蒂尔比约恩尽起约姆斯堡大军东征，在东方诸国四下开战，降服了大陆与海岛上的许多君王部族；约姆斯堡之名的威慑，笼罩那些地方的全体居民，达到了前所未闻的地步。因为斯蒂尔比约恩每每只带几条船奔袭突袭，仿佛把一切危险都甩在了身后喘气；任何军队都挡他不住，也逃不脱他的突袭。关于他的传闻飞遍了文德兰、加德王国、埃斯特兰和东方海域的列邦：说他不是人，是刀枪不入的巨魔；他的敌人便是怀着这份恐惧上阵的。而凡被他战败征服的，他都盟誓约束，逼得远近之众随他出征。人们还注意到他身上一件怪事：尽管人人畏他，可但凡与他照过面、亲自识得他的人，此后几乎没有不乐于听命于他、投到他麾下的。他吸引人，如磁石吸铁。


===Original===
After that, he bare war into Wendland, holding it time that the Wends should stand no longer in doubt whether Jomsburg, that was planted on their land’s edge, were theirs or its own. But here gathered against him the war-rush of the Wends, and therewithal King Burisleif, having with him so huge a throng of fighting men as outmanned the Jomsburgers by five or six to one, seized the river banks of both sides below the fleet that had rowed far upstream into the inland parts. And the Wends cast booms athwart the river, cutting off for the men of Jomsburg their way of flight, had that seemed best to them. Their battle there was of the hardest, and there fell many a man of either side, for the Wends were good fighters and the odds by common reckoning hopeless against the men of Jomsburg. Yet in the end, after a long day’s battle, Styrbiorn won the day. These were his peace terms with King Burisleif, that Palnatoki should henceforth hold an Earl’s name and dignity, and the Wends should be at peace henceforth with them of Jomsburg and acknowledge them for their friends and allies, and should aid and comfort them every way they might from that day forth. King Burisleif gave Styrbiorn great store of gifts and treasures, and bound himself to fetch Styrbiorn ships and men to go a-warring with him whensoever he should call for them.

It now drew toward midsummer. They brought home their spoil to Jomsburg and bestowed it there and set their gear in order. Then Styrbiorn said, “That will I now, that we sail in west-viking. And first, into Denmark.”


===Chinese===
随后他兵进文德兰——他认为时机已到，文德人不应再拿不准：坐落在他们国门口的约姆斯堡，究竟是他们的，还是自成一家的。文德人却在此时集结了战潮般的兵力，布里莱夫王也在其中；他麾下战士之多，与约姆斯堡人相比众寡悬殊，五六对一。他们扼住了河道两岸——约姆斯堡的舰队已划行深处上游内陆，退路就此被断。文德人又在河面横起拦栅，把约姆斯堡人万一想走的那条退路也封死了。那一战打得空前惨烈，双方都折损了许多人：文德人是能战的，而照一般的算法，约姆斯堡人毫无胜算。然而鏖战一整日之后，斯蒂尔比约恩还是赢了。他与布里莱夫王议定的和约是：帕尔纳托基此后享有伯爵的名号与尊位；文德人从此与约姆斯堡人和睦相处，认他们为友邦盟邦，并自那日起倾力相助。布里莱夫王赠给斯蒂尔比约恩大批礼物珍宝，并立誓：斯蒂尔比约恩何时开口，他就何时为他调集船队兵员，随他出征。

这时已近仲夏。他们把掳获运回约姆斯堡收贮，整顿好器械。斯蒂尔比约恩说：“我如今想的是：咱们西征去。头一站，丹麦。”


===Original===
It was easy to see that Palnatoki liked not well of this rede. “A Dane am I by kin,” said he, “and my home is in Fion. And plainly I hold not myself for a bigger man than King Harald Gormson, seeing I was content to be fosterer of his son. Give us not too hard a choice.”

At that, Styrbiorn looked black on him for an instant. Then his brow cleared. “I ought not to have asked thee for this, Palnatoki,” he said, and took him by the hand. “Let be, then. We will think on some other way.”

“That is generous in thee,” said Palnatoki. “And that is but what we have learnt to look for in thee, we that be thy friends. But now I will not be behindhand with thee in friendship, nor use thee so unhandsomely, seeing I swear oath to aid thee. And thou shalt have thy way, be it even into Denmark.”

So this was now determined on. Only it seemed good to Palnatoki that he should not himself be with them on this sailing, thinking that, when the time should come, the King should then the easier be brought to eat out of his hand if he should have had no share in the putting of force upon him. So this was their rede, that Palnatoki with those sons of Strut-Harald and one half of the whole fleet that was now in Jomsburg should sail again eastaway and withal raise more power in the east there, and be at tryst with Styrbiorn in Jomsburg the first full moon after midsummer. So might they thereafter all fare together a-warring until summer’s end.


===Chinese===
谁都看得出，帕尔纳托基不喜欢这个主意。“论血亲我是丹麦人，”他说，“家又在菲英。我也从不自命比哈拉尔王·戈姆松更大——我给人家儿子当养父，不是当得心甘情愿么。别把抉择逼得太狠。”

听了这话，斯蒂尔比约恩的脸黑了一瞬，随即开朗。“我不该对你开这个口，帕尔纳托基，”他说着握住他的手。“罢了。咱们另想别的路子。”

“你真是宽宏，”帕尔纳托基说。“这也正是我们这些做你朋友的，一贯指望于你的。可在交情上，我也不能落在你后头，更不能那样亏待你——何况我起过誓要助你。你要怎么干便怎么干，哪怕当真打去丹麦。”

事情就此定了。只是帕尔纳托基觉得，这一航自己不宜同行：他想，来日到了节骨眼上，若他未曾参与对王动强，王便更容易俯首帖耳、任他摆布。于是计议定了：帕尔纳托基带着斯特鲁特-哈拉尔的几个儿子和现驻约姆斯堡的半数船队，重新东行，同时在东方再扩增兵势；仲夏后第一个望月，与斯蒂尔比约恩在约姆斯堡会齐。这样，此后便可合兵一处，一路征战到夏末。


===Original===
Styrbiorn, having with him Biorn and the sons of Veseti and the rest of the host, sailed now in west-viking into Denmark and there made great unpeace. When he had gotten three victories in sea-fights there, he sailed through the Jutland sea into the Limfirth and found there the Dane-King and his host. The King deemed it not hopeful to fight with Styrbiorn, and they made peace there. King Harald had in those days a fair great house of his at Alaburg, and he bade Styrbiorn come ashore there and take guesting. Styrbiorn said yea to that, and now were the ships of King Harald and the ships of Styrbiorn some of them drawn up on the strand of the sea below Alaburg, while other some rode at anchor, for that was a windless haven and a wondrous good riding-place for ships. The King handselled peace to Styrbiorn before all the folk there, but they put off till the morrow all talk of terms and bargains.

That afternoon it so fell out that Styrbiorn, walking in the sun about the home-mead and whiling away the time with looking on the good byres and sheep-pens and on the fire-halls and bowers and other fair buildings that the King had in Alaburg, came on a sudden round a corner of the great hall face to face with Thyri, Harald’s daughter. She was bareheaded and the sun shone in blue gleams amid the jet-black curls and folds of her hair. She wore a gown of dark-blue woollen stuff and a silken cloak, rich and costly, of like colour. She had in her arms a little furry rabbit that nestled against her bosom and, with long ears laid back, thrust its nose under her arm. It so befell that Styrbiorn was humming a tune and Thyri too was singing softly to her rabbit, rocking it and smiling to see it nestle so and to feel its soft nose nuzzling. So meeting, they stopped suddenly from their singing and halted as if each would have turned back to avoid the other, and their faces flushed red. Styrbiorn stepped aside now to let her pass. But Thyri stood still as if waiting for him to say somewhat. She stood looking down at the little rabbit in her arms, stroking its ears and head. After a little she looked up at him and said, “There was a foumart caught it, but I saved it.”


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩带着比约恩、维塞蒂的儿子们和大队人马，扬帆西征，杀入丹麦，掀起了大乱。他在海上三战三捷之后，穿过日德兰海驶入利姆峡湾，在那里遇上了丹麦王和他的大军。王自知与斯蒂尔比约恩交战没有胜算，双方便在那里讲和。哈拉尔王那时在阿拉堡有一座宏伟的宅邸，便请斯蒂尔比约恩上岸做客。斯蒂尔比约恩应允了。于是哈拉尔王的船与斯蒂尔比约恩的船，一部分拖上阿拉堡下方的海滩，一部分抛锚泊在港中——那是一个没有风浪的良港，泊船好得出奇。王当着那里众人之面，与斯蒂尔比约恩行礼授和；至于条款交涉，则一概推到明天。

那天下午，斯蒂尔比约恩在阳光下信步走到宅前的草场，看看那些讲究的牛栏羊圈，看看火房、闺阁和哈拉尔王在阿拉堡的其他漂亮屋舍，消磨时光。转过大厅一角，他冷不防与哈拉尔的女儿蒂丽撞了个对面。她没戴帽子，阳光在她乌黑鬈发的波纹间闪出蓝幽幽的光。她穿一件深蓝毛料长裙，罩一件同色的绸缎斗篷，华贵考究。她怀里抱着一只毛茸茸的小兔子：兔子偎在她胸口，长耳朵贴伏在背上，鼻子直往她臂弯底下钻。说来也巧，斯蒂尔比约恩正哼着调子，蒂丽也正柔声给她的兔子唱歌，一边摇着它，看它偎得那个亲热劲儿、觉着它软软的鼻子乱拱，不由微笑。这样撞见，两人歌声戛然而止，都顿住了脚，仿佛各自都想退回去避开对方，脸上一齐红了。斯蒂尔比约恩侧身让路，请她先过。蒂丽却站着不动，像在等他开口。她低头望着怀里的小兔，抚着它的耳朵和脑袋。过了一会儿，她抬眼望着他说：“先前叫鸡貂咬着了它，是我救下来的。”


===Original===
Styrbiorn said nothing, meeting her gaze in an uneasy silence. Then on the sudden they both smiled.

That night the King made Styrbiorn and his folk good entertainment. Styrbiorn spake most to Thyri, and she was easy and open in her talk with him. There was no word nor look of hers that pointed to that bad night that had parted them in Upsala, but it was as if her mind had been wiped clean now of all knowledge and remembrance of it. And wondrous it seemed with what friendship she took up the threads of their days that had gone before, so that no dear brother and sister, meeting again after many years, might have known more familiarly the jests and likings each of other.


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩一言不发，不安地沉默着，迎住她的目光。突然，两人都笑了。

当夜，王殷勤设宴款待斯蒂尔比约恩一行。斯蒂尔比约恩同蒂丽说话最多，她同他谈得也自在坦然。她的一言一语、一颦一笑，都不带一点那晚在乌普萨拉拆散他们的阴影，仿佛她心里已把那件事的知识与记忆，擦拭得干干净净。她怀着何等深的情谊，把往日相处的线头一根根接了起来——亲兄亲妹阔别多年重逢，也不过这样熟悉彼此的玩笑与喜好罢了。


===Original===
Suddenly he said to her, “Men have told me thou art to wed King Burisleif.”

“So it is,” said she, as if roused suddenly from some dream.

“Is that with thy good will?” asked Styrbiorn.

All her easy way with him was gone. She lifted her head proudly as if to say, what had he to do with these things? Then her eyes met his. She lowered her gaze and said, scarce to be heard, “No.”

“It is an old man?” said Styrbiorn.

“So I am told,” she said, soft as a breath.


===Chinese===
忽然他对她说：“有人告诉我，你要嫁布里莱夫王了。”

“是这样。”她说，仿佛猛然从什么梦里惊醒。

“这是你自愿的么？”斯蒂尔比约恩问。

她那份自在顿时消失无踪。她傲然抬起头，仿佛在说：这些事与你何干？随即她的眼睛遇上了他的。她垂下目光，声音低得几乎听不见：“不是。”

“他是个老头子？”斯蒂尔比约恩说。

“人家这么说。”她说，轻得像一口气息。


===Original===
They kept silence for a while. Then Styrbiorn said, “I did wrong. Is it too late?”

Thyri lifted her eyes full on his. “No,” she said.

---

			


===Chinese===
两人沉默了一阵。随后斯蒂尔比约恩说：“当初是我错了。现在还来得及么？”

蒂丽抬眼直直望着他。“来得及。”她说。

---

			



===Original===
Next morning Styrbiorn and Harald the King went forth to speak together apart upon a bluff of rock that overlooks the strand of the sea below Alaburg. These were Styrbiorn’s peace-terms that he laid before the King: that the Danes should fetch Styrbiorn an hundred ships full-manned to follow and fight for him in three great battles, and that he besides should have to wife Thyri, Harald’s daughter.

It was easily seen that the King was not minded to have aught to do with such terms.

So now they talked on this for a long while, turning it over this way and that, but no whit the nearer were they agreed together. “That have I heard,” said the King at length, “that thou hast been little of a tame horse to lead for thine unfriends. But this thou asketh passeth all reason, and I will not do it.”


===Chinese===
次日清晨，斯蒂尔比约恩与哈拉尔王出到外面，在俯瞰阿拉堡下方海滩的一处石崖上单独会谈。斯蒂尔比约恩向王提出的和平条款是：丹麦人须为斯蒂尔比约恩备齐百条满员的战船，随他征战三场大战；此外，还要把哈拉尔的女儿蒂丽嫁给他为妻。

谁都看得出，王无意理会这样的条款。

于是两人就此事谈了许久，翻来覆去地磨，却半点谈不拢。“我听说过，”王终于开口，“对你的仇家来说，你从来不是一匹牵得住的驯马。可你这个要价，越出了一切情理，我不答应。”


===Original===
“That is ill, then,” said Styrbiorn. “For so it is, King, that hereon hangeth all our friendship betwixt you and us Jomsburgers.”

The King looked him in the eye. “I would have thee answer me this,” he said: “Who art thou, that Jomsburg lieth in thy mouth? Let Palnatoki speak for Jomsburg, and thou for thyself.”

Styrbiorn laughed. “Well,” he said, “I will speak for myself.”

“And I,” said the King, “have given thee thine answer.”

“But it is an answer I will not take,” said Styrbiorn.


===Chinese===
“那就糟了，”斯蒂尔比约恩说。“王啊，事情就是这样：你我之间、你们与约姆斯堡人之间的全部交情，都系在这一桩上。”

王直视着他的眼睛。“我要你回答我，”他说，“你算老几，张口闭口约姆斯堡？约姆斯堡的事，让帕尔纳托基来说；你，只说你自己。”

斯蒂尔比约恩笑了。“好吧，”他说，“我只说我自己。”

“而我，”王说，“答复也给你了。”

“可这个答复，我不收。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。


===Original===
The King looked angry as a man might be. For a time he held his peace, then, suddenly looking up at Styrbiorn, “Would thou mightest go from hence,” he said fiercely, “and all the fiends of hell go with thee. There was never ill words, let alone ill deeds, ’twixt me and Jomsburg, till that thou camest hither. With Palnatoki and every man else of Jomsburg I should lightly be set at one. Would I might never look upon thy face again.”

Styrbiorn smiled. “I have tried your temper over much, King. And now I’m sorry for it. The ships I will forgo. But to knit our friendship the closer you shall give me your daughter.”

“That is not to be thought of,” said King Harald. “Thou hast had thy chance of that, and little with my good will, when thine uncle backed thee. I scarce think he’ll back thee again, though.” With that, he gave Styrbiorn a lowering look. “Truly thou hast the front of a dog,” said he: “ ‘Be wary with ale and with another’s wife.’ When thou’st learnt that, come and ask me again for my daughter.”

The hair of Styrbiorn’s head rose up a little, and his face became dark as blood. “I’ the meantime,” said the King, “she is promised to King Burisleif. And the wedding is to be this summer.”


===Chinese===
王怒形于色，怒得像常人所能有的那样。他沉默了一阵，随后猛地抬眼望着斯蒂尔比约恩。“巴不得你这就走，”他狠狠地说，“叫地狱的群魔一齐跟你走。在你来之前，我与约姆斯堡之间从无恶言，更休说恶行。同帕尔纳托基、同约姆斯堡的每一个人，我都轻易讲得拢。但愿我这辈子再不用见你这张脸。”

斯蒂尔比约恩笑了。“我把你的性子逼得太狠了，王。这一节，是我对不住你。船，我可以不要。可要教咱们的交情结得更紧，你得把女儿给我。”

“休想。”哈拉尔王说。“这门亲事你的机会有过一回，还是你叔父替你撑腰——本就没多少是我的情愿。依我看，他这回可不会再替你撑腰了。”说着，他阴沉地瞪了斯蒂尔比约恩一眼。“你真真长着一张狗脸，”他说，“‘美酒与别人的妻，须防着些。’等你学乖了这条，再来向我求女儿吧。”

斯蒂尔比约恩的头发根根微微竖起，脸色黑得像血。“眼下，”王说，“她已许给布里莱夫王，婚礼就在今年夏天。”


===Original===
“Is that with her good will?” said Styrbiorn.

“It is my will,” said the King.

“Wilt thou leave it in her free choice?”

“I will not,” said the King.


===Chinese===
“这是她自愿的么？”斯蒂尔比约恩说。

“是我的意愿。”王说。

“你不肯让她自己拿主意？”

“我不肯。”王说。


===Original===
Styrbiorn came a pace nearer to Harald the King and stood over him like as one fighting dog stands a-bristling over another. “I love not talk and chat,” said he; “yet, to please your whim, King, I have spent two hours a-talking. Here be no eavesdroppers. Thou seest under thy feet there my ships and thine. Think well: for this choice I give thee, either to stretch out thine hand and betrothe to me thy daughter, and swear friendship to me, and these hundred ships too; or if not, I will flit thee ashipboard this very night, over seas to Kirialaland or the Finn-heaths of Smaland or otherwhere, and deliver thee up to savage men shall shear off thy nose and thine ears and draw out thy tripes for their dogs to eat. Choose, then.”

His words, tripping and stumbling, came like a clatter of weapons about the King’s ears. The King looked up in his eyes without blenching, a deadly look. A dirty greyish pallor was on the King’s cheek and brow: his lips were drawn back, showing in a set grin his teeth and gums and that ugly great tooth of his that jutted forth like a wild swine’s tusk blue and monstrous in the left-hand corner of his mouth. His right hand was clenched like iron on his sword-hilt, but his sword abode safe home in the scabbard. When Styrbiorn had ended there was deep silence, save for the faint sound of the wash of the waves on the seashore below them.


===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩朝哈拉尔王逼近一步，居高临下罩住他，像一条斗狗耸毛竖立，罩住另一条。“我素来不喜欢磨嘴皮子，”他说；“可为了迁就你的兴致，王，我已足足费了两个钟头讲谈。这里没有偷听的耳朵。你脚下便是我的船和你的船。想清楚些：我给你两条路——要么伸手与我结亲，把女儿许给我，对我立誓修好，那百条船也一并算上；要么，今夜我就把你装上船，渡海送去基里亚拉兰、送去斯莫兰的芬兰荒原，或别的什么地方，把你交给蛮人，割掉你的鼻子耳朵，掏出你的肚肠喂他们的狗。你挑吧。”

他的话磕磕绊绊、急促踉跄，像一阵兵器碰撞的铿锵，在王的耳边炸响。王抬眼直望进他的眼睛，毫不畏缩，那目光是致命的。王的颊上额上罩着一层脏兮兮的灰白：嘴唇咧开，牙和牙床板成一副僵笑，左边嘴角支着那颗丑陋的大死牙，青黑狰狞，像野猪的獠牙。他的右手铁一般攥住剑柄，剑却稳稳睡在鞘里。斯蒂尔比约恩说完，四下里一片深沉的静默，只听见下方海边浪头拍岸的轻响。


===Original===
“So it is, Styrbiorn,” said King Harald at length, “that thou art a strong man. But I think thy strength shall one day overreach itself. I’ the mean season, thou must have thy way, here as in what else besides. But if thou wilt humour me so far, to forbear from shaming me before mine own folk, that will seem somewhat.”

“Then is the bargain made,” said Styrbiorn. His wrath was gone by like a squall in March, and that good and merry look that his friends knew in him crept like spring sunlight from his eyes and brow down to his mouth. He reached out his hand. The King’s hand in Styrbiorn’s grip was like a dead fish. Styrbiorn said, “True it is, there is no witness of our bargain. Yet I think thou’lt keep troth with me. But I will have thine oath too, King.”


===Chinese===
“事情就是这样，斯蒂尔比约恩，”哈拉尔王终于开口，“你是个强者。可我想，你的强大终有一天会跌在自己的强上。眼下的局面，也同别的一样，由你。只是你若肯给我留这一分情面，别当着我自家人的面羞辱我，那也算得一点意思。”

“那么，买卖成了。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。他的怒气已如三月的一场狂风扫荡而逝，朋友们熟悉的那副良善欢快的神色，如春日的阳光，从他的眉眼间一路漫到嘴角。他伸出手去。王的手被斯蒂尔比约恩攥在掌中，像一条死鱼。斯蒂尔比约恩说：“不错，这桩买卖没有见证。可我想，你会与我守信的。不过我还要你起个誓，王。”


===Original===
The King sware by Christ and Mary.

“I’ll have thee tied all ways,” said Styrbiorn. “Thou must now swear by Thor.”

The King sware by Thor.

Styrbiorn said, “Now we understand one another, thou and I. And now I shall think well of our friendship.”


===Chinese===
王指着基督与马利亚起誓。

“我要把你四面八方都捆牢，”斯蒂尔比约恩说。“现在你还得指托尔起誓。”

王指托尔起了誓。

斯蒂尔比约恩说：“如今你我彼此心里都明白了。往后，我便信咱们的交情是真。”

## XIII / 十三



===Original===
The Sailing of the Fleet

When it was given out by what agreement King Harald and Styrbiorn were made friends men wondered much, thinking that here was a new strong wind set in, to blow so suddenly away that old standing bridal pact made with King Burisleif. The King’s men for the most part thought that he had shown wisdom in these dealings, and in a day or two were all quarrels forgot that had arisen betwixt the Dane-folk and them of Jom; and most men accounted this to Styrbiorn. And they marked how even the King himself seemed, in outward show at least, of one mind with Styrbiorn and entreated him as he had been his very son. Styrbiorn and Thyri were wed now without more ado.

Now was the time come near for their keeping tryst with Palnatoki, and Styrbiorn sent Biorn Asbrandson his foster-brother to Jomsburg to let Palnatoki know they were with King Harald and how that affair had sped, so that Palnatoki might come now with all his power of fighting ships to Styrbiorn in Alaburg, instead of waiting for him in Jomsburg as they before determined. For this was in Styrbiorn’s mind now, that they should sail that summer west over England’s-main and lay under them those western lands. Men spoke well of the choice of land there, and the land was called a good land and worth the harrying.

In due time came Biorn back alone. He had this to say: that Palnatoki was there indeed in Jomsburg with his host, and had besides a great multitude of men and ships gathered from the east; but when he would have had these follow him to Alaburg, then said they all that they were come thither to Jomsburg to meet with Styrbiorn, and that they were not minded to go thence at any man’s bidding until Styrbiorn should come to fetch them; but that when he should come, then they would go with him whithersoever he would, and do his commands. Styrbiorn saw there was naught for it but he must fare to Jomsburg. That was the tenth night now of their guesting with King Harald in Alaburg. They sat at supper, and Styrbiorn was glad and merry as he had not been these many months.

===Chinese===
舰队启航

哈拉尔王与斯蒂尔比约恩因何缘故修好，一经传扬开去，众人都大为称奇，心想这真是一股陡然转起的强劲新风向，竟把与布里莱夫王订下的那桩老大婚约一下子吹了个无影无踪。王的人多半以为他此事处置得明智，不出一两日，丹麦人与约姆斯堡人之间生出的种种嫌隙便尽数忘却了；多数人都把这算在斯蒂尔比约恩账上。人们还留意到，连王本人似乎——至少表面看来——也与斯蒂尔比约恩一心一意，待他如亲生儿子一般。斯蒂尔比约恩与蒂丽就此成婚，再无多话。

与帕尔纳托基会师之期将近，斯蒂尔比约恩派义弟比约恩·阿斯布兰德松前往约姆斯堡，告知帕尔纳托基他们已在哈拉尔王处，此事如何了结，好让帕尔纳托基如今就率他全部战船的兵力来阿拉堡与斯蒂尔比约恩会合，不必再照先前商定的在约姆斯堡空等。因为斯蒂尔比约恩此时心里打的主意是：当年夏天西渡英格兰本土，把那些西方土地收归脚下。人们都称道那边土地的选择，说那是块好地，值得一趟劫掠。

比约恩如期独自回来了。他带回这样的话：帕尔纳托基确已率部众到了约姆斯堡，此外还有从东方聚来的大批人马船只；但当他要这些人随他去阿拉堡时，他们都说，他们是到约姆斯堡来会斯蒂尔比约恩的，斯蒂尔比约恩不来接，任谁的号令他们也不走；但他若来了，他们便随他去天涯海角，听他差遣。斯蒂尔比约恩明白，别无他法，只好亲赴约姆斯堡。这时他们在阿拉堡做客哈拉尔王处已是第十夜。众人坐着用晚饭，斯蒂尔比约恩欢喜畅快，几个月来不曾这般。

===Original===
It was wonderful stormy weather that night, and it so befell that there was sailing by the Jutland coast a little north of the east mouth of the Limfirth a man of Iceland bound south out of the Swede-realm on a cheaping voyage. The wind and the sea drave his ship aland and she was broken in pieces in the shallows, but the skipper and his ship’s company saved themselves and came ashore. That was about sunset. This chapman was named Worm Grimkelson. Oft had he sailed a-cheaping into the land of the Danes aforetime, and it was ever King Harald’s wont to make him welcome. There was no dwellings of men thereabouts whereas Worm and his shipmates were come ashore, so they took this rede, to make haste to Alaburg and crave shelter of Harald the King. They fell in with no man on their way thither through the wind and rain, and so it was that when Worm came into the hall and stood before the King and gave him greeting he knew not at all that these were Jomsburgers a-guesting there with King Harald, nor that Styrbiorn was there; nor was Styrbiorn known to him by sight nor any of those that were with him.

The King bade thralls look to Worm’s men and give them to eat and drink, but for Worm himself the King let make room at the board. The King asked him was there good cheaping this year, and Worm answered it was better than some years and worse than others. The King asked whence they were come, and Worm answered they were come from Sigtun.

“Thou art the first man this year, Worm,” said King Harald, “to come south hither out of the Swede-realm. What tidings hast thou to tell us thence? For I think it good sport to hear tales and tidings a-nights when men have eaten and supped.”

The chapman answered and said, “There is naught newer, Lord, than that which befell last summer’s end, when Styrbiorn the Strong was fled away out of Sweden because of King Eric, and the King let crown his young son Olaf joint King with him in Styrbiorn’s stead. And he is called Olaf the Lap-King, because he was crowned a-sitting in his mother’s lap, being but two winters old as is said. And this is in every man’s mouth there, and is thought great tidings in the realm of Sweden.”

===Chinese===
那夜风雨大得惊人。恰有一名冰岛人驾船从瑞典国境南下贩货，正沿着日德兰海岸、利姆峡湾东口以北约莫一点的地方行驶。风浪把他的船直推向岸，船在浅水里撞得粉碎，好在船主和船伙们自救上了岸。那时正当日落。这位商贾名叫沃姆·格里姆凯尔松。他从前常航海去丹麦人地面做买卖，哈拉尔王素来有款待他的惯例。沃姆和船伙们上岸的地方四下无人烟，他们便定下这条计策：赶紧赶往阿拉堡，求哈拉尔王收留庇护。一路风雨，他们没遇见一个人；于是当沃姆走进大厅立在王前行礼问安时，他全然不知这些是在哈拉尔王处做客的约姆斯堡人，不知斯蒂尔比约恩就在此地；斯蒂尔比约恩的模样他不认得，随行的那些人他一个也不认得。

王吩咐奴仆们照看沃姆的随从，给他们吃喝；至于沃姆本人，王命人在桌边让出个位子。王问他今年买卖可好，沃姆答说比有的年份好些，比有的年份差些。王问他们从何处来，沃姆答说从西格图纳来。

“沃姆，你是今年头一个从瑞典国境南来此地的人。”哈拉尔王说，“那边有什么消息讲给我们听？夜里众人吃饱喝足了，听听说部新闻，我想是一桩乐事。”

那商人答道：“主上，最新的莫过于去年夏末的事：大力士斯蒂尔比约恩被埃里克王逼得逃离瑞典，王便让自己幼子奥拉夫加冕为共治之王，顶了斯蒂尔比约恩的位子。那孩子人称‘膝上王’奥拉夫，因为加冕时他坐在母亲膝上，据说当时不过两岁。此事在那边人人挂在嘴上，瑞典国中都当作天大的新闻。”

===Original===
King Harald’s face was lighted with a smile full of evil will. “These be tidings indeed,” said he. “Take heed and rule thy speech, though for yonder in the high seat sitteth Styrbiorn the Strong, and I thought thou hadst known him, having thyself ere now fared a-cheaping in the Swede-realm.”

The chapman’s knees were loosened when he understood that this was Styrbiorn. And now were all men’s eyes turned on Styrbiorn to see how he would take these news. Now so it was that at the first naming of his name by the chapman Styrbiorn waxed red to the ears and the roots of his hair. But when he heard of the crowning of Olaf the Lap-King he turned white with anger. He was leaned forth over the table on his elbows, with nostrils wide and in his eyes a light like as is in a lynx’s eyes about to spring. He had in his right hand in that instant an ale-horn, and so mightily his hand shut on the horn that the horn cracked and burst under his handgrip and the ale was wasted on the board. The chapman was afeared beholding him, and all men beheld him with some dread, for he seemed like to a man with the berserk-gang upon him, and well they deemed that that should be the bane of many a man there if Styrbiorn should break forth in that kind amongst them. So for a full minute’s space not a man stirred nor spake in that hall, gazing all on Styrbiorn. Then, seeming to master himself, he spake and said, “When was this crowning?”

“Lord,” answered the chapman, shaking and trembling, “they told me that was the third day after your going out from Upsala.”

Styrbiorn smote down with a crash on the table the crushed ale-horn and threw back his head with a great laugh. Men thought there was naught good in the sound of that laughter. Then he drew from his arm a ring of gold, heavy and broad, and tossed it to Worm saying, “There’s thanks for thy news of the Lap-King.” Then he rose up and took by the hand Thyri, that was somewhat pale and shaken with these things. “Come, sweetheart,” said he, “let’s to bed. I must sail at daybreak for Jomsburg.”

===Chinese===
哈拉尔王脸上浮起一个满含恶意的笑。“这可真是新闻。”他说，“说话小心些，措辞谨慎些，因为那边高座上坐着的，正是大力士斯蒂尔比约恩。我还当你认得他呢，你自己从前也去瑞典地面贩过货。”

商人一听此人便是斯蒂尔比约恩，膝弯都软了。此刻满厅的眼睛都转向斯蒂尔比约恩，看他如何消受这消息。当初商人头一次提到他的名字时，斯蒂尔比约恩涨红了脸，红到耳根、红到发根；待听到“膝上王”奥拉夫加冕，他气得脸色煞白。他两肘撑在桌上，身子前倾，鼻孔张大，眼里一道光，如山猫扑前眼中的光。那一瞬他右手里正握着一只酒角，手一收，握力之猛，酒角在他掌下迸裂，麦酒泼了一桌。商人看得害怕，众人看他也都带着几分畏惧，因为他像是狂战士之怒已然上身的样子；他们心里有数：斯蒂尔比约恩若当着他们发作起来，只怕在场的许多人都要遭殃。就这样整整一分钟，厅中无人动一动、说一句话，人人盯着斯蒂尔比约恩。然后，他像是强自按捺住了，开口道：“加冕是在何时？”

“主上，”商人答道，浑身发抖，“他们对我说，是您离开乌普萨拉之后第三天。”

斯蒂尔比约恩把那只捏碎的酒角砰地砸在桌上，仰头放声大笑。人们觉得那笑声里没有一点吉音。随后他从臂上褪下一只又重又阔的金环，抛给沃姆，说：“谢你膝上王的新闻，这个给你。”然后他起身，拉起蒂丽的手——这几番光景已叫她有些发白、有些发颤。“来，心肝，”他说，“睡觉去。天一亮我就得动身去约姆斯堡。”

---

===Original===
Styrbiorn busked him for the east next day at sunrise with nine ships. Ere he set forth he talked nigh an hour secretly with Biorn in Biorn’s shut bed afore that men were astir. Biorn fared with him and Bessi Thorlakson. The rest of the host he set under the command of Bui the Thick, and bade them await his coming thither again to Alaburg, and that should be on the thirtieth day. King Harald noted these things. He was eager to know what Styrbiorn meant to do, but Styrbiorn would tell him nothing, only bidding him wait for him in Alaburg. “And what of Thyri?” said the King. “That thou shouldst sicken of her after a seven-nights’ turtle-doving is a strange unheard of thing.”

“Thyri saileth east with me,” he answered.

“East to Jomsburg?” said the King. “Thou can’st not bring thy wife into Jomsburg. There hath never been any woman brought thither.”

“That concerneth not thee,” said Styrbiorn.

===Chinese===
第二天日出时分，斯蒂尔比约恩整装东行，带了九条船。动身之前，趁众人未起，他在比约恩闭掩的箱床里与比约恩密谈了将近一个时辰。比约恩与贝西·索拉克松随他同行。其余部众，他交由壮汉布伊统带，命他们在阿拉堡等他回来，日期定在第三十天。哈拉尔王把这些都看在眼里。他极想探知斯蒂尔比约恩意欲何为，斯蒂尔比约恩却什么也不肯说，只叫他在阿拉堡等着。“那蒂丽怎么办？”王说，“才燕好了七个夜晚就腻了她，这可是件旷古未闻的怪事。”

“蒂丽随我东行。”他答道。

“东去约姆斯堡？”王说，“你不能把妻子带进约姆斯堡。从来不曾有过女人被带进那里。”

“那与你无干。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。

===Original===
The King was very ill content.

They went aboard now and sailed down the firth. But in Alaburg Harald the King abode ill at ease. Much he questioned Bui and Sigurd and the other lords of the Jomsburgers that were there, but found them but a dry well to draw from. And much he chafed to have sailed away out of the Limfirth with his following nor await Styrbiorn’s return, or by what way soever to be rid of the Jomsburgers. But this seemed naught hopeful, but full of risk. So there he abode, chafing and discontented, and day by day the Dane-folk flocked to him in Alaburg according to the host-bidding he had made at Styrbiorn’s behest up and down the land.

Styrbiorn came to Jomsburg about noontide the third day. When Palnatoki beheld Thyri there he took Styrbiorn apart and said, “What is this? She must go back. Thou knowest our law, that there shall be no woman brought into Jomsburg.”

“There shall be now,” said Styrbiorn. His eyes were fierce, and his speech stumbled more than of wont. “All things else that are mine I stake on this throw, to fare north now into Sweden and take it by force. But not her. Her I leave with you in Jomsburg to keep her safe for me. She is the Queen. She is the apple of mine eye, Palnatoki.”

===Chinese===
王心里极不痛快。

众人随即登船，顺峡湾而下。哈拉尔王留在阿拉堡，坐立不安。他再三盘问布伊、西格德和在那里约姆斯堡人的其他头领们，却发现他们好比一口打不出水的枯井。他也很想带着自己的人马驶出利姆峡湾一走了之，不必等斯蒂尔比约恩回来，或者想个什么法子脱开约姆斯堡人。但这看起来毫无指望，倒满是凶险。于是他便待在那里，焦躁而怏怏；丹麦人遵照他奉斯蒂尔比约恩之命在全国上下的征召，一天天涌到阿拉堡来投他。

第三天晌午时分，斯蒂尔比约恩到了约姆斯堡。帕尔纳托基看见蒂丽在那里，便把斯蒂尔比约恩拉到一旁说：“这是怎么回事？她必须回去。你是知道我们的法律的：不得携任何女人进入约姆斯堡。”

“如今就有了。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。他目光凶狠，说话比平日更加结巴。“我所有其余的一切，都押在这一掷上：即刻北征瑞典，凭武力夺取它。唯独她不行。我把她留在约姆斯堡你这里，替我看护她的周全。她就是我的王后。她是我的眼珠子，帕尔纳托基。”

===Original===
Palnatoki stood staring at him an instant with his eagle eyes; then, “This is a new turn,” he said. “To Sweden?”

“I have heard tidings,” said Styrbiorn. “Slow was I to raise war against King Eric. Until now he hath done me good. Thou knowest I abode quiet when the fates of ill luck sundered me and him, albeit he withheld from me my father’s inheritance. I had rather let all go than bear war against him. But now hath he wrought me this shame and evil, to let crown a brat in my stead. And that is as much as to say I shall be cast out of my kingdom forever. And that pat too, without thought or delay, while I near burst myself holding myself in. And now there is no help. It must be tried out now whether he or I be the stronger, and which of us must be King in Upsala.”

Palnatoki took him by the hand. “I will do this for thee, Styrbiorn,” he said. “And it is a thing I would not do for any other man, nor for myself neither. She shall come in. And now we must take rede for these greater matters.”

===Chinese===
帕尔纳托基用他那双鹰眼盯了他一瞬；然后说：“这可是个新转机。去瑞典？”

“我听到了消息。”斯蒂尔比约恩说，“要我对埃里克王兴兵，我本是迟迟不肯的。至今为止他待我不薄。你知道，当年厄运把我们两个拆散时，我安分守己，尽管他扣着父亲遗产不分给我。我宁可什么都不要，也不愿对他开战。可如今他给了我这场羞辱、这桩恶行：让一个奶娃娃加冕顶替我。这无异于说，我要被永远逐出我的王国。而且来得这么轻巧，连想都不想、拖都不拖，就在我拼命按捺自己的当口。如今已无转圜。现在必须见个分晓：是他强还是我强，乌普萨拉的王座该归我们中的哪一个。”

帕尔纳托基握住他的手。“这件事我为你破例，斯蒂尔比约恩。”他说，“换任何一个人我都不肯，连我自己也不肯。让她进来吧。如今这些大事，我们须好好合计合计。”

---

===Original===
In such wise was Queen Thyri taken into Jomsburg. Twenty-two days they abode there fitting out their ships and mustering the Wendland levies and others from those eastern parts which Styrbiorn had of late laid under him, till there were well nigh six score ships, both great ships and small, all manned and armed in Jomsburg harbour. At dawn of the twenty-third day they went ashipboard. It was a grey and misty morning, windless, with a drizzle of rain in the air. Styrbiorn came down to the ships with Palnatoki and Sigvaldi. Sigvaldi was to bide in Jomsburg as captain there. Palnatoki had been sick of a sickness these four or five days past, but there was no holding him from faring with Styrbiorn.

Styrbiorn had bound raven’s wings on his helm. When Sigvaldi saw it, he said, “Some men would say thou wast fey, Styrbiorn, seeing thee commit so proud a blasphemy as bear raven’s wings on thy helm. For this is a thing befitteth no man, nor yet the lesser Gods neither, but the All-Father alone. If thou wilt be counseled by me, leave them off. For well I think they shall bring us ill luck.”

“They shall be taken off,” answered he, “when they shall be hewn off in battle before Upsala. Or when I sit King there. No other way.”

“I counsel thee,” said Sigvaldi, “out of my love and loyalty. A man might say, to look on thee, thou hadst now clapt up thy good luck in a cage of gold. But I feared thee less in thy mad melancholy discontents last winter.”

===Chinese===
蒂丽王后就这样进了约姆斯堡。他们在那里盘桓了二十二天，修整船只，召集文德兰及东方各地——斯蒂尔比约恩新近收服的那些地方——的兵员，直到约姆斯堡港中聚起将近一百二十条船，大小俱全，人员军械齐备。第二十三天拂晓，众人登船。那是个灰蒙蒙的雾天清晨，无风，空中飘着蒙蒙细雨。斯蒂尔比约恩与帕尔纳托基、西格瓦迪一同下到船边。西格瓦迪将留守约姆斯堡，做那里的统领。帕尔纳托基已病了四五天，但谁也拦不住他随斯蒂尔比约恩同行。

斯蒂尔比约恩在头盔上绑了一对渡鸦翅膀。西格瓦迪见了，说道：“斯蒂尔比约恩，有人要说你死期临头了，看你行下这等狂傲的渎神之事，竟把渡鸦翅膀戴在头盔上。这等物事，凡人受用不起，连次一等的天神也受用不起，唯有万物之父才行。你若肯听我劝，就取下来吧。我很疑心它们要给我们带来厄运。”

“自会取下来的。”他答道，“在乌普萨拉城下的战场上被人砍下来的时候。或者，等我坐上那里的王座的时候。再无别的方式。”

“我劝你，”西格瓦迪说，“是出于我的爱与忠心。看你这样子，人家要说，你如今把自己的好运关进了一只金笼子。可去年冬天你狂郁不平的时候，我倒还没这么怕你。”

===Original===
Styrbiorn laughed. “A doomed man’s ice-hole,” he said, “is never frozen. Have done with thy womanish fears.”

But now befell this misfortune, that Palnatoki going aboard of his ship slipped foot and fell and brake his leg there. Men thought this a strange ill hap. Spite of this, and his sickness yet heavy upon him, Palnatoki would not be left behind; till in the end Styrbiorn got his way with him, that he being unmeet for battle should stay this while in Jomsburg and let Sigvaldi fare in his stead with Styrbiorn into Sweden. There were men that laid their heads together, seeing in this thing an omen, and in the raven’s wings. But most of them trowed so much in their own might and main and in Styrbiorn that they were in no mind to quiddle upon such light matters as these. Sigvaldi held his peace, and busked him without more ado to sail in Palnatoki’s stead. But men who knew him thought they knew that his heart was not in that sailing.

Thyri came down with them to the ships. She was brave and jolly of mien. But Biorn bethought him now of her dream in Roiskeld two years since; and he thought he knew she was finding it hard to put on so blithe a face and press down her fears, seeing her lord now in very earnest about faring North with a great host of war.

They rowed out now from Jomsburg sea-gates in a windless calm. Styrbiorn stood on the poop of his great ship *Ironbeak*. She was both longer and taller of build than the other ships, so that every man else in that host must still be looking up at Styrbiorn and Styrbiorn down on them. Huge and dark was the spread of the raven’s wings above his head, fitter for a God than for a mortal man. The mist thinned and broke, and the sun looked over the land’s edge and beamed full on him with the fresh and clear brightness of morning.

===Chinese===
斯蒂尔比约恩笑了。“命定要死的人，”他说，“他冰上的窟窿是冻不上的。收起你那娘们儿的恐惧吧。”

不料这时出了桩不幸：帕尔纳托基登船时脚下打滑，摔倒在那里，跌断了腿。众人都觉得这是个古怪的凶兆。尽管如此，加上病体沉重，帕尔纳托基还是不肯留下；直到最后斯蒂尔比约恩才说动了他：他既不堪一战，就这一回暂留约姆斯堡，让西格瓦迪替他随斯蒂尔比约恩进瑞典。有人交头接耳，把这事、把渡鸦翅膀都看作预兆。但大多数人太信自家的力气和斯蒂尔比约恩，不肯在这些细枝末节上计较。西格瓦迪一言不发，随即收拾停当，准备代帕尔纳托基出征。但了解他的人自以为看得出：他这一趟，心思并不在船上。

蒂丽随他们下到船边。她神情勇敢而欢快。但比约恩想起两年前她在罗斯基勒做的那个梦；他心想他看得分明：眼看丈夫当真要率大军北征，她是费了多大的劲才装出这么一张快活面孔、压住心头的恐惧。

舰队在海面无风的平静中，从约姆斯堡海门划出。斯蒂尔比约恩立在他那艘巨舰*铁喙号*的船尾楼上。这条船比别的船都更长更高，于是全军上下人人都得仰视斯蒂尔比约恩，而斯蒂尔比约恩俯视他们。他头顶渡鸦双翼张开，又巨又黑，那是配神明、不配凡人之物。雾气渐薄渐散，太阳从地平线上探出头来，以清晨清新澄澈的光芒正照在他身上。

===Original===
Thyri stood there looking him farewell from the seawall of Jomsburg as the fleet steered west, and she was as a flower that is seen afar lonely on the sheer rock face of some wind-grieved mountain. The fog rolled up again and shut out Thyri and Jomsburg, leaving but a circle of sea about the ships and bright air and sunlight overhead. But as the ships passed out to sea under the outer headland they saw shadowy in the fog above them the shape of Moldi, gazing after them from the limit of the land.

So on the fourth day, being the thirtieth day appointed, came Styrbiorn back into the Dane-realm, and with him Sigvaldi and Biorn and other lords of the Jomsburgers, and they had five score and eleven ships of all sorts, fully manned. Near upon ten score ships of the Jomsburgers were already there in the Limfirth over against Alaburg, and some seven score of the King’s ships.

Styrbiorn came aland and went to see Harald the King straightway. The King said, “Wilt thou bridge the Limfirth with thy ships, Styrbiorn?” Styrbiorn said he would have the King call a Thing now forthright in Alaburg. So the King let call a Thing, and when that was done then Styrbiorn let them know what was in his mind to do. “And now,” he said, “I shall look for more than good words from you, father-in-law. There be here three hundred ships of us Jomsburgers ready to fare north with me. And I shall look to you for such furtherance as you may, both in men and ships.”

“That,” answered King Harald, “must give us a weighty cause to think upon. I seek no quarrel with the Swede-King. In west-viking we of the Dane-realm go our gate, whither we will. But that is an old saying, that the waves of the east sea do chant their songs to please the King of the Swedes.”

===Chinese===
舰队转向西行时，蒂丽立在约姆斯堡海堤上目送他远去；她像一朵花，远远地孤悬在某座悲风高山笔直的岩壁上。雾又卷拢来，把蒂丽和约姆斯堡一并遮没，只剩一圈海水环绕船只，头顶是明亮的空气和阳光。当船队驶过外侧海岬出海时，他们看见雾中高处一个朦胧的人影，是莫尔迪，正从大地的尽头目送他们。

到第四天，正是约定的第三十天，斯蒂尔比约恩回到了丹麦地面，西格瓦迪、比约恩和约姆斯堡人的其他头领随行，带了一百一十一条各式船只，兵员满额。利姆峡湾内阿拉堡对面，已停着近两百条约姆斯堡船，另有约一百四十条王的船。

斯蒂尔比约恩登陆后，径直去见哈拉尔王。王说：“斯蒂尔比约恩，你是要用你的船在利姆峡湾上架桥么？”斯蒂尔比约恩说，要王立刻在阿拉堡召开庭会。王便下令召集庭会；事毕，斯蒂尔比约恩便让大家知道他心里想做什么。“如今，”他说，“岳父大人，我指望你的可不只是漂亮话。我们约姆斯堡人三百条船就在此地，随时准备随我北征。人和船，你力所能及的资助，我都要指望你。”

“这个嘛，”哈拉尔王答道，“可得让我们好好掂量掂量。我无意与瑞典王寻衅。西面海盗的营生，我们丹麦人各行其道，愿去哪便去哪。但有句老话：东海的波涛唱歌，唱的是给瑞典王听的。”

===Original===
“This is not the answer that will content me,” said Styrbiorn.

The King said, “Thou hast still that trick, to fill thy purse with other men’s fee.” And he said, “Seeing thou art my son-in-law, for all I am not in this quarrel, we will yet lend you help of two score ships well found and manned.”

“I must learn thee to leave thy niggishness,” said Styrbiorn then. And this choice he gave them: either that they should fetch him two hundred ships, and that man withal whom he should choose to go with him, either else would Styrbiorn and his host sit down now in the land and dwellings of the Danes and eat up all their substance. “And that will seem a hard choice to you, and harder to bear. Yet shall you find the stronger must rule.”

In the end, bethinking them that need giveth little choice, the King and his folk said yea to this. Styrbiorn said he would choose his man now to go with him, “And that shall be Harald the King.” The King was mad wroth, but for none of his pleas nor offers would Styrbiorn be moved. Styrbiorn was like a man who, with mind at ease now, sitteth with his hand on the helm steering with a fair wind toward his set resolve.

===Chinese===
“这样的回答，不能满足我。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。

王说：“你还是那个老毛病，拿别人的钱装满自己的钱袋。”又说，“念你是我女婿，虽然这场争执我并不沾身，我们还是借你们四十条船资助，船械人员齐备。”

“看来我得教教你，改掉你这小气劲儿。”斯蒂尔比约恩说。于是他给他们出了这道选择题：要么，给他们凑足两百条船，外加由他点名随行的那个人；要么，斯蒂尔比约恩和他的大军这就坐进丹麦人的土地和家园，吃光他们的全部家底。“这选择在你们看来是硬的，受起来更硬。但你们终会发现：强者说了算。”

到头来，想到急需之下无可选择，王和他的人只得应承。斯蒂尔比约恩说，他现在就点名要随他同行的人：“那就是哈拉尔王本人。”王气得发疯，但无论他如何恳求、如何许诺，斯蒂尔比约恩毫不动摇。斯蒂尔比约恩像一个心思已定的人，安然稳坐，手扶舵柄，乘着顺风，径直驶向他既定的主意。

---

===Original===
The fourth day after these things aforesaid Styrbiorn, with the whole great host of the Jomsburgers and the Danes, sailed for the north. King Harald would have fared aboard of his own ship, but Styrbiorn made him go with him in *Ironbeak*. The King had learnt now the wisdom of that saying, “Never strive to match the sea,” and he would contend no more against Styrbiorn. Styrbiorn said to Biorn, “I will trust my father-in-law very discreetly. Till this fight be over, I will keep him by me like a doll. That will be good for him and good for his following. For they will guess well that if they keep not faith with me I will slay him out of hand.”

So now they rowed out from the haven and down the Limfirth, and so set sail and hove out into the open sea. And first they steered southeastward for the narrow seas betwixt Sealand and Skaney, meaning to coast so along Skaney-side and so north along the land toward Sigtun and Upsala. And that fleet needed wide sea-room to sail, as with foaming wake they stood out from the land, and it was long between the first ship and the last, and between that which steered outermost and that which steered nearest to landward.

But upon these things did the Danes that were left behind in Jutland sing this stave:

===Chinese===
上述诸事之后第四天，斯蒂尔比约恩率约姆斯堡人与丹麦人的整支大军北征。哈拉尔王本想上自己的船，斯蒂尔比约恩却让他随自己上*铁喙号*。王如今算是学会了那句格言的智慧——“永远别与大海较劲”——也就不再与斯蒂尔比约恩相争。斯蒂尔比约恩对比约恩说：“我要十分小心地信任我的岳父。这一仗打完之前，我要把他像个人偶似的拴在身边。这对他好，对他的部下也好。因为他们心里明白：他们若对我不守信，我会当场杀了他。”

于是众人划出海港，顺利姆峡湾而下，张帆驶入外海。他们先朝东南，取西兰岛与斯科讷之间的窄水道，打算沿斯科讷海岸一路向北，傍着陆地朝西格图纳和乌普萨拉进发。这支舰队需要宽阔的水面才铺得开：众船拖着白沫翻腾的尾流离岸而行，首船与末船之间遥遥相望，走最外侧的船与贴岸最近的船之间也隔着老远。

留在日德兰的丹麦人却为这些事编了这样一支歌：

===Original===
“Ne’er would the Jute-folk

					Yield gild to swift ships,

					Till by the land stood

					Sea-deer of Styrbiorn.

					Now must the Dane-lord

					Follow that hosting:

					Land-lorn and folk-lorn

					Drees he his weird now.”

===Chinese===
“日特兰人从来不曾

					向快船献纳贡金，

					直到那一天，岸边

					泊满斯蒂尔比约恩的海鹿。

					如今丹麦之王

					只得随军远征：

					失地失民，

					独咽他的命数。”

## XIV / 十四



===Original===
King Eric’s Hosting

Eric the King had espial since winter’s end of all that was done in the south there. When news was brought him of the gathering to a head of that power in Jomsburg and the Dane-realm he took counsel first with Thorgnyr the Lawman. “And now,” said the King, “is that need come upon us that we must lay our plans not as ’twere but Danes only and outlanders we must cope withal. For this, if it come about, shall bring into the land upon us a man of our own blood and line; and a man not to be driven alive out of the Swede-realm, I well think, once he shall have reached out hand to take it in my despite; and a man well loved of the folk too.”

Thorgnyr looked and beheld awhile in silence the face of the King. Then, “Lord,” he said, “there be two ways at every turning.”

“I see but one,” answered the King.

===Chinese===
埃里克王的征召

开春以来，南方那边的一举一动，埃里克王都有耳目探报。约姆斯堡与丹麦地面那股力量拧成一股绳的消息传来时，他先与松格尼尔（法律宣讲人）商议。“如今，”王说，“落到我们头上的这桩危难，已不容我们只当是对付丹麦人、对付外邦人来布置方略了。因为这一回来犯的，若是成了气候，将是我们自家血脉苗裔里的人；这个人，我想得很明白，一旦伸手要在我眼前把这王国抓到手，就不是能活着逐出瑞典境的人了；而且他也是众民爱戴的人。”

松格尼尔默然注视王的脸庞片刻。然后说：“主上，每个岔路口都有两条路。”

“我只看见一条。”王答道。

===Original===
“That,” said Thorgnyr, “is because you do hold your head so high that you may not of your greatness see this other way. And that is right, for it beseemeth not Kings to see all that groundlings can. Only, if you bid me counsel you in this, I must be no partial counseller. There be two ways.”

“Stand or flee away?” said King Eric. “There is but one way for Kings.”

Thorgnyr said, “You have answered me.” And he fell silent again. The King had turned his gaze away, and sat there leaning his chin heavily on one hand. Thorgnyr had liberty so to study the King’s face, and it seemed to him the face of a man that hath put behind him both good and ill, and goeth in some deep resolve unto his fate. Thorgnyr pondered these things awhile. Then he said, “Now I shall not be slow to tell you my rede, Lord. And this it is: that you let call a Thing straightway in Upsala. There, what griefs soever the bonders have, right them out of hand, and better thus and thus the laws for the common sort and do men right, that men’s hearts may incline mightily unto you. And therewithal let shear up the war-arrow, and call an all-folk hosting. And that were good rede, too, to stick the seaways that leadeth to Upsala, so as their ships may find no fairway to come up at us through the Low.”

They talked long on these things, and the King deemed good of Thorgnyr’s redes and let do accordingly. And the King let hallow the holy Thing, and there was huge going together of the folk in the Thing-mead before Upsala, with the throng of the bonders and the common sort and the King’s men and the following of the Earls and lords and landed men that were thither come at the King’s bidding from all the lands and countries of the realm of Sweden. And thither were gathered Earl Aunund of Fiadrundarland and the Earls of Helsingland and Tenthland and East Gautland, Bodvar Auzurson of Leikberg with a great company, Hermund the Old from Eyland, Koll Sigmundson of Acre-eres and Hiarrandi his brother, An the Black from Jarnberaland, Slaying-Starkad of Raening-oyce, Kalf Ongialdson of Kalmar, Steinfinn of Hising, Day Heriolfson and the lords of Jamtland and the Dales, Jorund of Vear, Liot Liotson of the Knolls, Oliver Leatherneck of Dalby, and many more. And earliest come of all the great men was Skogul-Tosti, the father of Sigrid the Queen. For in that same hour when the King’s bidding reached Tosti he took horse and rode from Arland, two days and a night without sleep or stay, till he was come into Upsala.

Men were all agog now with tidings likely and unlikely of Styrbiorn’s farings: one saying that he was already come aland beside Mirkwood and had burnt up the whole wood with fire: another that he was gotten into Norway and had slain Earl Hakon there in a pitched battle, and would shortly fare over the Keel into Sweden; another that he was turned away east for Garthrealm with all his ships, and all danger past: another that they were shut up in Jomsburg by the Wends: another that he was dead: another that he and the King were friends again, and that he was even now secretly in the King’s house in Upsala. With all which busy and contrary talk was the whole Thing in an hubbub. Nor was it clear how much men were like to hold by the King and follow him when it should come to fighting, and how much their minds inclined to Styrbiorn. But when the King spake to them of that which he was minded to do for the bettering of the laws and righting of the wrongs of men, according to the wholesome redes of Thorgnyr, then were they that had been eager before to do him service yet the more eager when they heard him say those words, and went all together praising the King and shouting that he should lead them into battle; and they that were of the other party held their peace or changed their minds.

And now the King let proclaim an all-folk hosting.

===Chinese===
“那是因为，”松格尼尔说，“您的头抬得太高，以您的尊贵，看不见那另一条路。这原也正当，因为国王本不该看清凡夫俗子看得清的一切。只是，您既命我为此事进言，我便不能做一个偏私的谋臣。路有两条。”

“站着，还是逃走？”埃里克王说，“国王的路只有一条。”

松格尼尔说：“您已经回答我了。”他又沉默下来。王已把目光转向别处，坐在那里，下巴沉沉地拄在一只手上。松格尼尔得以这样端详王的脸，他觉得那是一张已把善恶置之度外的脸，正怀着某个深沉的决意走向自己的命运。松格尼尔把这情形思量了一阵。然后他说：“主上，现在我便不迟疑，把我的主意说给您听。是这样的：即刻在乌普萨拉召开庭会。庭会上，邦德们有什么冤屈，马上替他们昭雪；把庶民的法度往好里改了又改，秉公待人，好教人心归向您。与此同时，传发战箭，召集全民出征。还有一条好主意：把通往乌普萨拉的水道钉死，叫他们的船在低地水路上找不到航路开到我们跟前。”

这些事他们谈了很久，王称许松格尼尔的计议，一一照办。王下令圣化这场神圣庭会，于是乌普萨拉城前的庭会草坪上人山人海：邦德与平民、王的人马、还有奉王命从瑞典王国各地赶来的伯爵、领主、有地之士的部从，济济一堂。到场的有菲亚德伦达兰的奥努德伯爵，海尔辛兰、滕特兰、东约特兰的诸位伯爵，莱克贝格的博德瓦尔·奥祖尔松率一大队人马，埃兰来的老赫尔蒙德，阿克雷勒斯的科尔·西格蒙德松及其弟希亚兰迪，亚恩贝拉兰的黑安，拉宁河汊的杀人斯塔卡德，卡尔马的卡尔夫·翁吉亚尔德松，希辛的斯坦芬，达格·赫里奥尔松，耶姆特兰与达拉诸谷的众领主，维尔的约伦德，丘陵的利奥特·利奥特松，达尔比的硬脖子奥利弗，还有许多旁人。众大人物里到得最早的是斯科古尔-托斯蒂，西格丽德王后的父亲。王的诏令传到托斯蒂那里的那一刻，他跨上马便从阿尔兰动身，两天一夜，不眠不停，直到乌普萨拉。

如今众人满心都是斯蒂尔比约恩行踪的消息，有的说得有鼻子有眼，有的不着边际：有的说他已在密尔克森林旁边登陆，一把火把整座林子烧了个精光；有的说他进了挪威，在一场对阵战里杀了哈康伯爵，不日便要翻越凯尔岭进瑞典；有的说他带着全部船只转向东边的加德王国去了，危机已过；有的说他们被文德人困死在约姆斯堡；有的说他死了；又有的说他与王重修旧好，此刻就秘藏在乌普萨拉王的宅子里。这些七嘴八舌、相互矛盾的话，把整个庭会搅得沸沸扬扬。真打起来时，有多少人会站在王一边随他出战、有多少人心向斯蒂尔比约恩，也看不分明。但当王依照松格尼尔的良谋，向众人讲说他有意如何修明法度、昭雪民冤时，先前热心为他效力的人听了这话愈发热心，齐声颂扬王，高喊愿随他上阵杀敌；原本心向另一边的人，也闭了口，或者转了念头。

于是王传令：召集全民出征。

---

===Original===
The fifth night after, King Eric and his men being served and set to the supper, came a man of his with sure tidings from the south that Styrbiorn, with so great an host of warships as had not been seen together till that day by living man, was sailed out of the Limfirth and thence along Skaney-side for Sweden. The King had not yet fully eaten, that there came into the hall a man of his bodyguard to let the King know that hither was come Earl Wolf, Styrbiorn’s foster-father and mother’s brother, ridden up to the King’s garth dirtied up to the horse’s belly, “And would fain have speech with you, Lord, but durst not trust himself unto you but and if you will handsel him peace and safety.”

“There is little need of that,” said the King. “When hath he known me to be sly, quaint and false, were it my very foe in mine hand to do of him as I will? But this is my friend and kinsman. Tell him he shall have peace.”

When Earl Wolf was come into the hall and stood before King Eric, the King said, “Thou rodest not hither to the Thing. A poor man that is true is better than an Earl if he be false.”

“That I am come to you now, King,” answered the Earl, “let that speak for my truth. And never was greater need of good will ’twixt kinsman and kinsman. And ’tis that hath brought me, ’gainst all wise fears and mine own good.”

===Chinese===
第五夜，埃里克王与手下人正在用晚饭，有个人从南方赶来，带来确切消息：斯蒂尔比约恩率着一支当世活人从未见过如此规模的战船大军，已驶出利姆峡湾，沿斯科讷海岸向瑞典而来。王还没吃罢，他的一名卫侍进厅禀报：斯蒂尔比约恩的义父兼舅父沃尔夫伯爵来了，一路骑到王的院外，马身直到肚腹溅满泥浆。“他很想与您说话，主上，却不敢自行见您，除非您肯向他立约，许他和平与安全。”

“用不着这样。”王说，“纵然是我的仇敌落在我手里，任凭我处置，他几时见我使过阴招、玩过花样、行过欺诈？何况这是我的朋友、我的亲族。告诉他，他自有和平。”

沃尔夫伯爵进了大厅，立在埃里克王面前。王说：“开庭会时你没有来。一个忠诚的穷人，也强过一个虚伪的伯爵。”

“王啊，我如今到您这里来了，”伯爵答道，“这就足以为我的忠诚作证。亲族与亲族之间，从来不曾这样急切地需要善意。正是这个把我驱来的——违着一切明智的疑惧，违着我自己的好处。”

===Original===
“Thou look’st afeared,” said the King. “Yet I have known thee for a brave man.”

“I fear not for myself,” answered he, “but for you, King; and for others.”

“Hath Styrbiorn sent thee to me?” asked the King.

“No,” he said. “I’ll walk in no nets, thinking I am hid. He hath not sent me. Yet I think he’ll welcome me with both hands if you will send me to him.”

===Chinese===
“你面带惧色。”王说，“可我知道你是个勇士。”

“我怕的不是自己，”他答道，“是怕您，王啊；也怕旁人。”

“是斯蒂尔比约恩派你来见我的么？”王问。

“不是。”他说，“我不愿自以为藏得住身而往网里钻。他没有派我来。不过您若肯派我去他那里，我想他会张开双臂欢迎我。”

===Original===
“Hast had speech of him?”

The Earl said, “Yes.”

“Is he landed?”

“King,” answered the Earl, “he is landed Southway by Mirkwood. Late yester-evening.”

===Chinese===
“你与他说过话？”

伯爵说：“说过。”

“他登陆了？”

“王啊，”伯爵答道，“他已在南边密尔克森林旁登陆。昨夜深夜。”

===Original===
“Alone?” asked the King.

“With an host not to be dealt with in battle,” said the Earl. “There is little time left: you see me ridden hot haste to you: I have killed two horses under me﻿—”

“Why?” said the King. “To lend me aid against him?”

Looking in the King’s countenance that seemed hard and ice-cold, and hearing the accent of his speech, inhospitable and passionless as the voice of the sea grinding on a shingle beach, the Earl winced, as a man might wince under a wintry blast. “I came to you, King,” he said, “to make peace while there is yet time.”

===Chinese===
“独自一人？”王问。

“带着一支阵前无法匹敌的大军。”伯爵说，“时日无多：您看我火速赶来的模样：我胯下已经跑死两匹马——”

“为了什么？”王说，“来助我对抗他？”

伯爵看着王那副坚硬冰冷的面容，听着他话音里那股淡漠无情的腔调——像大海在砾石滩上碾磨的声音一样不近人情——不由得瑟缩了一下，如人在寒风里打的那一个冷战。“王啊，我来找您，”他说，“是要趁还来得及，缔结和平。”

===Original===
“He hath come aland,” said the King. “Time is gone by, then. I said that if ever he should come again into the Swede-realm, that should be his death.”

“Say not so, King,” said Earl Wolf, and his face was ashen-hued. “Is all your old memories worn so out of mind? Because you are like and like, do not for that sake break one another. You would not hear him, but did send him into this woeful banishment unheard. That which befell on that ill night﻿—”

“Hold,” said the King, breaking in upon him. “Dost thou not know that the man who speaketh of that night in my hearing shall lose nothing but his life? And my peace which I sware unto thee shieldeth thee not as for that.”

The Earl bowed his head. “For your old love sake and kinship, Lord, will you not bend, and it were but one finger’s breadth? For so meseemeth, even so little a thing might set all aright: even if you should but take back the whole kingdom into your own hand, and let him then be heir unto you. Even should you still hold by your great rigour against him, to ban him from the land all your lifedays, yet I think I should persuade his mind, fierce and masterful though he be, to swallow it. Though you should live (and that is to be hoped for) unto a deep old age, yet is it to be looked for that he should live longer than you, King, being he is now so young and you thrice his years: then might he take after you in Upsala; but now you have broken all his hope and done him the worst wrong, and that remediless, to crown your own young son to be King with you in his stead. Will you not undo this deed? And then, on my head be it if I send him not away again out of Sweden. I’ll ride all night to bring him the news.”

===Chinese===
“他既已登陆，”王说，“时机便已过去了。我说过：他若再踏入瑞典国境一步，那就是他的死期。”

“别这样说，王啊。”沃尔夫伯爵说，脸灰得像死灰，“难道旧日种种，您都忘得这样干净？正因为你俩彼此相像，才更不该因此互相摧折。您当初不肯听他分辩，便让他连一句话都没说就坠入这悲惨的流放。那凶夜里出的事——”

“住口。”王打断他，“你不知道么：在我面前提起那一夜的人，丢不了别的，只会丢掉性命。我对你立誓许的和平，在这一条上护不住你。”

伯爵垂下了头。“看在旧日的情分与亲族之谊上，主上，您就不能屈一屈，哪怕一根手指的宽度？因为依我看，就这一点点小事，便足以挽回全局：哪怕您只是把整个王国收回到您自己手里，让他做您的继承人。就算您仍要对他严苛到底，终您一生不许他踏入这片土地，我也想，凭我这张嘴，纵然他性子暴烈倨傲，也能劝他咽下这口气。您纵然长寿——这也正是我们所盼的——但他比如今这样年轻，您是他年纪的三倍，他多半会比您活得长久：到那时他自可接您的位，坐镇乌普萨拉；可如今您立您自己的幼子加冕为王、顶了他的位置，便把他的指望击得粉碎，害他受了对他是奇冤极枉、无可补救的伤害。您就不能收回这道成命？只要您肯，我拿这颗脑袋担保，定把他再送出瑞典。我连夜也要骑马把消息送到他那里。”

===Original===
Earl Wolf ended so, and stood waiting on the King’s answer. The King, that had harkened to all this with clouded brow and downward look, and with hands pressed open, palms downward, on the board before him, lifted his face now and gazed steadily in the Earl’s face. “Whet me or let me,” he said: “the harm is done. Much could I forbear and much treat on with a man of mine own blood, and hard it must go with me now to say nay to this. Yet so it must be. The man that hath once drawn sword against me, with him I will make no atonement. It must now be tried out whether of us twain must be King in Upsala.”

There was that in the King’s face, and in the hard and chilling command that was in his voice, that took all heart out of the Earl for further speech. For a minute he stood looking on the King: then he turned his gaze right and left about the shadowy hall, meeting but looks that were borrowed from the King’s, grim and set; save only that old man Thorgnyr, whose countenance none might read: Helgi and Thorir and Thorgisl with sneering looks and the swagger of court bravery: and last, the Queen’s lovely face with eyes dark and doubtful and with nostrils dilated a little, like some fierce and graceful beast’s at the scent of blood.

“Wilt thou stay with me, Earl, or go back to him?” said the King.

===Chinese===
沃尔夫伯爵说罢，站着等候王的答复。王一直愁眉不展、垂目听着这一切，两手摊开、掌心向下按在面前的桌板上；此刻他抬起脸，凝视伯爵的脸。“激我也罢，拦我也罢，”他说，“木已成舟。对本是自家血脉的人，多少事我都可以容忍，都可以商量；如今要我对这事说一个不字，对我也殊为艰难。然而非如此不可。凡曾对我拔过剑的人，我与他再无和解可言。如今总要见个分晓：我们两个，谁该做乌普萨拉之王。”

王的脸色里、他话音里那股坚硬逼人的威严，把伯爵继续说下去的心思全数夺走了。他立了片刻，看着王；随后他环顾这幽暗大厅的左右，所遇见的目光，无不是从王的脸色上借来的，严峻而刻板；只有老人松格尼尔除外，他的面色无人能读得出来；海尔吉、托里尔和托尔吉斯尔一脸讥诮，带着宫廷武人的昂昂做派；最后，是王后那张可爱的脸：双眼幽暗而犹疑，鼻翼微微翕张，像一头矫健凶猛的野兽嗅到了血腥。

“伯爵，你是留在我这里，还是回到他那里去？”王说。

===Original===
“Is it in my choice, Lord?”

“It is in thy choice,” said the King. “And if thou choosest to go, I will send thee with safe conduct. For like enow the ways will be little safe for one faring southward from Upsala, sith I have bid out a war-gathering with an arrow-bidding. And thou art not a man unknown to folk, nor is thy kindred unknown and ties of friendship.”

“This will ever be said of you, Lord,” said Earl Wolf then, “that you are of all kings the most openhanded and the most high-minded. But that you are too hard-hearted and stubborn of bent, it hath been seen tonight. And this may be the beginning of woe to many.”

===Chinese===
“这由得我选择么，主上？”

“由得你。”王说，“你若选择走，我派给你通行保障。我既已传箭聚兵，从乌普萨拉南去的路，只怕对行人大大的不安全。何况你不是无名之人，你的亲族、你的交游，也没有人不知道。”

“后世提到您，主上，”沃尔夫伯爵于是说，“总会说：您是列王之中最豪爽、最高尚的一位。可您心肠太硬、执拗成性，今晚已经看得明白。而这，也许正是许多人祸殃的开端。”

---

===Original===
Earl Wolf took leave of the King now and rode south again, heavy of heart, to Styrbiorn his foster-son. But in Upsala was all gotten ready for battle. By then were the stakes stuck in the fairway below Sigtun, and weapons and war-gear dealt out among the bonders, and the fighting men marshalled under their captains and lords so as each man should know his place and what to do when the time should come. But with such unlooked-for sudden speed had Styrbiorn fared north from Denmark, that the King wanted yet nigh the third part of his host he had looked to muster, and hour by hour from the south came men with tidings of Styrbiorn’s approach. And it was clear now that the King must fight with that force he had, for little likelihood there was that he should be able to hold off the battle long time enough for all his folk to gather under his banners.

In all this making ready had the King chiefest part, and men marked in what unwearying way he wrought, yet like a man that is without gladness in his task and without thought for the morrow; and he fared through the army and took counsel and issued his commands as a smith might smithy or a digger delve a ditch, so that men thought the heart was gone from him, and but his strength and wisdom only and his iron purpose yet living in him and ruling these things.

Sigrid the Queen came to Thorgnyr and said, “Thou mayst say this is not women’s work, but ’tis this now lieth us on hand, to hold the realm against Styrbiorn until our full host be gathered. And as things stand now, if he should avail to bring his host upon us and force the King to battle ere he be ready, like enough we were all shent.”

===Chinese===
沃尔夫伯爵辞了王，心情沉重，重又向南，往他义子斯蒂尔比约恩那里去了。而乌普萨拉这边，一切已在备战。西格图纳下游的航道里这时已钉下了木桩，兵刃甲械分发给邦德们，战兵也已归编在各队首领之下，只等时辰一到，人人知晓自己的位置与职守。但斯蒂尔比约恩从丹麦北进的速度快得出人意料，王指望征齐的大军尚缺将近三分之一；从南方来报信的人，一个时辰接一个时辰，带来的都是斯蒂尔比约恩逼近的消息。事已显然：王只能用手头这支兵力应战了，想拖到足够久、让所有部众齐集旗下再开战，已没有什么指望。

这一切筹备，王亲主其事。众人看他办事，不知疲倦，却像一个于所操之业并无欢趣、也不为明日打算的人；他巡行军中，与人计议、发号施令，如同铁匠打铁、掘者掘沟一般；于是人们都觉得，他的心已经不在了，只剩他的气力、他的智慧、他那铁一般的意志还活在他身上，支配着这一切。

西格丽德王后来见松格尼尔，说：“你尽可说这不是妇人的营生，可如今压在我们手上的正是这件事：要顶住斯蒂尔比约恩，守住王国，等我们大军齐集。而照眼下局势，他若得以率军扑向我们，逼王在准备停当之前接战，我们多半就全完了。”

===Original===
“That is true,” said Thorgnyr.

“Hast thou thought on this,” said the Queen: “to gather together all the beasts of draught, both horses and oxen, that you may, and bind pikes and bills upon them so as they shall stick forth from the beasts, and let thrall-folk and ill-doers be driven before you to drive on the beasts against Styrbiorn and his folk? That should fetch him mickle man-spill in the first brunt, and small loss to us, and should even the odds somewhat. Or what thinkest thou?”

“Some would think it an ill deed,” said Thorgnyr.

“If thou deemest hopefully of it,” said she, “I would have thee lay it before the King as thine own rede. He will not take aught from me. Moreover, I would not that he should know that ’twas I thought on’t.”

===Chinese===
“这话不假。”松格尼尔说。

“你可曾想过这一计，”王后说，“把能收罗的驮畜，马也罢牛也罢，尽数聚拢来，把长枪镰刀绑在它们身上，枪尖刀刃从牲畜身上四面戳出来；再驱赶奴隶和恶徒们在前面，逼他们赶着牲口冲斯蒂尔比约恩和他的部众？头一阵就能叫他大大折损人命，我们却损失甚微，多少扯平双方的胜负之数。你看如何？”

“有人会认为这是恶行。”松格尼尔说。

“你若觉得这计可行，”她说，“我愿你把它当作你自己的主意呈给王。从我这里，他什么也不肯采纳。再者，我也不愿让他知道，这主意是我想出来的。”

===Original===
The same hour Thorgnyr laid this before the King. The King listened without changing countenance and was silent awhile, as if weighing the matter. Then his look darkened and he said, “Many will blame me for this and say ’tis a poor game to play. But is it not all a poor game, Thorgnyr, that I have set mine hand to: to bear war against myself? Yet will I play it out, and leave naught untried: no, not this nor a worse thing, if thou canst find it. Let set about it straight.”

When Queen Sigrid knew that this rede of hers was taken by the King she was glad at heart. She let send for Helgi and charged him saying, “I will have thee do this, Helgi: thou shalt seek out that little hairy ox that Styrbiorn set so much store by, and so bring it about that he shall be arrayed in the midst and front of all the baggage-beasts. That will be good sport, that they should meet the last time in battle indeed, ’stead of as heretofore in make-believe.”

Helgi promised this, to do as the Queen bade him. But it came not about, for Moldi was now in Jomsburg.

===Chinese===
同一个时辰，松格尼尔便把这主意呈给了王。王听着，面色不动，沉默片刻，似在掂量。随后他神色沉了下来，说道：“为这事会有许多人责我，说这手段下作。可是松格尼尔，我如今着手干的这桩事，不整个就是一桩下作事么：对自己开战？然而我要把它打完，什么手段都要试尽：不止这一件，还有更狠的，只要你想得出来。立刻去办。”

西格丽德王后得知王采纳了她这条计策，心中欢喜。她派人叫来海尔吉，吩咐他说：“海尔吉，我要你办这件事：你去找到那头斯蒂尔比约恩极为宝贝的多毛小公牛，安排他排进全部驮畜的正中、最前排。他们两个从前只是做戏，这最后一回，却在真正的战场上相逢——这才有好戏看。”

海尔吉应承下来，照王后的吩咐去办。但事情没有成，因为莫尔迪此时已在约姆斯堡。

---

===Original===
Now was it the third evening since tidings were first brought to King Eric in Upsala of Styrbiorn’s coming aland at Mirkwood. And now was all ready to the King’s hand and his army weaponed and marshalled and the beasts furnished and their drivers held in readiness. And on this had he determined, to give Styrbiorn battle in the ings and meadows on the left bank of Fyriswater a few miles below Upsala. Nor did the King lack aught save only men; but that was a sore lack, and might, if it so fell out, danger his whole state and kingdom. But there were fresh forces that should swell the King’s army, some coming in hourly and others due on the morrow or the morrow’s morrow.

All that day had the King busied himself with the army, looking to every point, even to the weapons and gear of the meanest man of the host and the cooking gear and meat and drink, that every man might be well fed and in frame for the fight; and he spake with men and heartened them and bade them quit themselves well when it should come to the proof. And now, when all was to his mind, he with his bodyguard rode at evening with Thorgnyr down to the head of the firth at River-oyce, mainly to ease his mind with riding after the long day’s toil, but with this intent also, to see for sure that Styrbiorn’s fleet had not sailed up into the firth despite the sticking of the channel at Sigtun. For if that should befall, then might they come upon the King’s forces and Upsala from all another side than that whence they were looked for.

Day was fading as the King and Thorgnyr rode down to the desolate shore at the firth-head. The wind blew a gale from the west. The first was empty: not a ship nor a boat: only iron clouds, murky and unbroken, that tore out of the western airt and passed overhead and away endlessly down the dark eastern sky behind the windswept pinewoods. Eric the King sat silent on his horse there, looking through half-shut eyelids upwind as if into the heart or quickening womb of that turmoil. Thorgnyr sat silent beside him: neither he nor any man that was there durst speak word to the King, for he seemed fallen in a mood that brooks not speech. After a time the King, troubled perhaps by his horse’s fidgetting, dismounted, tossed his reins to a house-carle of his, and walked down a few paces to the edge of the low cliff that overhung the stony beach where the waves broke and thundered. There he stood alone. Night shut down. There was no moon, and sea and sky were now mingled and blurred together; and, to a man so striving to gaze up that baffling and hurtling wind, there was naught to be seen save row after row of breakers bodied in unending succession out of the womb of night, and livid white in the windy darkness. One after another broke into foam, first along but a short strip of wave, then spreading quickly its foaming crest left and right. And in that blackness, where landmark and sea-mark were swallowed up, that spreading gave the appearance of white things first seen afar then rushing shoreward with a speed that seemed terrible and beyond nature, broadside on the flood.

A full hour the King stood watching them rush out of the remote dark to their ruin on the shore, by tens and by hundreds, and naught left but the black backwash at his feet. Then, without a word, he turned about, took horse again, and rode with his company back through the night to Upsala.

===Chinese===
算来这已是斯蒂尔比约恩在密尔克森林登陆的消息初次传到乌普萨拉埃里克王耳中的第三个晚上。如今一切都已备在王的手边：大军披甲列编完毕，驮畜装配停当，赶牲口的人也整装待命。王已定下主意：在乌普萨拉下游数里、菲里斯水左岸的草泽草场上，与斯蒂尔比约恩会战。王别无所缺，唯独缺人；而这缺得厉害，倘若时运不济，便可能危及他的整个江山社稷。不过还有生力军正陆续来投：有的一个时辰一个时辰地到，有的定于明日、后日抵达。

一整天，王都在军中操劳，事事亲点：连军中最微贱小卒的兵刃装具、锅灶饮食都要过问，务使人人吃饱喝足、筋骨活泛，堪得一战；他与众人说话，给他们打气，嘱咐真到考验之时，务须奋勇争先。待一切称了他的心，傍晚时分，他带着卫侍，与松格尼尔一同骑马下到峡湾顶端的河汊口，一来是长日劳顿之后，骑骑马散散心，二来也有一层用意：亲眼确认斯蒂尔比约恩的舰队没有不顾西格图纳的封江木桩，径自驶进峡湾上来。因为若出了这种事，他们就可能从完全另一个方向扑向王的兵马和乌普萨拉，教人防不胜防。

王与松格尼尔骑到湾顶那片荒凉海岸时，天色正在暗下去。西风刮得正狂。湾中空无所有：不见一船一艇：只有铁色的云，阴沉不断，从西天撕掠而出，掠过头顶，在风吹雨打的松林后面，沿着黑暗的东方天空无穷无尽地远去。埃里克王默然坐在马上，眯着半合的眼睑迎着风望去，仿佛要望进那场骚动的心脏，或它孕育胎动的子宫。松格尼尔在旁边也默不作声：他也好，在场任何人也好，都不敢跟王说一句话，因为他像是坠入了一种容不得言语的心境。过了一阵，也许是马匹的骚动扰了他，王下了马，把缰绳丢给一个仆从，独自朝那道低崖边走了几步——崖下便是乱石海滩，浪头在那里轰然碎裂。他独自立在那里。夜合拢了。没有月亮，海与天已混作一片，模糊难分；对这样一个顶着那股迷眼狂风极目望去的人，眼前除了浪，还是浪：一排接一排的碎浪，从夜的子宫里无穷无尽地生成，在风声呼啸的黑暗中显出青白色。一个接一个，碎成白沫：先只沿着一小段浪脊，随即飞快地把翻沫的浪峰向左右展布开来。在那片吞没了陆标与海标的黑暗里，这展布看上去就像一些白色之物，先在远处乍现，随即以快得骇人、近乎超乎自然的速度，侧舷迎着洪水一般向岸扑来。

整整一个时辰，王立在那里，看它们成十成百地从遥远的黑暗里冲出来，在岸上撞得粉身碎骨，脚下只余黑色的回澜。然后，一言不发，他转过身，重新上马，带着随从穿过夜色，回乌普萨拉去了。

## XV / 十五



===Original===
Fyrisfield

Styrbiorn lay with his host two days’ march north of Mirkwood that night that Earl Wolf his foster-father came back from the north. The sentinels knew the Earl and brought him in through the camp. It was the deep and dead time of the night. The waning moon, scarce three hours risen, shone bright in a serene heaven that was without cloud save for a slanting band of mackerel sky down in the southwest, and the bigger stars that were not put out by the strong moonshine blinked and sparkled. There was rime on the grass, so that it crunched under their tread. As they came past the horse-lines, there was here and there the sound of a horse kicking or stamping: a long way off at the far end of the lines a torch showed red, where a man was changing the tethers to make his horse comfortable: the tap of his mallet sounded dead on the stillness. On every side, as far as the eye might reach, the low skin tents lay mushroom-like in the stark white and black of the moonlight.

The Earl looked now and then at the faces of the men that guided him, and their faces seemed white and wooden, so that hard it was to know them for men of right flesh and blood; and like to them seemed the faces of all other waking men they encountered, of other sentinels as they came through that sleeping camp. Everywhere the Earl heard, as he picked his way among the tents, little noises of sleep, of sleeping men breathing heavily, turning and grunting. And here and there were men asleep in the open, their heads and shoulders muffled in cloaks, and they slept in awkward shapes and looked dead and piteous. Their snores and uneasy sighings came ghostly on the vast emptiness of the night, and the peace of it, and the stillness, and the cold of the quiet moon.

===Chinese===
菲里斯原野

义父沃尔夫伯爵从北边回来的那一夜，斯蒂尔比约恩正率大军扎营在密尔克森林以北两天路程的地方。哨兵认得伯爵，领他穿过营盘。那是深夜最深最死的时辰。下弦月升起还不到三个钟头，在天朗无云的夜空里照得明亮，只有西南方低处斜着一条鲭鱼天似的云带；没被强月光压灭的大星星，眨着眼，闪着光。草上结了白霜，脚下踩得咯吱作响。走过拴马的队列，这里那里有马踢蹄刨地的声音：队伍最远的一头，远远一点火把发红，有人正在换拴绳，好让马儿舒服些：他敲木槌的声音，一声声闷在寂静里。四面八方，目力所及，低矮的皮帐篷如蘑菇一般，卧在月光的惨白与漆黑里。

伯爵不时看一眼引路人的脸，那些脸白得像木头，叫人难以认作是真正的血肉之人；他们一路穿过沉睡的营盘，所遇见的其他醒着的人、别的哨兵，脸也都像这样。在帐篷间择路而行时，伯爵到处听得见睡眠的细碎声响：酣睡的人们粗重地呼吸、翻身、哼哼。这里那里还有人睡在露天里，头肩裹在斗篷里，睡姿歪歪扭扭，看上去死气沉沉，可怜巴巴。他们的鼾声与不安的叹息，鬼魅似的落在夜的浩大空茫上，落在夜的安宁与沉静上，落在静月的光寒上。

===Original===
They brought in the Earl now to the mid part of the camp where Styrbiorn slept; and there was there a great fire burning, and Styrbiorn slept in his cloak beside the fire, for he loved not tents, and the men of his bodyguard were asleep there round about him. There was a house-carle of his named Thorhall kept guard at that hour. He greeted the Earl and asked him if he brought any tidings. The Earl said he would tell that to Styrbiorn. “If you bring peace,” said Thorhall, “tell him now. But if it is as we think likely, then that will be better to let him sleep his sleep out.”

The Earl stood silent a minute, looking down on Styrbiorn, and the sleeping face of him was tranquil as a man’s that is cast in his first sleep beside his bride. And that seemed wondrous in him that slept there on the cold ground in a camp of war, on the eve of doings that he was well minded should break either himself or all the rest of the North.

“Let it wait till morning,” said the Earl.

===Chinese===
众人把伯爵带到营盘中部斯蒂尔比约恩睡觉的地方；那里烧着一堆大火，斯蒂尔比约恩裹着斗篷睡在火边——他不喜欢帐篷——他的卫侍们环着他睡了一地。这时当值守夜的是他的一个仆从，名叫托尔豪尔。他向伯爵见礼，问他是否带来什么消息。伯爵说，这话要讲给斯蒂尔比约恩听。“您若带来的是和平，”托尔豪尔说，“现在就叫醒他。若是照我们想的那样，那就不如让他把这一觉睡完。”

伯爵默立了片刻，俯视着斯蒂尔比约恩。他睡着的脸宁静安详，像初拥新娘、坠入头一场酣睡的人。而此人竟睡在冰冷的地上、睡在军营之中、睡在一桩桩大事的前夜——他打定主意要让那些事不毁掉他自己，便毁掉整个北方——这真是不可思议。

“等天亮再说吧。”伯爵说。

---

===Original===
When the morrow dawned and men were astir again, the Earl came to Styrbiorn and told him all. Styrbiorn said, “I could have saved thee thy journey, foster-father.”

The Earl paused. Then he said, “Wilt thou not turn back, even now?”

Styrbiorn shook his head. “No,” he answered.

Styrbiorn bade strike camp now. He brought his army by great journeys all that day and the next, north through the woods and uplands till they came at even of the next day down to the flats of Fyrisfield below Upsala, and there lay the King his uncle with a very great host. Styrbiorn took a stand now and stayed his army in the open meadow-land over against the King. And so great were the armies of Styrbiorn and of the King that no man that was there had ever seen so great a war-gathering in all the Northlands. They deemed it over-late in the day to join battle, and both hosts camped there on the field.

===Chinese===
次日天明，众人起身活动，伯爵来见斯蒂尔比约恩，把一切告诉了他。斯蒂尔比约恩说：“义父，这一趟你本可以省下的。”

伯爵顿了顿。然后说：“事到如今，你还不肯回头么？”

斯蒂尔比约恩摇了摇头。“不。”他答道。

斯蒂尔比约恩随即下令拔营。当天与次日，他率军兼程急行，向北穿过森林与高地，直到第二天傍晚，下到乌普萨拉以南菲里斯原野的平川上。他的舅父王，正率一支极盛的大军驻在那里。斯蒂尔比约恩勒住阵脚，把军队屯在王军对面的开阔草场上。斯蒂尔比约恩与王的两军人马如此之众，在场之人，谁也不曾在北方诸国见过这般盛大的兵聚。众人觉得天色已晚，不宜接战，两军便都在原野上扎了营。

===Original===
Now when it was high day men busked them for war, and Styrbiorn let cast his host into battle array and set up the banners, and his captains and lords of the Jomsburgers gave out the word and bade their men take heed to their places whereas each man was marshalled. Styrbiorn set up his banner and there were the men of his bodyguard arrayed, Bessi and Gunnstein and Ere-Skeggi to wit and Valdimar of Holmgarth and many more with their messmates and housecarles; and Biorn the Broadwickers’ Champion was there, for he was minded that in this fight as in others aforetime he should be not afar from Styrbiorn. There were also in the mid battle those Swedes that had taken Styrbiorn’s side at his landing, and over these was Earl Wolf now set in command. But on the right hand from the main battle was the battle of Bui, and there fared along with him Sigurd Cape his brother and a great host of the Jomsburgers and the Wendish levies and others from the east. And on the left had those sons of Strut-Harald place, Heming and Thorkell the High, lacking their eldest brother: for Sigvaldi had sneaked away under the cloak of darkness on Skaney-side. Yet was there not the tenth part even of his own men would go with him, but bade him go to the devil and went all under Heming and Thorkell. And that made great wonder, for Sigvaldi was commonly a man well followed and obeyed. And on the left went also the Danes, King Harald Gormson’s following, and their captains were Ivar of Weatherisle and Hiort the son of Sighvat and Einar the Red. But King Harald himself Styrbiorn would not suffer to depart from under his own hand, so that he went willy-nilly in the mid battle with Styrbiorn. Styrbiorn let make a shield-burg about King Harald.

Styrbiorn now said to Biorn his foster-brother, “I was never a talker, and now thou must talk to them for me: and say thus and thus.” And therewith he taught Biorn, in his ear, with swift stuttering speech, what he should say.
